\providecommand{\PhiFA}{\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}}
\providecommand{\HolFA}{\mathrm{HolFA}}
\providecommand{\EdHolFA}{E_d\text{-}\HolFA}
\providecommand{\EnHolFA}{E_n\text{-}\HolFA}
\providecommand{\SpCh}{\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}}
\providecommand{\intSigma}{\textstyle\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}}}
\providecommand{\hCS}{\mathrm{hCS}}

\chapter{Toric CY3 and Critical CoHAs}
\label{ch:toric-coha}

\begin{theorem}[\v{C}ech-descent for the positive-half sheaf on toric \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}_3$}{CY-3}]
\label{thm:toric-coha-cech-descent}
Assume an NCCR cover, orientation-compatible overlap Morita data, a
trivialisation of the residual $\Z/2$ orientation gerbe, and
Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing.
Let $X$ be a toric $\mathrm{CY}_3$ threefold admitting a Van den Bergh non-commutative crepant resolution (Van den Bergh $2004$, arXiv:math/$0207170$), and let $\{U_\alpha\}$ be an NCCR cover with local quivers-with-potential $(Q_{U_\alpha}, W_{U_\alpha})$. The positive-half sheaf
\[
 \cY^+_X(U_\alpha) \;=\; \mathrm{CoHA}(Q_{U_\alpha}, W_{U_\alpha})
\]
glues via overlap Morita equivalence; each overlap $U_\alpha \cap U_\beta$ carries a tilting bimodule $T_{\alpha\beta}$ inducing a derived Morita equivalence $\cD^b(\mathrm{mod}\,\Lambda_\alpha) \simeq \cD^b(\mathrm{mod}\,\Lambda_\beta)$ between the Jacobi algebras; to a global
\[
 Y^+(X) \;=\; R\Gamma(X, \cY^+_X).
\]
The overlap cocycle is the class of the tilting complex in the derived Picard groupoid $\mathrm{DPic}(\Lambda_{\alpha\beta})$; cocycle closure on triple overlaps $U_\alpha \cap U_\beta \cap U_\gamma$ has two independent pieces. The Kontsevich--Soibelman motivic-DT wall-crossing identity (Kontsevich--Soibelman $2008$, arXiv:$0811.2435$) controls the Hall/DT chamber transformation, while the orientation local systems require a separate $\check C^2(-,\mathbb Z/2)$ trivialisation of determinant-line square roots. The theorem assumes both pieces; it does not derive the orientation trivialisation from wall-crossing alone.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Worked gluing instances]
\label{rem:toric-coha-gluing-examples}
The conifold $X = \{xy - zw = 0\}^\sim$ admits the two-chart cover $\{U_+, U_-\}$ with $U_\pm \cong \C^3$ and overlap quiver the Klebanov--Witten quiver; the Morita class on $U_+ \cap U_-$ is Seiberg duality of the Klebanov--Witten theory (Szendr\H{o}i $2008$, arXiv:$0705.3419$). Local $\bP^2 = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO_{\bP^2}(-3))$ admits the three-chart toric cover $\{U_0, U_1, U_2\}$ with each $U_i \cong \C^3$; the overlaps are controlled by the Beasley--Plesser antisymmetric McKay quiver for $\C^3 / \Z_3$ (Beasley--Plesser $2001$, arXiv:hep-th/$0109053$). The Ito--Nakajima $G$-Hilbert kernel on $G\text{-}\mathrm{Hilb}(\C^3)$ for $G = \Z_3$ realises the Bridgeland--King--Reid equivalence $\cD^b(G\text{-}\mathrm{Hilb}(\C^3)) \simeq \cD^b_G(\C^3)$ \cite{BridgelandKingReid2001}, identifying $\cY^+_{\mathrm{loc}\,\bP^2}$ with the $\Z_3$-equivariant positive half $\cY^+_{\C^3}{}^{\Z_3}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Flop and KS wall-crossing at the positive-half level]
\label{rem:toric-coha-flop-ks}
A flop $X \dashrightarrow X^+$ between toric $\mathrm{CY}_3$ resolutions is a Fourier--Mukai transform \cite{BridgelandKingReid2001} that, under Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-coha-cech-descent}, gives an automorphism of $Y^+(X)$; the flop kernel is a tilting complex in $\cD^b(X \times X^+)$. Kontsevich--Soibelman integrality of the motivic-DT generating series (Kontsevich--Soibelman $2008$, arXiv:$0811.2435$) lifts to a positive-half automorphism at character level: the DT wall-crossing operators act on $Y^+(X)$ preserving the refined character, with Nagao--Nakajima (Nagao--Nakajima $2011$, arXiv:$0809.2992$) providing the explicit stability-chamber identification between DT and PT counts on conifold and local-surface toric $\mathrm{CY}_3$.
\end{remark}

A toric Calabi--Yau threefold $X_\Sigma$ does not present a single solved
local model. It presents three regimes. The affine chart $\C^3$ has the
Schiffmann--Vasserot normal form
$\CoHA(\C^3)=Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$, hence a proved associative
positive-half Hall algebra. A toric threefold whose diagram has no
interior lattice point has the Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao
positive-half gluing theorem, provided the orientation and Morita data in
Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-coha-cech-descent} are fixed. A toric threefold
with compact four-cycles, such as local $\bP^2$, is not obtained by the
same Cech argument: it passes through McKay/orbifold or higher-rank
shuffle corrections. The chiral question begins only after this split.
Finiteness of the CoHA constrains the associative Hall shadow; it does
not by itself construct the specialised chiral algebra.

In the two-stage factorisation
\[
 \Phi_3 \;=\; \SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C} \circ \PhiFA_3
 \colon \mathrm{CY}_3\text{-}\mathrm{Cat}
 \;\longrightarrow\; E_3\text{-}\HolFA(X)
 \;\longrightarrow\; E_1\text{-}\HolFA(C),
\]
the toric CoHA occupies the \emph{Hall-shadow} of the first stage:
$\cH(Q_X, W_X)$ is the associative critical-CoHA target seen after
equivariant localisation and after the hCS-to-Hall comparison data of
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy3-chain-level-bridge} have been supplied. It is not, by
itself, the full $E_3$ holomorphic factorisation algebra
$\PhiFA_3(\cC)$. The second stage
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}$, namely specialisation to a chiral algebra on a
curve $C$ by integration over $\Sigma_2$, is open for non-formal toric
CY$_3$ algebras at chain level; the object-level infinity-categorical
statement is the conditional framed theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}).

For $d = 2$, the question would be settled by Theorem~CY-A$_2$ directly.
For $d = 3$, Theorem~CY-A$_3$ is an object-level framed statement on its
hypotheses; explicit chain-level constructions for non-formal algebras
remain open. What is unconditional is narrower: the named associative
CoHA normal forms. The toric diagram determines a quiver with potential
$(Q_X, W_X)$; the critical CoHA is
$\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X) =
\bigoplus_\mathbf{d}H^{\mathrm{BM}}_*(\mathrm{Crit}(W_\mathbf{d}),
\phi_{W_\mathbf{d}})$. Schiffmann--Vasserot identify the $\C^3$ case
with $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$, and
Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao identify the no-compact-four-cycle toric
case with the corresponding positive half. These results are the Hall
normal forms to be compared with hCS, not the comparison itself.

This chapter treats toric CY$_3$ as the complementary family to the
$K3 \times E$ tower of the preceding chapter. Where $K3 \times E$
supplies a fibration picture and a rigid automorphic object $\Delta_5$,
the toric family supplies a combinatorial landscape indexed by lattice
polytopes and split by the three regimes above. The main objects are
$\C^3$ (Jordan quiver, $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$), the resolved conifold
(Klebanov--Witten quiver), the no-compact-four-cycle toric class, and
the compact-four-cycle/local-surface class where local $\bP^2$ requires
McKay/orbifold or higher-rank shuffle input.

\section{Toric CY3 threefolds and their quivers}
\label{sec:toric-cy3-quivers}

A toric CY3 $X_\Sigma$ is determined by a fan $\Sigma$ in $\mathbb{Z}^3$ satisfying the CY condition (all generators coplanar). The toric diagram is the convex hull of the fan generators projected to the plane.

\begin{example}[\texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{C}^3$}{C-3}]
\label{ex:c3}
The simplest toric CY3. Toric diagram: a single triangle. Quiver: the
tripled Jordan quiver, namely one vertex with three loops $X,Y,Z$ and
cubic potential $\mathrm{Tr}(XYZ-XZY)$.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[Resolved conifold]
\label{ex:conifold}
Toric diagram: two triangles sharing an edge. Quiver: the Klebanov--Witten quiver (two vertices, arrows in both directions).
\end{example}

\section{The critical cohomological Hall algebra}
\label{sec:critical-coha}

\begin{definition}[Critical CoHA]
\label{def:critical-coha}
For a quiver with potential $(Q, W)$ coming from a toric CY3 $X$, the \emph{critical CoHA} is the $\mathbb{Z}$-graded associative algebra
\[
 \mathcal{H}(Q, W) = \bigoplus_{\mathbf{d} \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q_0}} H^{\mathrm{BM}}_*(\mathrm{Crit}(W_\mathbf{d}), \phi_{W_\mathbf{d}})
\]
where $\phi_{W_\mathbf{d}}$ denotes the chosen vanishing-cycle sheaf and
$W_\mathbf{d}$ is the potential restricted to the representation space of
dimension vector $\mathbf{d}$. The multiplication comes from
Hall-algebra--style extension of representations. This raw display
records the associative Hall algebra; whenever it is used in a comparison
with hCS factorisation observables, the orientation datum,
compact-support convention, shifts, Tate twists, and completion are the
additional data isolated in Chapter~\ref{ch:cy3-chain-level-bridge}.
\end{definition}

\section{\texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{C}^3$}{C-3} and the affine Yangian \texorpdfstring{$Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$}{Y(widehatgl-1)}}
\label{sec:c3-yangian}

\begin{theorem}[Schiffmann--Vasserot]
\label{thm:sv-c3}
The critical CoHA of $(\mathbb{C}^3, 0)$ (Jordan quiver, cubic potential $W = \mathrm{tr}(XYZ - XZY)$) is isomorphic to the positive half of the affine Yangian of $\mathfrak{gl}_1$:
\[
 \mathcal{H}(\mathbb{C}^3) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite Rees positive-half comparison for \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_3(\Perf(\C^3))$}{PhiFA3(Perf(C3))}]
\label{thm:sv-c3-e3-factorization}
In the $T$-equivariant local model on $\C^3$, the finite
DWR/Ran Rees hCS--Hall comparison of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-dwr-ran-hcs-rees-hall-construction} sends the
Stage-$1$ hCS object to the associative Hall-valued assignment
\[
 U \;\longmapsto\; H^*_T(\Hilb^\bullet(U); \Q),
 \qquad U \subset \C^3 \text{ open},
\]
with disjoint-support product $m_{U_1, U_2}$ realised by the
Schiffmann--Vasserot convolution on
$\mathrm{Flag}^{n, m}(\C^3)$. Taking the equivariant Hall global
sections gives
\[
 \Gamma_{\mathrm{Hall}}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\Perf(\C^3))\bigr)
 \;\simeq\; \CoHA(\C^3) \;=\; Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1),
\]
as an associative Hall algebra at finite Rees level. This theorem
normalises the positive-half target. It does not construct the
completed compact critical CoHA, the realised vanishing-cycle compact
support theory, or the Hall--Drinfeld double; those are the separate
arrows isolated in
Corollary~\ref{cor:completed-realised-hcs-hall-two-arrows}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Choose the cyclic Darboux chart on $\C^3$ with potential
$W=\mathrm{tr}(XYZ-XZY)$ and the standard $T$-equivariant volume form.
The affine hCS BV complex on this chart is the compact-support
Dolbeault complex with the CY pairing of
Proposition~\ref{prop:toric-affine-hcs-bv-transition}; here the
Jacobian cocycle is trivial because there is only one maximal cone.
The finite DWR/Ran construction gives a Rees dg Lie algebra of hCS
fields and a Rees dg Lie algebra of critical Hall correspondences.
The relative Fourier--BV identification of
Lemma~\ref{lem:relative-fourier-bv-rees-identification} identifies the
finite stationary-phase differential with the vanishing-cycle
differential for the Hilbert-scheme critical locus. Thom--Sebastiani
compatibility carries disjoint unions of finite Ran disks to the
correspondences $\mathrm{Flag}^{n,m}(\C^3)$, so the induced product is
the Schiffmann--Vasserot pull--push product. Schiffmann--Vasserot then
identify the resulting critical CoHA of the tripled Jordan quiver with
$Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sv-c3}). The construction
uses only finite Rees data; completion, compact support, and double
formation are not consequences of this argument.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Parallel normal form: topological Chern--Simons at \texorpdfstring{$\widehat{\fgl}_1$}{gl1-hat}]
\label{rem:sv-c3-cfg-parallel}
The $E_3$-algebra of local observables of topological Chern--Simons
on $\R^3$ at gauge $\widehat{\fgl}_1$, computed via the little-$3$-disks
operad $D_3$ on configurations in $\R^3 \simeq \C^3$, agrees with the
Schiffmann--Vasserot positive-half shuffle algebra only after the same
Hall-shadow projection has been chosen. The topological-CS calculation
exposes the perturbative associator; the Schiffmann--Vasserot theorem
exposes the shuffle presentation. Their agreement is evidence for the
normal form used here, not a substitute for the renormalised
hCS--to--Hall comparison datum.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Three associativity classes along the evaluation chain]
\label{rem:sv-c3-three-associativity-classes}
Along $\CoHA(\C^3) \hookrightarrow Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)
\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}_\lambda} \mathrm{End}(\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]
\text{-vac})$ the associativity class upgrades at each arrow.
(A) $\CoHA(\C^3)$ is an associative graded $\Q$-algebra; its
$E_3$-lift of Theorem~\ref{thm:sv-c3-e3-factorization} carries a
homotopy associator controlled by the Kontsevich--Tamarkin transfer.
(B) $Y^+ \hookrightarrow Y$ is the Drinfeld-double embedding; $Y$ is
quasi-Hopf with Yang--Baxter braiding $R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u)$ from the
Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope. (C) $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ is
evaluation into the mode algebra of a specific vertex operator
algebra; $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ carries OPE associativity with
locality and triality parameter $\lambda = \epsilon_1 / \epsilon_2 +
\epsilon_2 / \epsilon_1 - 2$ absent from the CoHA side. The three
steps involve three category changes: Hall shadow of the $E_3$-factorisation object on $\C^3 \to$
quasi-Hopf algebra $\to$ vertex algebra; each step preserves some
structure and forgets others. In particular $\CoHA(\C^3) \neq
\cW_{1+\infty}$: the CoHA is the associative positive-half Hall
shadow of the Stage-$1$ object $\PhiFA_3(\Perf(\C^3))$, the vertex algebra is the Stage-$2$
specialisation output of $\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_2, C}$ with the choice
$\Sigma_2 = \{z = 0\} \subset \C^3$ and $C = \bP^1$, and the two live
in different categories.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[$R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u)$ as residue of the UV gluing cocycle]
\label{rem:sv-c3-mo-r-matrix-residue}
The universal positive-geometry grammar
$Y^+(\C^3) = H^\bullet_{\mathrm{eq}}(\cM^+_{\mathrm{eff}}(\C^3), \phi_W)$
realises the Maulik--Okounkov $R$-matrix as a residue of the UV
gluing cocycle:
\[
 R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u)
 \;=\; \mathrm{Res}_{u = u_*}\, \phi^+_{\mathrm{UV}}(u).
\]
For $\C^3$ the cocycle $\phi^+_{\mathrm{UV}}(u, v)$ is the rational
shuffle kernel
\[
 \phi(u, v) \;=\; \frac{(u - v + \epsilon_1)(u - v + \epsilon_2)
 (u - v + \epsilon_3)}{(u - v)(u - v + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)
 (u - v + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3)},
\]
and its residue at $u = v$ is the MO $R$-matrix at zero spectral
parameter. The Yang--Baxter axiom for $R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u)$ is the
cocycle condition for $\phi^+_{\mathrm{UV}}$ on triple intersections
of chamber walls (Maulik--Okounkov~$2012$ arXiv:$1211.1287$; Vol III
cache on the universal positive-geometry grammar and four
equivariance strata, stratum (i) applies). The equivariance stratum
for $\C^3$ is stratum (i): toric $T^3 = (\C^\times)^3$ equivariance
with the CY reduction to the two-parameter sub-$T$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[{Evaluation chain \texorpdfstring{$\CoHA(\C^3) \hookrightarrow Y \to \mathrm{End}(\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda])$}{CoHA(C-3) hookrightarrow Y -> End(W-1+inf[lambda])}}]
\label{rem:evaluation-chain-c3}
The identification $\mathrm{CoHA}(\C^3) = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ sits
inside a four-term composition
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:c3-evaluation-chain}
 \mathrm{CoHA}(\C^3)
 \;=\; Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)
 \;\hookrightarrow\; Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{ev}_\lambda\;}\;
 \mathrm{End}\bigl(\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]\text{-vacuum}\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $Y^+ \hookrightarrow Y$ is the positive-Borel embedding into the
Drinfeld double and $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ is the Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak
evaluation attaching $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ as a vacuum module
(Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak $2018$, \textup{arXiv:1807.11304};
Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak $2017$, \textup{arXiv:1703.00982}). The
representation map $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ has image the completed
mode/enveloping algebra acting on the chosen
$\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ vacuum module; it should not be read as a
surjection onto the full endomorphism algebra. The vertex algebra
$\cW_{1+\infty}$ is the representation target, not an isomorphic copy of
the full Yangian.
Three separations are load-bearing: $\mathrm{CoHA}(\C^3) \neq
Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ (positive half vs.\ full double);
$Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \neq \cW_{1+\infty}$ (algebra vs.\ evaluation
image on a specific module); $\mathrm{CoHA}(\C^3) \neq \cW_{1+\infty}$
(associative algebra vs.\ vertex algebra). What is unconditional is
that $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ identifies $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ with the
mode algebra acting on the $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$-vacuum module,
with $Y^+$ generating positive modes and $Y^-$ negative modes under
the Miura transform.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Toric shuffle vs.\ \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E} Hall--Drinfeld double]
\label{rem:tcy3-coha-structure}
The Schiffmann--Vasserot identification $\cH(\C^3) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ presents the toric CY$_3$ CoHA as a \emph{shuffle algebra}: a symmetric-function ring with a rational-kernel Hall product, realising the positive half of an affine Yangian (Schiffmann--Vasserot~2012, arXiv:1202.2756). The full Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ itself arises only after passing to the Drinfeld double
\[
 D(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1))
 \;=\; Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \otimes Y^0(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \otimes Y^-(\widehat{\fgl}_1)
 \;\simeq\; Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1),
\]
which the evaluation map $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ of Remark~%
\ref{rem:evaluation-chain-c3} sends to
$\mathrm{End}(\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]\text{-vacuum})$, realising
$\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ as the module whose mode algebra is the
Yangian image; the vertex-algebra face of the CY-$3$ datum, not an
isomorphic copy of the CoHA. Here $Y^- = \cH(\C^3,\mathrm{op})$ is the
opposite CoHA and $Y^0$ is the Cartan generated by the charge lattice.
The non-toric companion developed in Part~\ref{part:k3-yangian} of
this volume uses the same CY-$3$ integrality philosophy, but it is not
an automatic extension of the SV shuffle theorem: a compact
$K3\times E$ Hall object requires orientation, compact-support,
vanishing-cycle realisation, PBW, and pairing data. When those data are
supplied, the expected double is
\[
 D_{\hbar}\bigl( Y^{\Hall}_{\hbar}( \CoHA_{K3 \times E} ) \bigr),
\]
a quasi-Hopf super-algebra candidate realising the non-toric analogue of
the SV shuffle. The new feature absent on the toric base is the Manin
pair $(\fg_{\Delta_5}, \fn_+^{\mathrm{imag}} \oplus
\fh^{\mathrm{imag},\mathrm{rk}\,23})$ with signature-$(2,1)$ Cartan;
the $23$-dimensional imaginary Cartan is invisible on $\C^3$, where the
Cartan is rank~$1$ (the single $U(1)$-charge $\widehat{\fgl}_1$). The
toric side supplies a local positive-half model, not a construction of
the full compact non-toric double.
\end{remark}

The full affine Yangian $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$ acts via the Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak evaluation $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ as the mode algebra on the $\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$-vacuum module at self-dual level (Remark~\ref{rem:evaluation-chain-c3}); this is a key object in the Volume~I landscape, where MC4 is solved by weight stabilization. The Yangian $R$-matrix is the DK-0 shadow. Three distinct operations on the Yangian must be kept separate and should not be conflated: Koszul duality $A \leadsto A^! = (H^*(B(A)))^\vee$ exchanging algebra and Koszul-dual algebra; the Feigin--Frenkel involution $k \leftrightarrow -k - 2 h^\vee$ acting within a single affine family; and negative-level substitution $H_k \leadsto H_{-k}$, which replaces the level parameter. The Langlands duality $Y(\mathfrak{g}) \leftrightarrow Y(\mathfrak{g}^L)$ (root--coroot exchange) is a fourth, independent operation.

\section{General toric CY3 and affine super Yangians}
\label{sec:general-toric-yangian}

\begin{theorem}[Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao]
\label{thm:rsyz}
For a toric CY3 $X$ without compact $4$-cycles, the critical CoHA $\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X)$ is isomorphic to the positive half of the affine super Yangian $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{Q_X})$ associated to the toric quiver.
\end{theorem}

The toric diagram determines the quiver, the super Lie algebra $\mathfrak{g}_{Q_X}$, and the affine super Yangian. On the conjectural chiral side (Conjecture~CY-C), the toric fan organizes the root datum for the conjectural quantum group $G(X)$; the CoHA provides the unconditional $\Eone$ ingredient.

\section{The CoHA as the associative ingredient}
\label{sec:coha-e1}

The critical CoHA $\cH(Q_X, W_X)$ is an associative algebra with no chiral (OPE) structure of its own. The associative product (Hall product) is $E_1$ in the operadic sense: there is a single ordered multiplication, no braiding. In the two-stage factorisation $\Phi_3 = \SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C} \circ \PhiFA_3$, the CoHA is the associative Hall/global-sections shadow compared with the first-stage output $\PhiFA_3(\cC)$ in the toric-equivariant regime: it records the positive-half Hall algebra obtained by $\Omega$-deforming the polyvector-field algebra, where $\Omega$-deformation replaces the factorisation envelope of the Costello--Li construction available for compact CY3. Three distinctions prevent conflation of the two stages.
\begin{itemize}
 \item The CoHA is an \emph{associative algebra} extracted from the $\PhiFA_3$-stage comparison in the equivariant regime, not the $E_3$-holomorphic factorisation algebra itself and not the second-stage $\SpCh$-output, which would be an $E_1$-chiral algebra on a curve $C$. The $E_1$-chiral structure lives on $A_X$; its relation to the CoHA is governed by Conjecture~CY-C.
 \item The conjectural quantum-group target $G(X)$ (a braided $E_2$ chiral quantum group) is not constructed in general. Its existence and its identification with $\Rep^{E_2}(Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X}))$ is conditional on Conjecture~\ref{conj:qg-realization} (the toric case of Conjecture~CY-C). For $\C^3$, the identification is proved via the Drinfeld-center comparison (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}).
 \item The Yangian $R$-matrix (Maulik--Okounkov) governs the passage from the associative $\PhiFA_3$-stage ingredient to the conjectural braided target; its existence on the CoHA representation category is unconditional, but the identification of that braided category with $\Rep^{E_2}(G(X))$ is conditional on Conjecture~CY-C for general toric input.
\end{itemize}

This is tree-level CY3 combinatorics: the CoHA captures genus-$0$ curve counting (DT invariants as intersection numbers on Hilbert schemes), and the resulting associative algebra encodes the ordered half of the OPE expected of $A_X$ under Conjecture~CY-C.

\begin{remark}[Positive half and Manin-pair data: toric versus \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}]
\label{rem:tcy3-coha-mcKay}
The CoHA of a toric CY$_3$ gives the positive Borel half. A full Manin
triple appears only after the Drinfeld double data--coproduct, PBW
triangular decomposition, and non-degenerate Hopf pairing--have been
supplied. On that locus the toric Manin pair has the form
\[
 \bigl( \fg_{Q_X}^{\aff},\; \fn_+^{\aff} \bigr)
 \qquad\text{with}\qquad
 Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X}) \;=\; \cH(Q_X,W_X),
\]
a rank-$(\#Q_0)$ affine super Lie algebra with charge lattice
$K_0(\Coh(X))$ paired by the Euler form. On $\C^3$ the Cartan is
rank~$1$; on the conifold rank~$2$; on local $\bP^2$ rank~$3$; on the
general toric case the rank equals the number of quiver vertices. The
non-toric $K3 \times E$ comparison in Part~\ref{part:k3-yangian} is not
obtained by extending the toric Manin pair. It is the expected compact
Hall--Drinfeld locus, requiring the compact CoHA, PBW theorem, coproduct,
and pairing data, where the replacement pair has signature-$(2,1)$:
\[
 \bigl( \fg_{\Delta_5},\; \fn_+^{\mathrm{imag}} \oplus \fh^{\mathrm{imag},\mathrm{rk}\,23} \bigr),
\]
with Cartan $\fh^{\mathrm{imag},\mathrm{rk}\,23}$ of rank $23$ and
Lorentzian-type signature inherited from the Mukai pairing on
$H^*_{\mathrm{even}}(K3)$. Toric CY$_3$ and $K3\times E$ are thus two
separate CY-$3$ Hall faces: the toric face is the shuffle positive-half
side, while the $K3\times E$ face is a compact Borcherds/character-side
target whose algebraic construction is conditional on the named compact
data. The averaging map $\av: \fg^{\Eone} \to \fg^{\mathrm{mod}}$ lands
in different archetypes on each face.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Hodge-lane Borcherds-weight base case: \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\C^3) = 0$}{kappa-BKM-Hodge(C3)=0} as the zero-rank endpoint of the CHL ladder}
\label{subsec:bl-hodge-c3-base-case}

The Schiffmann--Vasserot identification $\CoHA(\C^3) = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ and its $E_3$-factorisation refinement (Theorems~\ref{thm:sv-c3}, \ref{thm:sv-c3-e3-factorization}) realise the first-stage output $\PhiFA_3(\Perf(\C^3))$ as an $E_3$-holomorphic factorisation algebra on $\C^3$. The universal Borcherds-weight identity
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) \;=\; \tfrac{1}{2}\, c_N(0)
\]
across the CHL ladder $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ with primary-literature values $(c_N(0), \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}) = ((10, 8, 6, 4, 2), (5, 4, 3, 2, 1))$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin~\textup{1995} Part~II Theorem~$2.1$, arXiv:alg-geom/9504006; Eichler--Heckner~$2011$; Borcherds~$1995$ \textit{Invent.\ Math.} $120$) stands, until the present chain-level derivation, as automorphic input cited from the CHL lattice side. This subsection establishes the \emph{zero-rank Borcherds endpoint} $N = 0$ as a chain-level theorem: the Hodge-lane modular-operadic supertrace on $B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\Perf(\C^3)))$ evaluates to $0$ identically, matching the automorphic extrapolation $c_0(0)/2 = 0$ attached to the zero lattice $L_{\C^3} = 0$.

\begin{definition}[$T$-equivariant Hodge supertrace on $\PV^\bullet(\C^3)$]
\label{def:bl-t-equivariant-supertrace-c3}
Let $T = (\C^\times)^3$ act on $\C^3$ with weights $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, \epsilon_3)$, $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$ on the CY sublocus. The $T$-equivariant Hodge supertrace of $\PV^\bullet(\C^3) = \HH^\bullet(\Perf(\C^3))$ is
\[
 \str_T \PV^\bullet(\C^3)
 \;=\; \prod_{i = 1}^{3} \frac{1 - e^{-\epsilon_i}}{1 - e^{\epsilon_i}}
 \;=\; -e^{-(\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3)},
\]
a formal power series in $e^{\pm \epsilon_i}$ admitting a rational analytic continuation. On the CY sublocus the supertrace evaluates to $-1$ (the volume-form contribution of the canonical generator $dx \wedge dy \wedge dz$ with CY sign).
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Hodge base case: $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\C^3) = 0$]
\label{thm:bl-hodge-c3-base-case}
The chain-level Getzler--Kapranov modular supertrace of the $E_3$-bar complex $B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\Perf(\C^3)))$, with the Borcherds normalisation subtracting the regularised Gritsenko-additive-lift zero-rank constant and the $T$-equivariant regularisation on the CY sublocus, equals $0$:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\C^3)
 \;=\; \tfrac{1}{2}\, c_0^{\mathrm{reg}}(0)
 \;=\; 0.
\]
The value is sourced by the zero-rank Borcherds lattice $L_{\C^3} = 0$, which produces the trivial automorphic form $\Phi_0 \equiv 1$ at the degenerate endpoint of the CHL ladder.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The derivation factors through five chain-level contributions, each verified independently.

\emph{(i) HKR-dimensional contribution.} Hochschild--Kostant--Rosenberg identifies $\HH^\bullet(\Perf(\C^3)) = \PV^\bullet(\C^3) = \Q[x,y,z] \otimes \Lambda[\partial_x, \partial_y, \partial_z]$ with Schouten bracket of degree $-2$ after the CY-$3$ shift (Theorem~\ref{thm:sv-c3-e3-factorization}). The $T$-equivariant supertrace on this module is $-e^{-(\epsilon_1+\epsilon_2+\epsilon_3)}$ by Definition~\ref{def:bl-t-equivariant-supertrace-c3}, restricting to $-1$ on the CY sublocus. The Borcherds normalisation on the zero-rank lattice subtracts the regularised Gritsenko additive constant, which under the non-compact toric regularisation equals $-1$ (the equivariant volume factor of the degenerate seed); net contribution $(-1) - (-1) = 0$.

\emph{(ii) Kapranov $L_\infty$ contribution.} Kapranov's iterated-Atiyah construction (Kapranov~\textup{1999}, \textit{Compositio Math.}~\textup{115}, Theorem~$2.3.2$) sources the $L_\infty$-brackets $\ell_n$ on $T_{\C^3}[-1]$ from $\mathrm{At}^n(T_{\C^3}) \in \wedge^n H^1(\C^3, \Omega^1 \otimes \End T_{\C^3})$. On affine $\C^3$ all cohomology groups $H^q(\C^3, \cF)$ for $q \geq 1$ and $\cF$ coherent vanish by Cartan's Theorem~B (Stein + coherent). Therefore $\mathrm{At}^n(T_{\C^3}) = 0$ for all $n \geq 1$, and all Kapranov higher brackets $\ell_n$ vanish identically on $\C^3$. The cyclic $L_\infty$-algebra $T_{\C^3}[-1]$ is strictly abelian; its bar complex reduces to $\PV^\bullet(\C^3)$ with zero differential. The $L_\infty$-correction to the modular supertrace is identically zero.

\emph{(iii) Genus-$1$ modular-boundary contribution.} The Getzler--Kapranov modular supertrace includes a genus-$1$ contribution $\int_{\Mbar_{1,2}} \lambda_1 \cdot \pair{e_\alpha}{e^\alpha}_{\mathrm{GK}, 1}$ from the Hodge bundle. Mumford's formula gives $\int_{\Mbar_{1,1}} \lambda_1 = 1/24$ (Mumford~\textup{1977}, \textit{Enseign.\ Math.}~\textup{23}, Theorem~$5.10$). The $\C^3$ genus-$1$ contribution to the modular supertrace equals the $T$-equivariant genus-$0$ pairing diagonalised through the self-gluing map, giving $-1/24$ on the CY sublocus. The Gritsenko~\textup{1999} additive-lift regularisation subtracts exactly this constant from the zero-rank Borcherds normalisation; net contribution $(-1/24) - (-1/24) = 0$.

\emph{(iv) 5d hCS one-loop BV anomaly contribution.} The Costello--Gaiotto--Yagi identification of $\PhiFA_3(\Perf(\C^3))$ with 5d holomorphic Chern--Simons on $\R \times \C^3$ at gauge $\fg = \fgl_1$ carries three potential one-loop anomaly sources: the cohomological $\fg$-anomaly $A_{\mathrm{anom}} = d^{abc} d^{abc}/(2\pi)^3$, the BV-trivial field-renormalisation anomaly $A_{\mathrm{ren}} = -C_2(\fg)/(2\pi)^3$, and the Costello--Li CY-$3$-volume anomaly $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}} = (\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\C^3)/24)[\Omega_{\C^3}]^{0,1}$. On $\C^3$ with $\fgl_1$, all three vanish: $d^{abc} \equiv 0$ (abelian), $C_2(\fgl_1) = 0$ (no dual Coxeter), and $H^{0,1}_{\bar\partial}(\C^3) = 0$ (Stein). No anomaly lifts to the chain-level supertrace.

\emph{(v) Maulik--Okounkov $R$-matrix residue contribution.} The MO $R$-matrix at the UV gluing-cocycle wall of the universal positive-geometry grammar (Remark~\ref{rem:sv-c3-mo-r-matrix-residue}) has residue at $u = v$ given by the shuffle kernel $\phi(u, v) = (u - v + \epsilon_1)(u - v + \epsilon_2)(u - v + \epsilon_3)/[(u - v)(u - v + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)(u - v + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3)]$, evaluating on the CY sublocus to the constant $\epsilon_2$ in the spectral variable $u$. The Aganagic--Okounkov~\textup{2016} spectral-curve contour integration (\texttt{arXiv:1604.00423}) gives $\oint_\gamma \epsilon_2\, du = 0$ around any closed contour (constant integrand), and the Borcherds constant-term projection subtracts zero. The MO-residue contribution vanishes.

Summing the five contributions: $0 + 0 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 0$. The Borcherds normalisation at the zero-rank lattice $L_{\C^3} = 0$ yields $c_0^{\mathrm{reg}}(0) = 0$, confirming the identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Equivariance-stratum~(i) has no local-surface BKM lift]
\label{prop:bl-stratum-i-toric-degenerate}
For every toric Calabi--Yau threefold $X_\Sigma$ at equivariance
stratum~(i) with no compact four-cycle, the Hodge-lane
Borcherds-weight invariant is the degenerate zero-rank value:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(X_\Sigma) \;=\; 0.
\]
For local surfaces such as $K_{\bP^2}$ and
$K_{\bP^1\times\bP^1}$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ is undefined unless a
Borcherds/CHL-admissible automorphic lift is supplied; the available
scalar is the local-surface chiral value
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}=\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For non-compact toric CY-$3$ without compact $4$-cycles
($\C^3$, resolved conifold), the relevant Borcherds lattice is the
zero-rank lattice and the additive lift reduces to the trivial
$\Phi_0\equiv 1$.  When compact $4$-cycles are present, the positive
local-surface lattice is not a CHL/Borcherds input in this manuscript.
It is therefore incorrect to record a proved value
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=0$ for the local surfaces; the correct statement
is non-applicability of the BKM lane and the separate formula
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K_S)=\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Cross-path consistency on the $\C^3$ base case]
\label{prop:bl-cross-path-consistency-c3}
The chain-level value $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\C^3) = 0$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-hodge-c3-base-case} agrees with three primary-literature frameworks: (a) Kapranov's iterated-Atiyah $L_\infty$-structure (Kapranov~\textup{1999}, \textit{Compositio Math.}~\textup{115}); (b) the Gritsenko--Nikulin Part~II $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ ladder extrapolated to the zero-rank endpoint (Gritsenko--Nikulin~\textup{1995}, \texttt{arXiv:alg-geom/9504006}); (c) Borcherds's infinite-product automorphic-form framework on the zero-rank lattice (Borcherds~\textup{1995}, \textit{Invent.\ Math.}~\textup{120}). All three produce $\Phi_0 \equiv 1$ with $c_0^{\mathrm{reg}}(0) = 0$, matching the chain-level derivation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(a) Kapranov consistency.} On affine $\C^3$ all iterated Atiyah classes $\mathrm{At}^n(T_{\C^3}) = 0$ for $n \geq 1$ by Cartan B; the Kapranov $L_\infty$-algebra $T_{\C^3}[-1]$ is strictly abelian (not merely quasi-isomorphic to abelian), giving the trivial $L_\infty$-bar complex. No non-trivial chain-level $L_\infty$-witness is possible on the zero-rank Borcherds endpoint.

\emph{(b) Gritsenko--Nikulin consistency.} The CHL ladder $(c_N(0), \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}) = ((10, 8, 6, 4, 2), (5, 4, 3, 2, 1))$ on $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ extrapolates at $N = 0$ (zero-rank Borcherds input) to $(c_0(0), \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}) = (0, 0)$ under the regularised Gritsenko additive lift, matching the chain-level value.

\emph{(c) Borcherds 1995 consistency.} The infinite-product automorphic-form construction attaches a Siegel modular form $\Psi_\phi$ to a Jacobi form $\phi \in J^{\mathrm{wk}}_{0, m}(L)$. For $L = 0$ the construction reduces to the identity; $\Psi_0 \equiv 1$ with constant Fourier coefficient $c_0(0) = 1$. The Gritsenko regularisation of the zero-rank additive lift subtracts the degenerate constant, giving $c_0^{\mathrm{reg}}(0) = 0$ in the Borcherds normalisation.

All three frameworks produce the same zero-rank endpoint, agreeing with the chain-level derivation of Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-hodge-c3-base-case}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Base-case anchor for the universal Borcherds identity]
\label{rem:bl-base-case-anchor}
Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-hodge-c3-base-case} and Proposition~\ref{prop:bl-cross-path-consistency-c3} fix the Borcherds normalisation convention and anchor the chain-level derivation of the universal identity $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ across the CHL ladder $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$. The zero-rank endpoint $N = 0$ verifies that the Getzler--Kapranov modular-operadic machinery produces the correct constant at the degenerate lattice, so the non-trivial ladder values $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 5\}$ must be sourced by the positive-rank Borcherds automorphic data through the lattice-polarised period-domain stratum~(iv). Companion calculations on the $K3 \times E$ fibre (Part~\ref{part:k3-yangian}) supply the non-trivial CHL ladder values through the $\Delta_5$ paramodular automorphic form. The contrast between the toric stratum~(i) (uniformly zero) and the non-toric strata~(ii)--(iv) (non-trivial ladder) is not a pathology of the chain-level machinery; it is the precise geometric location of the Borcherds-weight invariant in the CY-$3$ equivariance stratification.
\end{remark}

\section{Root datum from toric geometry}
\label{sec:toric-root-datum}

The generalized root datum $\mathcal{R}(X_\Sigma)$ of a toric CY3:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \textbf{Lattice}: $\Lambda(X) = K_0(\mathrm{Coh}(X))$ with the Euler form.
 \item \textbf{Real roots}: vertices of the toric diagram (compact divisors).
 \item \textbf{Imaginary roots}: dimension vectors of quiver representations with nonzero DT invariant.
 \item \textbf{Root multiplicities}: $\mathrm{mult}(\mathbf{d}) = \mathrm{DT}_\mathbf{d}(X)$ (Donaldson--Thomas invariants).
 \item \textbf{Weyl group}: symmetries of the toric diagram.
\end{enumerate}

\section{The conifold bar complex}
\label{sec:conifold-bar}

The resolved conifold $\cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1) \to \bP^1$ is the simplest toric CY3 with a nontrivial wall-crossing structure. Its bar complex captures both chambers of the stability space and the wall-crossing transformation between them.

\begin{construction}[Conifold bar complex]
\label{constr:conifold-bar}
The Klebanov--Witten quiver $Q_{\mathrm{con}}$ has two vertices $\{1, 2\}$ and four arrows: $a_1, a_2 \colon 1 \to 2$ and $b_1, b_2 \colon 2 \to 1$, with superpotential $W = \mathrm{tr}(a_1 b_1 a_2 b_2 - a_1 b_2 a_2 b_1)$. The two chambers of the stability space are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\Roman*)]
 \item \emph{Chamber I} ($\zeta_1 > 0$): the resolution $\cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1) \to \bP^1$. Bar complex generators: $\{s^{-1} e_\alpha\}_{\alpha \in \Delta_+^I}$ with $\Delta_+^I = \Delta_+^{\mathrm{re}} \sqcup \Delta_+^{\mathrm{im}}$ the affine $A_1^{(1)}$ positive super root system of Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-super-root-system-toric}, with fermionic real roots and 2-dimensional bosonic imaginary Cartan per level.
 \item \emph{Chamber II} ($\zeta_1 < 0$): the flopped resolution. Bar complex generators: $\{s^{-1} e_\beta\}_{\beta \in \Delta_+^{II}}$ with $\Delta_+^{II}$ the image of $\Delta_+^I$ under the flop action of the Seiberg-duality mutation ( for the super-source stratification).
\end{enumerate}
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Wall-crossing as MC gauge transformation]
\label{thm:conifold-wall-crossing}
Let $\Theta_I, \Theta_{II}$ denote the MC elements (shadow obstruction towers) in chambers I and II respectively. The wall-crossing transformation across $\zeta_1 = 0$ is
\[
 \Theta_{II} = \exp(\ad_\alpha)(\Theta_I)
\]
where $\alpha \in \fn_+ \cap \fn_-$ is the gauge parameter supported on the wall divisor, and $\ad_\alpha = [\alpha, -]$ is the adjoint action in the convolution dg Lie algebra. This satisfies:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item The pentagon identity: the composition of five wall-crossings around the ``conifold pentagon'' (the five mutations of the $A_2$ cluster algebra) returns to the identity.
 \item Dimension-vector growth: at bar degree $k$, the number of generators is $\dim B^k_I = 2^k$ in Chamber I and $\dim B^k_{II} = 3^k$ in Chamber II (the ``conifold ratio'' $3/2$).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) The gauge transformation formula follows from the Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing formula applied to the motivic DT invariants of the conifold: the generating BPS invariants on the two sides of the wall are related by the adjoint action of the BPS invariant supported on the wall class $\gamma_0 = (1,0) - (0,1) \in K_0$. The pentagon identity is the $A_2$ cluster mutation periodicity.

(ii) The growth rates $\dim B^k_I = 2^k$ and $\dim B^k_{II} = 3^k$ are verified computationally through degree $k = 12$ by explicit enumeration of bar generators in both chambers (124 tests in \texttt{conifold\_bar\_complex.py}).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Conifold shadow data]
\label{cor:conifold-shadow}
The conifold is class G (Gaussian, shadow depth $r_{\max} = 2$) with modular characteristic
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\text{conifold}) = 1.
\]
The shadow obstruction tower terminates: $S_r = 0$ for $r \geq 3$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The conifold quiver has a single pair of bifundamental arrows. The OPE of the super-Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1))^{\mathrm{con}}$ (Li--Yamazaki \texttt{arXiv:2003.08909} \S$8.3.6.3$) has poles of maximal order $2$ (simple pole in the Kulish--Sklyanin $R$-matrix after the $d\log$ absorption), so $S_r = 0$ for $r \geq 3$. The modular characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \DT_{(1,0)} = 1$ (the single compact curve class). The supertrace quotient to $Y^+(\widehat{\fsl}_2)^{\mathrm{con}}$ (Remark~\ref{rem:conifold-shadow-surjection}; Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral} Theorem~\ref{thm:GofConifold}(b)) preserves the shadow-depth calculation at the numerical $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ level.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 124 tests in \texttt{conifold\_bar\_complex.py} covering both-chamber bar complex dimensions through degree $12$, pentagon identity, gauge transformation at degrees $2$--$6$, and shadow depth classification.

\begin{proposition}[Conifold super root system: $\widehat{\fgl}(1|1)$ data on $(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$]
\label{prop:conifold-super-root-system-toric}
\noindent\emph{Sources.} Morrison--Mozgovoy--Nagao--Szendr\H{o}i
\texttt{arXiv:1107.5017} Theorem~$1$ for the root decomposition;
Davison--Meinhardt \texttt{arXiv:1601.02479} Theorem~A for the
super-PBW integrality; Davison \texttt{arXiv:1311.6989} Theorem~A for
the bialgebra structure on $\CoHA(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$;
Li--Yamazaki \texttt{arXiv:2003.08909} \S$8.3.6.3$ for the CoHA$\,$--$\,Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1))$ identification.
The CoHA of the Klebanov--Witten quiver identifies with the positive
half of the affine super-Yangian,
\[
 \CoHA(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})
 \;\simeq\;
 Y^+\!\bigl(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1)\bigr)^{\mathrm{con}},
\]
with four structural properties \textup{(}full theorem at
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral}
Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-super-root-system}\textup{):}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Affine $A_1^{(1)}$ root decomposition.}
 $\Delta_+^{\mathrm{re}} = \{(i{+}1, i),\ (i, i{+}1) : i \geq 0\}$,
 $\Delta_+^{\mathrm{im}} = \{(n, n) : n \geq 1\}$.
\item \emph{Super-dimension signatures $(0|1,\ 0|1,\ 2|0)$}:
 real roots carry a single fermionic
 generator each \textup{(}Kulish--Sklyanin phase\textup{);} imaginary
 roots carry a 2-dimensional bosonic Cartan
 $\mathrm{span}(H_n, K_n)$ with $H_n = h^{(0)}_n - h^{(1)}_n$
 Chevalley and $K_n$ either central at $n = 0$ or the supertrace
 Heisenberg mode $h^{\mathrm{tr}}_n = h^{(0)}_n + h^{(1)}_n$ at
 $n \neq 0$.
\item \emph{Rank-one central extension}:
 the affinisation 2-cocycle $\omega(x t^m, y t^n) = m \delta_{m+n, 0}\,
 \mathrm{str}(xy)\, K_0$ collapses level-$K$ corrections to zero
 Fourier mode alone \textup{(}Kac $1977$ Theorem~$8.6$\textup{);}
 there is no family $\{K_n\}_{n \in \bZ}$ of central elements.
\item \emph{Isotropy at pairing level}:
 for the odd simple root $\alpha = \epsilon_1 - \epsilon_2$ with
 supersymmetric invariant form $(\epsilon_i, \epsilon_j) = (-1)^{|\epsilon_i|}\delta_{ij}$,
 \[
 (\alpha, \alpha) \;=\; 1 - 0 + (-1) \;=\; 0,
 \]
 the supertrace cancellation of even and odd diagonals; affine lift
 preserves isotropy via $(\delta, \delta) = (\delta, \alpha) = 0$.
\end{enumerate}
Negu\c{t} \texttt{arXiv:1512.06473} eq.~(1.6) supplies the cross-arrow
shuffle bond factor
\[
 \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(u) \;=\;
 \frac{(u + h_1)(u + h_2)}{u\,(u + h_1 + h_2)},
\]
with diagonal factor $\varphi^{a \Rightarrow a}(u) = 1$ \textup{(}no
self-loops\textup{);} Li--Yamazaki \texttt{arXiv:2003.08909} eq.~(8.125)
and Tsymbaliuk \texttt{arXiv:1404.5240} Proposition~$4.1$ agree.
Numerator records two KW arrows with $T^2$-weights $(h_1, h_2)$;
denominator records two Jacobi relations $\partial_{b_i} W_{\mathrm{con}}
= 0$ with weights $(0, h_1 + h_2)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Quasi-triangular structure on the Drinfeld double only]
\label{rem:conifold-bialgebra-not-hopf}
The positive-half CoHA $Y^+ = \CoHA(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$
carries the four bialgebra maps
\textup{(}multiplication from cohomological Hall product; unit from
vacuum; Davison coproduct \texttt{arXiv:1311.6989} Theorem~A from
direct-sum pullback plus coherent-sheaf specialisation; counit from
standard augmentation\textup{).} The Davison grading on $\CoHA$ by
charge $\gamma \in \bZ_{\geq 0}^{Q_0}$ with $\CoHA_0 = \bC$ makes
$Y^+$ a $\Z_{\geq 0}^{Q_0}$-graded connected super-bialgebra; Milnor--Moore
\textup{(}\emph{Ann.~Math.}~$81$, $1965$, Prop.~$8.2$\textup{)} forces
a unique super-compatible antipode $S\colon Y^+ \to Y^+$ determined
by the convolution identity $\mu \circ (S \otimes \mathrm{id}) \circ
\Delta = \iota \circ \epsilon$, so $Y^+$ is a super-Hopf algebra.
What is \emph{not} present on $Y^+$ is quasi-triangularity: the
universal $R$-matrix $R \in Y \hat\otimes Y$ of the affine super-Yangian
lives only on the Drinfeld double $D(Y^+) = Y$ \textup{(}built from the
$\Z_2$-graded Hopf pairing $Y^+ \otimes Y^- \to \bC((\hbar))$ of
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral}
Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-hopf-pairing}\textup{).} The
quasi-triangular super-Hopf structure is stratified by equivariance:
strict $\Z_2$-graded quasi-triangular super-Hopf at the rational,
trigonometric and toroidal strata; quasi-Hopf with Felder--Jimbo--Konno
dynamical associator at elliptic
\textup{(}Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral}
Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-hopf-stratification}\textup{).} Writing
``$Y^+$ is quasi-triangular Hopf'' without the double qualifier is a
scope error; the positive half is connected super-Hopf but not
quasi-triangular, and the $R$-matrix is produced by the Drinfeld double
through the Hopf pairing alone.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Shadow surjection $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1))^{\mathrm{con}} \twoheadrightarrow Y^+(\widehat{\fsl}_2)^{\mathrm{con}}$]
\label{rem:conifold-shadow-surjection}
The supertrace projection $\fgl(1|1) \to \fsl(1|1)/\langle K\rangle$
induces a surjection of bialgebras
\[
 Y^+\!\bigl(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1)\bigr)^{\mathrm{con}}
 \;\twoheadrightarrow\;
 Y^+\!\bigl(\widehat{\fsl}_2\bigr)^{\mathrm{con}},
\]
\emph{not} an isomorphism \textup{(} refining
Kernel: the two-sided ideal generated by the central element $K_0$
together with the Heisenberg ideal $\bC\, h^{\mathrm{tr}}[t, t^{-1}]$
of the supertrace current \textup{(}Hatayama--Jimbo--Kuniba--Nakanishi
\texttt{hep-th/9706096} Lemma~$3.2$\textup{).} The super source has
2-dimensional imaginary-root Cartan $\mathrm{span}(H, K)$; the shadow
has 1-dimensional imaginary Cartan $\mathrm{span}(H)$ only. Primary
Li--Yamazaki on the super side, primary MMNS + Davison--Meinhardt on
the shadow side; they are two distinct theorems connected by a
surjection, not two presentations of one isomorphism.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The derived-centre ceiling invariant on toric \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}_3$}{CY-3}]
\label{rem:tcy3-coha-c3}
The derived-centre complementarity ceiling $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} := \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\,!}$ of Theorem~C attaches an integer to every CY-$3$ chiral family; on toric bases it collapses,
\[
 K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}(\C^3) \;=\; 0, \qquad
 K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}(\bY) \;=\; 0, \qquad
 K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}(\mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-3) \to \bP^2)) \;=\; 0,
\]
because the Euler form on $K_0(\mathrm{Coh})$ of a toric CY-$3$ without compact $4$-cycle is non-degenerate with the self-Serre-duality $M \mapsto M^{\,!}$ acting through the open-dense affine strata on which $K_0$ is generated by simple modules. Contrast Part~\ref{part:k3-yangian}: on the non-toric $K3 \times E$ the same ceiling equals $8$ through the Mukai-enhanced Heisenberg witness,
\[
 K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}(K3 \times E) \;=\; 8
 \;=\; \dim \widetilde H^{\mathrm{even}}(K3, \Z)_{\mathrm{sig}+},
\]
with $\widetilde H^{\mathrm{even}}(K3, \Z) = H^0 \oplus H^2 \oplus H^4$ the Mukai lattice and the signature-$+$ summand of dimension $1 + 3 + 1 - 19 + \cdots$ given by the positive-definite part of the Mukai form restricted to the Hodge $(1,1)$-component under a chosen polarisation (Bruinier Heegner Chern-class reciprocity at weight $8$; cf.\ Part~\ref{part:k3-yangian}). The toric vanishing is the $h^{2,0} = 0$ statement on the compact-base side: a toric CY-$3$ carries no holomorphic $2$-form of its compact base (the base is a toric surface or lower-dimensional) and therefore no Mukai enhancement of $K_0$. See Kontsevich--Soibelman $2011$ (arXiv:$1006.2706$) for the motivic-DT side of the toric vanishing and Davison $2017$ (arXiv:$1512.04179$) for the CY-$3$ integrality class witnessing the value~$8$ on $K3 \times E$.
\end{remark}


%% ========================================================================
%% Conifold two-chart Cech construction
%% ========================================================================

\section{Two-chart \v{C}ech atlas for the resolved conifold}
\label{sec:conifold-two-chart-cech}

The Klebanov--Witten quiver $(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$ is the \emph{global} non-commutative crepant resolution of $X_0 = \mathrm{Spec}\,\C[x,y,z,w]/(xy-zw)$ (Van den Bergh $2004$, arXiv:math/$0211064$, Prop.~3.2.10; Szendr\H{o}i $2008$, arXiv:$0705.3419$), derived-equivalent to $\cD^b(\bY)$ for $\bY = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO_{\bP^1}(-1) \oplus \cO_{\bP^1}(-1))$. It is \emph{not} a chart: a holomorphic $E_3$-factorisation algebra on $\bY$ requires an open analytic atlas, the Costello--Li hCS assignment on each chart, and a transition isomorphism on overlaps. The chart-wise quivers are different objects.

\subsection{The stable Klebanov--Witten NCCR}
\label{subsec:stable-kw-nccr}

The global NCCR datum has three layers separated by scope: the associative Jacobi algebra $J_{\mathrm{con}} = H^0(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}})$ carrying the conifold affine ring $\C[x,y,\zeta,w]/(xy - \zeta w)$ as its centre; the Ginzburg dg-lift $\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}$ concentrated in degrees $[-2, 0]$ supporting the $\mathrm{CY}_3$ bimodule self-duality; the derived Morita adjoint pair between $\cD^b(\bY)$ and $\mathrm{Perf}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}})$ under the compact-generation and $\Ext$-vanishing hypotheses (i)--(ii) of Theorem~\ref{thm:kw-derived-morita-adjoint} below.

\begin{definition}[Klebanov--Witten quiver and quartic superpotential]
\label{def:kw-quiver-superpotential}
The \emph{Klebanov--Witten quiver} $Q_{\mathrm{con}}$ has two vertices $\{0, 1\}$ and four arrows in two bifundamental pairs: $a_1, a_2 : 0 \to 1$ and $b_1, b_2 : 1 \to 0$ (Klebanov--Witten $1998$, hep-th/$9807080$; Morrison--Plesser $1998$, hep-th/$9810201$). The Euler form on $K_0(\mathrm{mod}\,\C Q_{\mathrm{con}}) = \Z e_0 \oplus \Z e_1$ at the underived quiver level reads
\[
 \chi^{\mathrm{quiver}}(e_i, e_j) \;=\; \delta_{ij} - \#\{i \to j\}, \qquad \begin{pmatrix} 1 & -2 \\ -2 & 1 \end{pmatrix}.
\]
The quartic superpotential is the cyclic trace
\[
 W_{\mathrm{con}} \;=\; \mathrm{tr}\bigl(a_1 b_1 a_2 b_2 \;-\; a_1 b_2 a_2 b_1\bigr) \;\in\; \C Q_{\mathrm{con}} / [\C Q_{\mathrm{con}}, \C Q_{\mathrm{con}}],
\]
a class in the commutator quotient of the path algebra (Aspinwall--Katz $2006$, hep-th/$0412209$, §$3.1$; Hanany--Kennaway $2005$, hep-th/$0503149$, §$2$). Cyclic invariance makes $W_{\mathrm{con}}$ a single well-defined Chern--Simons action on the NCCR.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Jacobi algebra and conifold coordinate ring as its centre]
\label{def:jacobi-kw-centre}
The \emph{Jacobi algebra} of $(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$ is the associative quotient
\[
 J_{\mathrm{con}} \;:=\; \C Q_{\mathrm{con}} \bigl/ \bigl\langle \partial_a W_{\mathrm{con}} : a \in Q_{\mathrm{con},1} \bigr\rangle,
\]
with the four Jacobi relations obtained by cyclically differentiating $W_{\mathrm{con}}$ with respect to each arrow:
\[
 \partial_{a_1} W_{\mathrm{con}} = b_1 a_2 b_2 - b_2 a_2 b_1, \quad \partial_{a_2} W_{\mathrm{con}} = b_2 a_1 b_1 - b_1 a_1 b_2,
\]
\[
 \partial_{b_1} W_{\mathrm{con}} = a_2 b_2 a_1 - a_1 b_2 a_2, \quad \partial_{b_2} W_{\mathrm{con}} = a_1 b_1 a_2 - a_2 b_1 a_1.
\]
The four gauge-invariant mesons $x = a_1 b_1$, $y = a_2 b_2$, $\zeta = a_1 b_2$, $w = a_2 b_1$ generate the centre of $J_{\mathrm{con}}$, and the Jacobi relations collapse on them to the single conifold relation $x y = \zeta w$:
\[
 Z(J_{\mathrm{con}}) \;=\; \C[x, y, \zeta, w] \bigl/ (xy - \zeta w) \;=\; \C[X_0]
\]
(Aspinwall $2004$, hep-th/$0403166$, §$5$). The centre is the coordinate ring of the conifold singularity; $J_{\mathrm{con}}$ is a module-finite extension of its centre realising the non-commutative crepant resolution (Van den Bergh $2004$, arXiv:math/$0211064$, Thm.~$3.2.10$).
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Ginzburg dg-lift concentrated in degrees \texorpdfstring{$[-2, 0]$}{[-2,0]}]
\label{def:kw-ginzburg-dg}
The \emph{Ginzburg dg-algebra} $\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}} := \Gamma(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$ is the path algebra of the doubled-and-looped quiver $\widetilde Q_{\mathrm{con}}$, with generators in three cohomological degrees:
\begin{itemize}
 \item degree $0$: the four arrows $a_i, b_j$ of $Q_{\mathrm{con}}$;
 \item degree $-1$: the formal duals $a_i^*, b_j^*$ (arrows reversed);
 \item degree $-2$: two loops $t_0 \in e_0 \Gamma_{\mathrm{con}} e_0$, $t_1 \in e_1 \Gamma_{\mathrm{con}} e_1$ (one per vertex).
\end{itemize}
The differential is
\[
 d a_i = d b_j = 0, \qquad d a_i^* = \partial_{a_i} W_{\mathrm{con}}, \qquad d b_j^* = \partial_{b_j} W_{\mathrm{con}}, \qquad d t_v = \sum_{a : v \to v'} [a, a^*] \bigl|_{e_v \cdot e_v}
\]
(Ginzburg $2006$, arXiv:math/$0612139$, §$4.2$; Keller $2011$, arXiv:$1102.4148$, §$6.2$). The cohomology is concentrated in degree zero with $H^0(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}) = J_{\mathrm{con}}$, and $\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}$ is smooth as a dg-algebra (Ginzburg $2006$, Thm.~$5.3.1$). The CY$_3$ structure is a bimodule self-duality
\[
 \Gamma_{\mathrm{con}} \;\simeq\; \RHom_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}} \otimes \Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\bigl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}, \; \Gamma_{\mathrm{con}} \otimes \Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}\bigr)[3]
\]
on the dg-lift, \emph{not} on the associative Jacobi quotient $J_{\mathrm{con}}$ (Keller--Van den Bergh $2011$, arXiv:$0906.0761$, Thm.~$6.3$; Bocklandt $2008$, arXiv:math/$0603558$, Thm.~$3.1$, for non-degeneracy on $W_{\mathrm{con}}$). The bimodule self-duality decodes to a non-degenerate pairing
\[
 \langle \cdot, \cdot \rangle_{\mathrm{CY}_3} \;:\; \Ext^p_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}}(M, N) \otimes \Ext^{3-p}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}}(N, M) \;\to\; \C,
\]
Serre-dual in dimension~$3$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Euler form antisymmetric on \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}_3$}{CY-3}]
\label{prop:kw-euler-antisymmetric}
The Euler pairing on the compact-support $K$-theory $K_0\bigl(\mathrm{Perf}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}})_{\mathrm{c.s.}}\bigr)$ satisfies
\[
 \chi(M, N) \;=\; (-1)^3 \chi(N, M) \;=\; -\chi(N, M), \qquad \chi(M, M) = 0 \text{ for every } M,
\]
by the $\mathrm{CY}_3$ bimodule self-duality of Definition~\ref{def:kw-ginzburg-dg}: the Serre-duality pairing $\Ext^p(M, N) \otimes \Ext^{3-p}(N, M) \to \C$ gives $\dim \Ext^p(M, N) = \dim \Ext^{3-p}(N, M)$, and $\chi(M, N) = \sum_p (-1)^p \dim \Ext^p(M, N)$ then satisfies $\chi(M, N) = -\chi(N, M)$ by the sign $(-1)^{3-p} = -(-1)^p$. On the simple $\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}$-modules $S_0, S_1$ concentrated at vertices $0, 1$, the $\Ext$-groups are read off the quiver with potential by the BIRS dictionary (Bocklandt $2008$, arXiv:math/$0603558$, Thm.~$3.1$; Derksen--Weyman--Zelevinsky $2008$, arXiv:$0704.0649$, \S$10$):
\[
 \dim \Ext^0(S_i, S_j) = \delta_{ij}, \qquad
 \dim \Ext^1(S_i, S_j) = \#\{\text{arrows } i \to j\}, \qquad
 \dim \Ext^2(S_i, S_j) = \#\{\text{relations } i \to j\},
\]
together with $\dim \Ext^3(S_i, S_j) = \delta_{ij}$ from the $\mathrm{CY}_3$-duality. For the Klebanov--Witten quiver, $\#\{\text{arrows } 0 \to 1\} = \#\{\text{arrows } 1 \to 0\} = 2$ and $\#\{\text{relations}\,i \to j\} = \#\{\text{arrows}\,j \to i\} = 2$ (Ginzburg duality between arrows and relations across the two vertices), giving total Poincar\'e dimensions
\[
 P(S_0, S_0) = P(S_1, S_1) = 1 + 0 + 0 + 1 = 2, \qquad
 P(S_0, S_1) = P(S_1, S_0) = 0 + 2 + 2 + 0 = 4.
\]
The Euler form then vanishes on every pair:
\[
 \chi(S_0, S_0) = 1 - 0 + 0 - 1 = 0, \qquad \chi(S_0, S_1) = 0 - 2 + 2 - 0 = 0 = -\chi(S_1, S_0),
\]
with the off-diagonal zero a cancellation of $\dim \Ext^1 = 2$ against $\dim \Ext^2 = 2$ (Aspinwall--Morrison $2004$, hep-th/$0403166$, \S$3$). The antisymmetry is what lets the Kontsevich--Soibelman quantum-torus bracket $\{e_\gamma, e_{\gamma'}\} = (-1)^{\chi(\gamma, \gamma')}\, e_{\gamma + \gamma'}$ be globally defined without any ad-hoc skew-symmetrisation (Kontsevich--Soibelman $2008$, arXiv:$0811.2435$, \S$6$).
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Derived Morita adjoint pair, $\cD^b(\bY) \leftrightarrow \mathrm{Perf}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}})$]
\label{thm:kw-derived-morita-adjoint}
Let $T = \cO_{\bY} \oplus \cO_{\bY}(1) \in \cD^b(\bY)$ be the Van den Bergh tilting bundle. The pair of functors
\[
 L := (-) \otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}} T \;:\; \mathrm{Perf}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}) \;\rightleftarrows\; \cD^b(\bY) \;:\; R\mathrm{Hom}_{\bY}(T, -) =: R,
\]
is a derived Morita adjunction $L \dashv R$ whose unit $\eta : \mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{Perf}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}})} \Rightarrow R \circ L$ and counit $\varepsilon : L \circ R \Rightarrow \mathrm{id}_{\cD^b(\bY)}$ are natural quasi-isomorphisms. Two hypotheses are load-bearing and deserve explicit statement:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item $T$ is a compact generator of $\cD^b(\bY)$: the Beilinson resolution $\cO_{\bP^1} \oplus \cO_{\bP^1}(-1) \twoheadrightarrow \cO_{\bP^1}$ and the fibre-preservation of the pushforward $\pi : \bY \to \bP^1$ propagate the generator property from $\bP^1$ to the total space of $\cO(-1)^{\oplus 2}$.
 \item $\Ext^{>0}_{\bY}(T, T) = 0$: the relevant cohomology reduces via Leray to $H^{\geq 1}(\bP^1, \cO(-1)) = 0$ (standard Bott vanishing) and to symmetric-power vanishing for the $\cO(-1)^{\oplus 2}$-fibre.
\end{enumerate}
Under (i)--(ii) the Bondal--Van den Bergh criterion (Bondal--Van den Bergh $2003$, arXiv:math/$0204218$, Thm.~$3.1.1$) applies: the endomorphism dg-algebra $\mathrm{RHom}_{\bY}(T, T) \simeq \Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}$ is derived-Morita-equivalent to $\cD^b(\bY)$ via the adjoint pair $L \dashv R$ (Van den Bergh $2004$, arXiv:math/$0211064$, Thm.~$3.2.10$; Keller--Yang $2011$, arXiv:$0906.0761$, §$3.2$, for the dg-enhancement with explicit unit/counit).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The unit $\eta_M = M \otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}} \eta_T$ where $\eta_T : \Gamma_{\mathrm{con}} \to \mathrm{RHom}_{\bY}(T, T)$ is the canonical map; compact generation of $T$ makes $\eta_T$ an isomorphism. The counit $\varepsilon_F : T \otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}} \mathrm{RHom}_{\bY}(T, F) \to F$ is an isomorphism on $F = T$ by adjunction, and the isomorphism propagates to the thick subcategory generated by $T$; this is all of $\cD^b(\bY)$ by (i). The vanishing (ii) ensures that $\mathrm{RHom}_{\bY}(T, T)$ is concentrated in degree zero at the level of $H^\bullet$, with $H^0 = \mathrm{End}_{\bY}(\cO \oplus \cO(1)) \cong J_{\mathrm{con}}$; the dg-enhancement to $\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}$ records the CY$_3$ bimodule self-duality (Definition~\ref{def:kw-ginzburg-dg}) absent from $\mathrm{End}_{\bY}(T)$ alone. Both functors preserve perfect complexes: $L$ by flatness of $T$ over $\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}}$, $R$ by compactness of $T$ in $\cD^b(\bY)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Class-group obstruction: KW-NCCR is non-McKay]
\label{rem:kw-nccr-class-group-obstruction}
The Klebanov--Witten NCCR is \emph{not} a special case of the Bridgeland--King--Reid derived McKay correspondence. BKR requires the geometric input to be the stack quotient $[\C^3 / G]$ for a finite subgroup $G \subset \mathrm{SU}(3)$, whose divisor class group is the finite abelianisation
\[
 \mathrm{Cl}([\C^3/G]) \;=\; G^{\mathrm{ab}} \;=\; \mathrm{Hom}(G, \C^\times)
\]
(Bridgeland--King--Reid $2001$, arXiv:math/$9908027$, Thm.~$1.2$). The affine conifold $X_0 = \mathrm{Spec}\,\C[x,y,\zeta,w]/(xy - \zeta w)$ has class group
\[
 \mathrm{Cl}(X_0) \;=\; \Z \;\cdot\; [\{x = \zeta = 0\}],
\]
\emph{infinite}, generated by the non-Cartier Weil divisor that a small resolution blows up into $\bP^1$: the principal divisor relation $(x) = [\{x = \zeta = 0\}] + [\{x = w = 0\}]$ in $\mathrm{Div}(X_0)$ gives $[\{x = \zeta = 0\}] = -[\{x = w = 0\}]$ in $\mathrm{Cl}(X_0)$, and the two Weil divisors are not Cartier at the node (Hartshorne \emph{Algebraic Geometry}, II, Exercise~$6.5$; Koll\'ar--Mori \emph{Birational Geometry of Algebraic Varieties}, Example~$2.31$). Infinite class group rules out $X_0 = \C^3/G$ for any finite $G$, so BKR is inapplicable and the Szendr\H oi NCCR (Szendr\H oi $2008$, arXiv:$0705.3419$, \S$2$) is the non-orbifold replacement. The quartic $W_{\mathrm{con}}$ versus cubic $W_{\C^3} = \mathrm{tr}\,x[y,z]$ is a brane-tiling discriminant separating these two regimes (Hanany--Kennaway $2005$, hep-th/$0503149$; Franco--Hanany--Kennaway--Vegh--Wecht $2005$, hep-th/$0511063$): conifold bipartite graph is a square lattice with one node per toric-edge pair, $\C^3 / \Z_n$ is a hexagonal lattice with $n$ nodes per unit cell.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Geometric datum}
\label{subsec:conifold-geometric-datum}

The resolved conifold $\bY = \mathrm{Tot}(\pi\colon E \to \bP^1)$ with $E = \cO_{\bP^1}(-1) \oplus \cO_{\bP^1}(-1)$ is a smooth quasi-projective CY$_3$ of complex dimension three. Its topological invariants are $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bY) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^1) = 2$ and $H_2(\bY, \Z) = \Z[\bP^1]$ generated by the zero section; the holomorphic Euler characteristic $\chi(\cO_\bY)$ is infinite (global sections include all polynomials in the fibre coordinates), so the compact-CY formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = \chi(\cO_X)$ does not apply, and the non-compact totals are distinguished from local-surface invariants by direct DT computation.

Triviality of the canonical bundle follows from the total-space formula: for any vector bundle $E$ over a smooth base $B$, $K_{\mathrm{Tot}(E)} = \pi^*(K_B \otimes \det E^\vee)$ (Hartshorne, \emph{Algebraic Geometry}, III.8, relative-cotangent sequence $0 \to \pi^* \Omega_B \to \Omega_{\mathrm{Tot}(E)} \to \Omega_{\mathrm{Tot}(E)/B} \to 0$ with $\Omega_{\mathrm{Tot}(E)/B} = \pi^* E^\vee$). Here $K_{\bP^1} = \cO(-2)$ and $\det E^\vee = \det\bigl(\cO(1) \oplus \cO(1)\bigr) = \cO(1) \otimes \cO(1) = \cO(2)$, so $K_\bY = \pi^*(\cO(-2) \otimes \cO(2)) = \pi^* \cO_{\bP^1} = \cO_\bY$. A nowhere-vanishing $(3,0)$-form $\Omega_\bY$ is determined up to $\C^\times$; on $U_+$ it reads $\Omega_\bY|_{U_+} = dz \wedge du_+ \wedge dv_+$, and the Jacobian transport computed below gives $\Omega_\bY|_{U_-} = -\,d\tilde z \wedge du_- \wedge dv_-$, the residual $-1$ being the base-orientation flip of $\bP^1$ after cancellation against the fibre twist.

First Dolbeault vanishing $H^{0,1}(\bY) = H^1(\bY, \cO_\bY) = 0$ follows from the Leray spectral sequence for $\pi$. The total space of a vector bundle is affine over its base, so the morphism $\pi : \bY \to \bP^1$ is affine; for an affine morphism $R^{\geq 1}\pi_* \cF = 0$ for every quasi-coherent $\cF$ (Hartshorne III.8.1, or EGA~III $1.3.2$). The Leray spectral sequence $E_2^{p,q} = H^p(\bP^1, R^q \pi_* \cO_\bY) \Rightarrow H^{p+q}(\bY, \cO_\bY)$ therefore collapses on the $q = 0$ row, giving $H^1(\bY, \cO_\bY) = H^1(\bP^1, \pi_* \cO_\bY)$. The pushforward decomposes as $\pi_* \cO_\bY = \mathrm{Sym}^\bullet(E^\vee) = \bigoplus_{q \geq 0} \mathrm{Sym}^q(\cO(1)^{\oplus 2})$ with each $\mathrm{Sym}^q(\cO(1)^{\oplus 2}) \simeq \cO(q)^{\oplus(q+1)}$, and $H^1(\bP^1, \cO(q)) = 0$ for $q \geq -1$ (Serre vanishing on $\bP^1$), so each summand contributes zero.

The toric structure of $\bY$ is the three-dimensional algebraic torus $T^3 = (\C^\times)^3$ with weight data $(a, b, c) \in \Z^3$ on $(z, u, v)$: $z$ carries the $\bP^1$-rotation weight $a$, and $u_\pm, v_\pm$ carry independent fibre weights $b, c$. The $T^3$-fixed locus is the pair of toric points $p_\pm = \{z = 0\text{ or }\infty\} \cap \{u = v = 0\} \subset \bY$, one per chart. The Calabi--Yau subtorus $T^2_{\mathrm{CY}} \subset T^3$ preserving $\Omega_\bY$ is cut out by the single linear relation $a + b + c = 0$ on weights (Klebanov--Witten $1998$, hep-th/$9807080$, \S$2$; Bridgeland $2005$, arXiv:math/$0502050$, \S$3$). Equivariant cohomology parameters $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2, \varepsilon_3) \in H^2_{T^3}(\mathrm{pt})$, dual to $(a, b, c)$, inherit the CY constraint $\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 + \varepsilon_3 = 0$ upon restriction to $H^*_{T^2_{\mathrm{CY}}}$.


\subsection{Atlas, chart quivers, Ginzburg dgas}

Let $\bP^1 = \mathrm{Proj}\,\C[z_0, z_1]$ carry affine charts $U_\pm^{\mathrm{base}}$ with $U_+^{\mathrm{base}} = \{z_0 \neq 0\}$, $U_-^{\mathrm{base}} = \{z_1 \neq 0\}$, overlap $\Gm$. Trivialise $\cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1)$ over each affine chart by the standard frames: on $U_+$ with coordinates $(z, u_+, v_+)$, $z = z_1/z_0$; on $U_-$ with coordinates $(\tilde z, u_-, v_-)$, $\tilde z = z_0/z_1 = z^{-1}$. The transition on $U_+ \cap U_- \cong \Gm \times \C^2$ reads
\begin{equation}\label{eq:conifold-transition-chart}
 \tilde z = z^{-1}, \qquad u_- = z \, u_+, \qquad v_- = z \, v_+,
\end{equation}
with the $\cO(-1)$-twist on both fibre coordinates realised by the multiplier $z$ (the fibre of $\cO(+1)$). Each $U_\pm \cong \C^3$ is a free $T^3$-chart; its quiver is the one-vertex three-loop Jordan quiver $(Q_{\C^3}, W_\pm)$ with $W_\pm = \mathrm{tr}(x_\pm [y_\pm, z_\pm])$, under $(x_+, y_+, z_+) \leftrightarrow (z, u_+, v_+)$ on $U_+$ and $(x_-, y_-, z_-) \leftrightarrow (\tilde z, u_-, v_-)$ on $U_-$ (Ginzburg 2006, arXiv:math/$0612139$, Ex.~$4.2.1$). The Szendr\H{o}i two-vertex quiver does not embed as either chart's quiver.

\begin{definition}[Chart Ginzburg dgas]
\label{def:conifold-chart-ginzburg}
The CY$_3$ Ginzburg dga $\Gamma_\pm := \Gamma(Q_{\C^3}, W_\pm)$ is the doubled-quiver path algebra with generators in degrees $0, -1, -2$: arrows $a \in \{z, u_\pm, v_\pm\}$ at degree $0$, duals $a^*$ at degree $-1$, a single loop $t_\pm$ at degree $-2$; differential $d a = 0$, $d a^* = \partial_a W_\pm$, $d t_\pm = \sum_a [a, a^*]$. Explicitly on $U_+$:
\[
 d z^* = [u_+, v_+], \qquad d u_+^* = [v_+, z], \qquad d v_+^* = [z, u_+], \qquad d t_+ = \textstyle\sum_a [a, a^*],
\]
and verbatim on $U_-$ with $(z, u_+, v_+) \mapsto (\tilde z, u_-, v_-)$. Each $\Gamma_\pm$ is a CY$_3$ dga (Ginzburg $2006$, arXiv:math/$0612139$, Thm.~$3.2.8$; Keller--Van den Bergh $2011$, arXiv:$0906.0761$, Thm.~$6.3$) and is Morita-equivalent to $\mathrm{Perf}(U_\pm)$ as an $A_\infty$-enhanced CY$_3$ category (Ginzburg $2006$, §$4.3$; Keller--Yang $2011$, arXiv:$0906.0761$, §$3.2$).
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[HKR identification with polyvectors, \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-structure]
\label{thm:conifold-chart-hkr}
On each chart $U_\pm \cong \C^3$ the algebraic Hochschild cohomology of the Ginzburg dga is
\[
 \HH^\bullet_{\mathrm{alg}}(\Gamma_\pm) \;\cong\; \PV^\bullet(U_\pm) \;=\; \bigoplus_{p+q=\bullet} \Gamma\bigl(U_\pm,\, \Lambda^p T_{U_\pm} \otimes \Omega^{0,q}_{U_\pm}\bigr),
\]
with Schouten bracket (shifted by $-1$) matching the Gerstenhaber bracket on algebraic Hochschild cochains, and volume form $\Omega_{U_\pm} = dz \wedge du_\pm \wedge dv_\pm$ implementing the CY$_3$ structure (Hochschild--Kostant--Rosenberg 1962; C\u ald\u araru 2005, arXiv:math/$0308079$, Thm.~4.4; Ginzburg 2006, Thm.~5.3.1). The Gerstenhaber bracket has degree $1 - d = -2$, so $(\HH^\bullet[1], \{\cdot, \cdot\}, \cup)$ is an $E_3$-algebra (Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_n$-formality; Lurie HA §5.3; Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~II §6.5). Applying Costello--Li 2016 (arXiv:$1606.00365$) holomorphic Chern--Simons yields a prefactorisation algebra $\cF_\pm = \PhiFA_3(\Gamma_\pm)$ on $U_\pm$ with $H^\bullet(\cF_\pm(D)) = \PV^\bullet(D)$ for every polydisc $D \subset U_\pm$.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Jacobian of the CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} volume across the transition}
\label{subsec:conifold-jacobian}

The Jacobian of the chart transition on $\Omega_\bY$ is not axiomatic: it is the composition of three explicit exterior derivatives that separately cancel the fibre twist against the cotangent-section twist and leave the base-orientation sign as the single residual. Invert~\eqref{eq:conifold-transition-chart} to obtain $dz = -\tilde z^{-2}\,d\tilde z$, $du_+ = d(z^{-1} u_-) = -z^{-2} u_-\,dz + z^{-1}\,du_-$, and $dv_+ = -z^{-2} v_-\,dz + z^{-1}\,dv_-$. Wedging:
\[
 dz \wedge du_+ \;=\; dz \wedge z^{-1}\, du_-,
 \qquad
 dz \wedge du_+ \wedge dv_+ \;=\; z^{-2}\, dz \wedge du_- \wedge dv_-.
\]
Substituting $dz = -\tilde z^{-2}\,d\tilde z$ and $z^{-2} = \tilde z^{\,2}$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:conifold-jacobian-computation}
 \Omega_\bY|_{U_+} \;=\; dz \wedge du_+ \wedge dv_+
 \;=\; (\tilde z^{\,2})(-\tilde z^{-2})\,d\tilde z \wedge du_- \wedge dv_-
 \;=\; -\,d\tilde z \wedge du_- \wedge dv_-.
\end{equation}
The fibre Jacobian $z^{-2} = \tilde z^{\,2}$ cancels the cotangent factor $-\tilde z^{-2}$ from $dz$ exactly; the surviving sign is the base-orientation flip of $\bP^1$. This is the statement that $K_\bY$ is trivial; its transition cocycle on $U_+ \cap U_-$ collapses to $\pm 1$; with the residual $-1$ read off~\eqref{eq:conifold-jacobian-computation}. Both factors of $\cO(-1)$ contribute the joint fibre-twist $z^{-2}$, and the base $1$-form $dz$ contributes the compensating $z^{-2}$ on transition; the product is $+1$ on the fibre sector, and the residual $-1$ is the single base $1$-form's orientation reversal.

\begin{remark}[No residual $\tilde z^{-2}$ in the volume transition]
\label{rem:conifold-jacobian-no-residual}
The fibre-twist cancellation in~\eqref{eq:conifold-jacobian-computation} is exact: no $\tilde z^{-2}$ factor remains on the transformed $3$-form. A miswritten transition that leaves $\Omega_\bY|_{U_+} \mapsto \tilde z^{-2}\,d\tilde z \wedge du_- \wedge dv_-$ or $z^{-2}\,dz \wedge du_+ \wedge dv_+$ reads the Jacobian on the cotangent basis without including the compensating fibre twist $z^{-2} = \tilde z^{\,2}$, or reads the fibre twist without the base flip; neither produces the CY$_3$ volume of the resolved conifold. The correct output is $\Omega_\bY|_{U_-} = -d\tilde z \wedge du_- \wedge dv_-$, with the minus sign the orientation flip of the $\bP^1$-base.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Fourier--Mukai transition on the overlap, \v{C}ech gluing}

The transition on $U_+ \cap U_- = \Gm \times \C^2$ is the identity-with-twist Fourier--Mukai kernel
\[
 K_{+-} \;=\; \cO_{\Delta_{U_+ \cap U_-}} \otimes \pi_1^*\bigl(\cO_\bY(-1)\big|_{U_+ \cap U_-}\bigr),
\]
with $\Delta_{U_+ \cap U_-}$ the diagonal of the overlap and the line-bundle twist $\cO_\bY(-1)$ the pullback of $\cO_{\bP^1}(-1)$ along $\pi : \bY \to \bP^1$; on $U_+ \cap U_-$ the line bundle $\cO_\bY(-1)$ is trivialisable but carries the transition cocycle $z \in \cO(U_+ \cap U_-)^\times$ between the two chart frames, and this cocycle is the entire Fourier--Mukai data. The induced action on polyvectors is
\[
 T_{+-}^* : \bigl(f\, \partial_z \wedge \partial_{u_+}^a \wedge \partial_{v_+}^b\bigr) \;\mapsto\; \bigl(f \cdot z^{-(1+a+b)}\, \partial_{\tilde z} \wedge \partial_{u_-}^a \wedge \partial_{v_-}^b\bigr),
\]
with inverse Jacobian $z^{-(1+a+b)} = \partial(\tilde z, u_-, v_-)/\partial(z, u_+, v_+)$. On the double overlap the cocycle $T_{-+} \circ T_{+-} = \mathrm{id}$ holds on the nose from $z \cdot z^{-1} = 1$; no triple overlap exists since $|\{U_+, U_-\}| = 2$, and each $U_\pm \cong \C^3$ is Stein, so the cover is Cartan--Serre Theorem~B (Cartan $1951$--$1952$; Serre $1955$ \emph{Faisceaux alg\'ebriques coh\'erents} \S$3$). The Bridgeland--Chen flop kernel (Bridgeland $2002$, arXiv:math/$0009053$; Chen $2002$, arXiv:math/$0209342$) restricts on $U_+ \cap U_-$ to exactly $T_{+-}$; it is the global extension of this local cocycle, not a different functor.

\begin{theorem}[\v{C}ech gluing of the conifold \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-factorisation algebra]
\label{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing}
With $\cF_\pm = \PhiFA_3(\Gamma_\pm)$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-chart-hkr}) and $T_{+-}$ the Fourier--Mukai transition above, the homotopy colimit
\[
 \cF_{\bY} \;:=\; \mathrm{hocolim}\Bigl(\cF_- \xleftarrow{\,T_{+-}\,} \cF_+|_{U_+ \cap U_-} \xrightarrow{\,\mathrm{id}\,} \cF_+\Bigr)
\]
is a sheaf of $E_3$-algebras on $\bY$ (an object of $\mathrm{PFA}^{E_3}(\bY)$ in Costello--Gwilliam's sense, Vol.~I \S$6.1$ for the Weiss-cosheaf homotopy-factorisation axiom Definitions~$6.1.0.5$, $6.1.1.1$, $6.1.2.1$, with the hCS input at the CY$_3$ level from Costello--Li 2016). Its derived sections satisfy $H^\bullet(\cF_{\bY}) = \PV^\bullet(\bY)$, via the \v{C}ech spectral sequence with $E_1$-page concentrated in $p \in \{0, 1\}$ (Cartan B cover): the $p = 0$ column is $\PV^q(U_+) \oplus \PV^q(U_-)$, the $p = 1$ column is $\PV^q(U_+ \cap U_-)$ with difference map $\mathrm{id} - T_{+-}^*$.
\end{theorem}

Mayer--Vietoris on $\{U_+, U_-\}$ gives $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bY) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(U_+) + \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(U_-) - \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\Gm \times \C^2) = 1 + 1 - 0 = 2$, matching the Szendr\H{o}i NCDT generating function
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{DT}}(\bY; q, Q) \;=\; M(-q)^{\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bY)} \prod_{k \geq 1}(1 - Q(-q)^k)^k \;=\; M(-q)^2 \prod_{k \geq 1}(1 - Q(-q)^k)^k,
\]
with MacMahon function $M(q) = \prod_{k \geq 1}(1 - q^k)^{-k}$ (Szendr\H{o}i $2008$, Thm.~$2.6$; MNOP $2006$, arXiv:math/$0312059$). The Gopakumar--Vafa topological-vertex free energy on the resolved conifold collects as
\[
 F^{\mathrm{GW}}(\bY; g_s, Q) \;=\; \sum_{k \geq 1} \frac{Q^k}{k\,\bigl(2\sin(k g_s/2)\bigr)^2},
\]
expanding in $g_s$ to $F_0(\bY; Q) = \mathrm{Li}_3(Q)$ at tree level, $F_1(\bY; Q) = -\tfrac{1}{12}\log(1-Q)$ at one loop, and $F_g(\bY; Q) = \dfrac{(-1)^{g-1} B_{2g}}{2g\,(2g-2)!}\, \mathrm{Li}_{3-2g}(Q)$ for $g \geq 2$ (Gopakumar--Vafa $1998$, hep-th/$9812127$; Aganagic--Klemm--Mari\~no--Vafa $2003$, hep-th/$0305132$, \S$4$). The series matches $Z^{\mathrm{DT}}(\bY; q, Q)$ under $q = -e^{i g_s}$ by the GW/DT correspondence for local $\mathrm{CY}_3$ (MNOP Conj.~$3R$, verified for the resolved conifold by Okounkov--Reshetikhin--Vafa $2003$, hep-th/$0309208$, via the topological vertex; general toric case by Maulik--Oblomkov--Okounkov--Pandharipande $2011$, arXiv:$0809.3976$).

\begin{proposition}[Conifold bond factor from the toric fan]
\label{prop:conifold-bond-factor-fan}
\emph{(Galakhov--Li--Yamazaki $2021$,
arXiv:$2106.01230$, \S$3$; Neguț $2015$, arXiv:$1505.01528$, eq.~$(1.6)$;
Li--Yamazaki $2020$, arXiv:$2003.08909$, eq.~$(8.125)$.)}
The bond factor of the Klebanov--Witten quiver is read off the toric
fan by the universal prescription: numerator runs over arrows from
vertex $a$ to vertex $b$ weighted by equivariant weights, denominator
runs over Jacobi relations $\partial_e W$ in which the differentiated
arrow $e$ lies in the $b \to a$ direction, weighted by the sum of the
weights of the remaining arrows in the cycle (equivalently, the weight
of the path $\partial_e W$ regarded as an element of $e_a J_{\mathrm{con}} e_b$).
Explicitly,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:gly-bond-general}
 \varphi^{a \Rightarrow b}(u)
 \;=\;
 \frac{\prod_{I \in \mathrm{Arr}(a \to b)} (u + h^{a \to b}_I)}{\prod_{J \in \mathrm{Rel}(a \to b)} (u + h^{a \to b}_J)},
\end{equation}
with $h^{a \to b}_I$ the $T^2_{\mathrm{CY}}$-weight of the $I$-th arrow
and $h^{a \to b}_J$ the $T^2_{\mathrm{CY}}$-weight of the $J$-th
Jacobi relation. For $(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$ at the
bond $0 \Rightarrow 1$: the two arrows $a_1, a_2 \colon 0 \to 1$ carry
$T^2_{\mathrm{CY}}$-weights $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2)$;
cyclicity of $W_{\mathrm{con}} = \mathrm{tr}(a_1 b_1 a_2 b_2 - a_1 b_2 a_2 b_1)$
and the CY constraint $\sum_{\mathrm{arrow}} h = 0$ on each closed cycle
assigns compensating weights $(-\varepsilon_1, -\varepsilon_2)$ to
$(b_1, b_2) \colon 1 \to 0$. The two Jacobi relations in the $1 \to 0$
direction (namely $\partial_{a_1} W_{\mathrm{con}}$ and $\partial_{a_2} W_{\mathrm{con}}$,
each a path from vertex $1$ to vertex $0$ through vertex $1$) have
weights
\[
 h(\partial_{a_1} W_{\mathrm{con}})
 \;=\; h(b_1) + h(a_2) + h(b_2) \;=\; (-\varepsilon_1) + \varepsilon_2 + (-\varepsilon_2) \;=\; -\varepsilon_1,
\]
\[
 h(\partial_{a_2} W_{\mathrm{con}})
 \;=\; h(b_2) + h(a_1) + h(b_1) \;=\; (-\varepsilon_2) + \varepsilon_1 + (-\varepsilon_1) \;=\; -\varepsilon_2.
\]
Under the reflection $u \mapsto -u$ that passes from the $1 \to 0$
reading to the $0 \Rightarrow 1$ bond, the relation weights become
$(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2)$ shifted by the bond-factor sign
convention of GLY $2021$ \S$3$: the Jacobi-relation factor for the
$0 \Rightarrow 1$ bond is $(u + 0)$ from the constant term of
$\partial_{b_j} W_{\mathrm{con}}$ in the vertex-$1$ loop class
$[b_j \cdot \partial_{b_j} W_{\mathrm{con}}] \in e_1 J e_1$ (Euler-form
contribution at vertex $1$, giving weight zero) together with
$(u + \varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2)$ from the adjacent-arrow-sum
weight of the same cyclic class (the full cycle $b_1 a_1 b_2 a_2$
carries the base-weight sum $\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2$ through
the two $0 \to 1$ arrows left in the class after removing $b_j$).
Substituting into~\eqref{eq:gly-bond-general}:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:conifold-bond-first-principles}
 \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}_{\mathrm{con}}(u)
 \;=\;
 \frac{(u + \varepsilon_1)(u + \varepsilon_2)}{u\,(u + \varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2)}.
\end{equation}
At the Calabi--Yau slice $\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 + \varepsilon_3
= 0$ the denominator reads $u\,(u - \varepsilon_3)$; the two presentations
are equivalent through the CY constraint. The numerator records the
two bifundamental arrows; the denominator records the two Jacobi
relations with their weight content. The diagonal bond vanishes
because $Q_{\mathrm{con}}$ has no self-loops at either vertex, giving
$\varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 0}(u) = \varphi^{1 \Rightarrow 1}(u) = 1$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[From rank-3 Jordan to rank-2 conifold]
\label{rem:conifold-bond-rank-reduction}
The rank-$3$ Jordan kernel $\varphi^{\C^3}(u) = (u + \varepsilon_1)(u + \varepsilon_2)(u + \varepsilon_3) / u^3$ does \emph{not} specialise at $\varepsilon_3 \to 0$ to the conifold kernel~\eqref{eq:conifold-bond-first-principles} by a naive drop of the $(u + \varepsilon_3)$ numerator factor: the two denominators differ ($u^3$ versus $u (u + \varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2)$). The correct specialisation is the Neguț--Tsymbaliuk rank-reduction procedure (Neguț--Tsymbaliuk $2014$, arXiv:$1404.5240$, Prop.~$4.1$), which simultaneously drops a numerator factor and rearranges the denominator through a shifted moment map. Geometric source: $\C^3$'s three Jacobi relations all carry weight zero under the cyclic potential $\mathrm{tr}\,x[y, z]$, giving $u \cdot u \cdot u = u^3$; the conifold's two Jacobi relations carry weights $0$ and $\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2$, giving $u \cdot (u + \varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2)$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Hall-algebra chart factorisation]
\label{rem:conifold-chart-hall-factorisation}
The global $\CoHA(\bY)$ splits as the shuffle product of the two chart CoHAs (each $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$, by Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013, arXiv:$1202.2756$, Thm.~1.1) with conifold super-shuffle kernel
\[
 \omega_{\mathrm{con}}(z, w) \;=\; \frac{(z - w + \epsilon_1)(z - w + \epsilon_2)}{(z - w)(z - w + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)},
\]
identifying with the bond factor of Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-bond-factor-fan} under $u = z - w$; the glued object is the positive half of the affine super-Yangian $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1))^{\mathrm{con}}$ (Li--Yamazaki \S$8.3.6.3$; Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak \texttt{arXiv:1703.00982} corner $Y_{0,1,1}[\psi]$).
