The $(-1, -1)$-splitting of the conifold forces the super-Yangian on the odd Chevalley direction; the alternative toric $\mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-2) \oplus \cO \to \bP^1)$ with the same $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bY) = 2$ realises quantum-toroidal $\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_2$ instead ( stratification; same Euler characteristic, different NCCR, different algebra). The non-trivial datum the $\C^3$ case lacks is the $T_{+-}$-twist on $\Gm \times \C^2$, which carries $c_1(\cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1)) = -2 = c_1(K_{\bP^1})$ into global polyvectors via Mayer--Vietoris.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Szendr\H{o}i / \v{C}ech reconciliation]
\label{rem:szendroi-cech-reconciliation}
The two-vertex NCCR algebra (Definition~\ref{def:jacobi-kw-centre}) decomposes against the chart atlas as
\[
 J_{\mathrm{con}} \;\cong\; \mathrm{End}_{\bY}\bigl(\cO_{\bY} \oplus \cO_{\bY}(1)\bigr) \;\cong\; \mathrm{hocolim}_{\{U_+, U_-\}}\, J_\pm,
\]
where $J_\pm = J(Q_{\C^3}, W_\pm) \cong \C[z, u_\pm, v_\pm]$ is the \emph{abelianised} Jacobi algebra of the single-vertex Jordan-triple quiver: the three Jacobi relations $[u_\pm, v_\pm] = [v_\pm, z] = [z, u_\pm] = 0$ are non-commutative relations in the path algebra that collapse on the rank-one matrix locus to commutativity. The homotopy colimit lifts this abelianisation to the two-vertex Klebanov--Witten global algebra: the chart arrows pair off across the overlap into the bifundamental $a_i, b_j$ of Definition~\ref{def:kw-quiver-superpotential}, the three Jordan-triple cubic relations per chart reassemble into the four Klebanov--Witten quartic relations, and the restricted tilting bundle $T|_{U_\pm} = \cO_{U_\pm}^{\oplus 2}$ witnesses the derived Morita equivalence $\cD^b(\bY) \leftrightarrow \mathrm{Perf}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{con}})$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:kw-derived-morita-adjoint} chart by chart.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Toric scope, contrast with \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{rem:conifold-toric-scope}
This construction uses $T^2$-equivariance on each chart's fibre directions (the $\C^3$ has $T^3$-symmetry, with CY$_3$ forcing $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 = 0$ for the fibre $T^2$ and the compact $\bP^1$-direction carrying no equivariant parameter). It is specific to toric local CY$_3$ and does not propagate to non-toric compact CY$_3$ such as the quintic or $K3 \times E$, which admit no toric atlas of this form and require the Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande categorified machinery or Kontsevich--Soibelman motivic DT on the non-toric side. The $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\bY) = 1$ value (Corollary~\ref{cor:conifold-shadow}) is $\mathrm{DT}_{(1,0)}(\bY) = 1$, the single-compact-curve contribution of the zero section; the topological Euler number $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bY) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^1) = 2$ is the Mayer--Vietoris count on the two-chart cover; the holomorphic Euler characteristic $\chi(\cO_\bY)$ is infinite (non-compact total space, global sections include all fibre polynomials), so a compact-type identification $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(\bY) \stackrel{?}{=} \chi(\cO_\bY)$ does not apply here. The three invariants $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\bY) = 1$, $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bY) = 2$, and $\chi(\cO_\bY) = \infty$ are distinct content, not three readings of a single invariant.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Descent target: quasi-coherent sheaves versus factorisation locality}
\label{subsec:conifold-weiss-vs-cech}

The two-chart atlas $\{U_+, U_-\}$ meets two distinct descent targets with disjoint locality requirements. On the quasi-coherent side, Cartan--Serre Theorem~B (Stein covers) together with Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008 (arXiv:$1006.2706$, §$6.3$, motivic-DT descent) supplies the refinement under which $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)$ descends as a sheaf of algebras on $\bY$. On the factorisation-algebra side, Costello--Gwilliam \emph{Factorization Algebras in Quantum Field Theory} Vol.~I, CUP~$2021$, Definition~$6.1.0.5$ imposes the \emph{Weiss condition}: a cover $\{V_\alpha\}$ of $X$ is Weiss when every finite set of points $\{x_1, \ldots, x_k\} \subset X$ embeds \emph{simultaneously} into a single $V_\alpha$. This fails for $\{U_+, U_-\}$ on $\bY$: a configuration with one point on $U_+ \setminus U_-$ and one on $U_- \setminus U_+$ admits no single chart that contains both.

\begin{proposition}[Two-chart cover refines to Weiss only through disjoint-union closure]
\label{prop:conifold-weiss-refinement}
Let $\mathfrak U = \{U_+, U_-\}$ be the two-chart atlas of the resolved conifold $\bY$. The Weiss refinement $\mathfrak U^\sqcup$ is the cover generated by all disjoint unions of contractible opens, each contained in $U_+$ or in $U_-$; it is strictly finer than~$\mathfrak U$ and recovers the Weiss condition by construction (Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I Definition~$6.1.0.6$, the \emph{generates} relation $\mathfrak U \rightsquigarrow \mathfrak U^\sqcup$). The canonical map
\[
 \cF_{\bY}^{\mathrm{QC}} \;:=\; \mathrm{hocolim}_{\mathfrak U}\, \cF_\pm
 \qquad\xrightarrow{\;\iota^\sqcup\;}\qquad
 \cF_{\bY}^{\mathrm{FA}} \;:=\; \mathrm{hocolim}_{\mathfrak U^\sqcup}\, \cF_\pm
\]
is \emph{not} an equivalence: the left-hand side is the quasi-coherent descent object (Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing} above, read as QC-descent), while the right-hand side is the $E_3$-prefactorisation algebra on~$\bY$ obtained by the homotopy-cosheaf axiom (Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I Definitions~$6.1.1.1$ and~$6.1.2.1$). The factorisation product $m_{x_+, x_-} : \cF(U_+' \sqcup U_-') \to \cF(\bY)$ for a disjoint union $U_+' \sqcup U_-'$ with $U_\pm' \subset U_\pm$ is visible only on the Weiss side; on the two-chart side it is invisible because $\{U_+, U_-\}$ contains no disjoint-union open of this type.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Scope and citation]
The Weiss refinement $\mathfrak U \mapsto \mathfrak U^\sqcup$ is Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I Definition~$6.1.0.6$; the equivalence of prefactorisation algebras satisfying the homotopy cosheaf axiom on Weiss covers with factorisation algebras on $\bY$ is the content of Definition~$6.1.2.1$ and \S$6.1.2$ of the same volume. This is the local-to-global axiom of \cite[\S 6.1.1--6.1.2]{CostelloGwilliam}, extending Beilinson--Drinfeld \emph{Chiral Algebras} (AMS~51, Chapter~$3$; the factorisation/chiral-Lie equivalence is Theorem~$3.4.9$), Francis--Gaitsgory 2011 (arXiv:$1103.5803$, Theorem~$1.2.4$, the chiral Koszul equivalence $\mathrm{Lie}\text{-alg}^{\mathrm{ch}} \simeq \mathrm{Com}\text{-coalg}^{\mathrm{ch,fact}}$), Francis 2013 (arXiv:$1303.0305$), and Lurie \emph{Higher Algebra} §$5.5.4$. The Kontsevich--Soibelman motivic-DT descent (arXiv:$1006.2706$, §$6.3$) operates at the QC-sheaf level; it does not produce the Weiss data. Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing} above reads correctly at both levels provided the descent target is declared: $\cF_{\bY}^{\mathrm{QC}}$ for the sheaf-of-algebras statement, $\cF_{\bY}^{\mathrm{FA}}$ for the factorisation-algebra statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Orientation: two load-bearing objects]
\label{rem:conifold-qc-fa-distinction}
The CoHA on the resolved conifold admits two parallel constructions through the same chart atlas. Quasi-coherent descent through $\{U_+, U_-\}$ produces $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)^{\mathrm{QC}}$: a sheaf of associative $\Eone$-algebras on $\bY$ whose stalk at a point of $U_\pm$ is the Ginzburg CoHA of $(Q_{\C^3}, W_\pm)$, with transition data the Fourier--Mukai twist $T_{+-}$ of Section~\ref{sec:conifold-two-chart-cech}. Factorisation locality through $\mathfrak U^\sqcup$ produces $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)^{\mathrm{FA}}$: an $E_3$-prefactorisation algebra recording both the chart-local $\Eone$-products and the disjoint-configuration $m_{x_+, x_-}$-products. The two coincide on their common overlap (single-chart configurations) but differ on genuinely two-chart configurations, where the $E_3$-factorisation product is the unique datum. Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing} and Remark~\ref{rem:conifold-chart-hall-factorisation} apply to $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)^{\mathrm{QC}}$; the factorisation-algebra counterpart requires Weiss refinement as Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-weiss-refinement} records.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Homotopy colimit is computed in the $(2,1)$-category of prestacks]
\label{rem:conifold-hocolim-2cat}
The homotopy colimits in Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing} and Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-weiss-refinement} are computed in the $(\infty, 1)$-category of presheaves of $\En$-algebras on $\bY$, equivalently in the $(2, 1)$-truncation given by the $2$-category of dg categories with $1$-morphisms dg functors and $2$-morphisms natural transformations. A $1$-categorical colimit (coequaliser of $\cF_+|_{U_+ \cap U_-} \rightrightarrows \cF_+ \times \cF_-$) produces a strict gluing that ignores the $T_{+-}$-twist up to coherent homotopy; it is the wrong invariant. The $(2,1)$-lift keeps the Fourier--Mukai kernel as a $1$-morphism and records its composition with itself (the cocycle $T_{-+} \circ T_{+-} \simeq \mathrm{id}$) as a coherent $2$-cell. Toën--Vezzosi (arXiv:math/$0404373$, §$1.4$) establishes the $(2,1)$-descent statement for dg categories; Lurie \emph{Higher Algebra} §$5.5.4$ gives the $(\infty,1)$-refinement. The distinction is load-bearing here because $T_{+-}$ is a non-trivial $\cO(-1)$-twist; the strict $1$-coequaliser destroys the twist data.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[$\mathrm{CY}_3$ volume unimodular across the transition]
\label{rem:conifold-jacobian-clean}
Reading~\eqref{eq:conifold-jacobian-computation} intrinsically: the transition matrix
$J(\tilde z, u_-, v_-; z, u_+, v_+) = \mathrm{diag}(-\tilde z^{-2}, \tilde z, \tilde z)$
on the $1$-form basis has $\det J = -\tilde z^{-2} \cdot \tilde z \cdot \tilde z = -1$,
exactly the unimodular condition $|\det J| = 1$ required for $K_\bY$ triviality. The
fibre-twist multiplier $\tilde z \cdot \tilde z = \tilde z^2 = z^{-2}$ cancels the
base-cotangent multiplier $-\tilde z^{-2} = -z^2$; the residual $-1$ is the
$\bP^1$-orientation reversal under $z \mapsto z^{-1}$. No residual $\tilde z^{-2}$
factor appears on the transformed $3$-form: the Fourier--Mukai kernel $K_{+-} = \cO_\Delta \otimes \pi_1^* \cO(-1)$ of~\eqref{eq:conifold-transition-chart} carries the complete twist data, and the unimodularity of the CY$_3$ volume is not an additional condition.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The Ran-space formulation and the factorisation cosheaf}
\label{subsec:conifold-ran-space-formulation}

The Beilinson--Drinfeld formulation of factorisation algebras is Ran-space-native: a factorisation algebra on $\bY$ is a cosheaf on the Ran space $\Ran(\bY) = \colim_{I} \bY^I / \mathrm{diag}$ of finite-configuration spaces with diagonal collapse maps (Beilinson--Drinfeld \emph{Chiral Algebras}, AMS~$51$, §$3.4.11$; the factorisation/chiral-Lie equivalence is Theorem~$3.4.9$ of the same volume, and Francis--Gaitsgory arXiv:$1103.5803$ Theorem~$1.2.4$ identifies chiral Lie algebras $\mathrm{Lie}\text{-alg}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\Ran X)$ with factorisation coalgebras via the chiral-homology functor $C^{\mathrm{ch}}$ and its left adjoint $\mathrm{Prim}^{\mathrm{ch}}[1]$). The Weiss cover axiom is precisely the condition for a cover to interact coherently with the Ran-space geometry: a Weiss cover $\{V_\alpha\}$ of $\bY$ induces an \emph{open cover} of each configuration space $\bY^{[k]} = \bY^k / \Sigma_k$ by opens $V_{\alpha}^{[k]}$ with points all in the same~$V_\alpha$, and these assemble to a Ran-space cover under the colimit.

\begin{proposition}[Ran-space test for the two-chart cover]
\label{prop:conifold-ran-test}
For $k \geq 2$, the configuration space $\bY^{[k]}$ is not covered by $U_+^{[k]} \cup U_-^{[k]}$: configurations with points in both $U_+ \setminus U_-$ and $U_- \setminus U_+$ lie in neither $U_+^{[k]}$ nor $U_-^{[k]}$. The Weiss refinement $\{U_+, U_-\}^\sqcup$ remedies this by including disjoint-union opens $V_+ \sqcup V_-$ that separately accommodate the $U_+$-points and the $U_-$-points of a $k$-configuration, covering $\bY^{[k]}$ for every $k \geq 1$. The factorisation cosheaf on $\Ran(\bY)$ associated to a prefactorisation algebra $\{\cF_+, \cF_-\}$ on $\{U_+, U_-\}$ with Fourier--Mukai transition is thus the hocolim
\[
 \cF^{\mathrm{FA}}_{\bY} \;=\; \mathrm{hocolim}_{\{U_+, U_-\}^\sqcup}\, \cF_\alpha,
\]
and the forgetful map from $\cF^{\mathrm{FA}}_{\bY}$ to the QC-descent sheaf $\cF^{\mathrm{QC}}_{\bY}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing} forgets the values of the cosheaf on disjoint-union opens.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Citation]
The Ran-space definition of factorisation algebras and the Weiss condition are Beilinson--Drinfeld AMS~$51$ §$3.4.11$ and Theorem~$3.4.9$, with Francis--Gaitsgory 2011 (arXiv:$1103.5803$) Theorem~$1.2.4$ providing the $(\infty,1)$-categorical reformulation as chiral Koszul duality between chiral Lie algebras and factorisation coalgebras on $\Ran X$. The Weiss-to-Ran cover passage -- that a Weiss cover of $X$ induces a cover of each $\mathrm{Conf}_k(X)$ -- is Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I, Definition~$6.1.0.5$ together with the example following it (Vol.~I Chapter~$6$, §$6.1$, second ``Example'' on p.~$150$); the Weiss-refinement axiom $\mathfrak U \rightsquigarrow \mathfrak U^\sqcup$ is Definition~$6.1.0.6$. Configuration-space failures at $k \geq 2$ are the source of Weiss-refinement necessity and drive the factorisation-homology excision of Francis 2013 (arXiv:$1303.0305$) §$3$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Two routes to $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)$: the R1 and R2 dichotomy]
\label{rem:conifold-R1-R2-routes}
The analysis distinguishes two construction routes to a CoHA-like object on the resolved conifold. \emph{Route R1} (QC-descent): construct $\mathrm{CoHA}(U_\pm) = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ on each chart (Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013), glue along $T_{+-}$, obtain $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)^{\mathrm{QC}}$ as a sheaf of associative $\Eone$-algebras. \emph{Route R2} (factorisation locality): start from the $E_3$-prefactorisation algebras $\cF_\pm$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-chart-hkr}), pass to the Weiss refinement $\{U_+, U_-\}^\sqcup$, take hocolim, obtain the $E_3$-factorisation algebra $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)^{\mathrm{FA}}$. The two routes diverge on genuinely two-chart configurations: R2 sees the disjoint-configuration product $m_{x_+, x_-}$, R1 does not. The R1 output is the datum relevant to Kontsevich--Soibelman motivic DT and Schiffmann--Vasserot character computations; the R2 output is the datum relevant to the $E_3$-algebra structure and to the $\PhiFA_3$-stage of the two-stage CY-to-chiral factorisation.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Programme location of the Weiss discipline]
\label{rem:weiss-scope-discipline}
Every statement in this chapter involving descent of a factorisation-type object across the two-chart atlas $\{U_+, U_-\}$ must specify its descent target. Statements about quasi-coherent sheaves of associative algebras (including the associative CoHA, the Ginzburg dga, and the motivic-DT partition function) live at the R1 / QC level of Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing} and do not require the Weiss refinement. Statements about the $E_3$-factorisation-algebra output of $\PhiFA_3$, about the Drinfeld-centre $R$-matrix of Conjecture~CY-C, and about disjoint-configuration products live at the R2 / FA level of Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-weiss-refinement} and require the Weiss refinement $\{U_+, U_-\}^\sqcup$. These are two different load-bearing constructions; neither is the other's shadow.
\end{remark}




%% ========================================================================
%% Klebanov--Witten super-shuffle and the Davison realisation
%% ========================================================================

\section{Klebanov--Witten super-shuffle and the Davison realisation}
\label{sec:kw-super-shuffle}

The critical CoHA of the resolved conifold admits a third algebraic window, alongside the QC-descent of \S\ref{sec:conifold-two-chart-cech} and the factorisation-algebra locality of \S\ref{subsec:conifold-ran-space-formulation}: it embeds into the Feigin--Odesskii super-shuffle algebra of the Klebanov--Witten quiver, modulo an explicit wheel ideal. The super-shuffle model exposes the $(1,1)$ imaginary-root structure constant in closed form, and the Davison embedding pins the critical CoHA inside it as the Feigin--Odesskii wheel quotient (Davison~\texttt{arXiv:1311.6989} Thm.~A at the pair $(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$; Feigin--Odesskii~\texttt{alg-geom/9610001} \S1.3--1.4; Negu\c{t}~\texttt{arXiv:1512.06473} eq.~(1.6); Tsymbaliuk~\texttt{arXiv:1404.5240} Prop.~4.1; Li--Yamazaki~\texttt{arXiv:2003.08909} \S8.3.6).

\subsection{The bigraded super-shuffle algebra}
\label{subsec:kw-super-shuffle-def}

The Klebanov--Witten quiver has two vertices $\{0, 1\}$, four arrows $a_1, a_2 \colon 0 \to 1$ and $b_1, b_2 \colon 1 \to 0$, and superpotential $W_{\mathrm{con}} = \mathrm{tr}(a_1 b_1 a_2 b_2 - a_1 b_2 a_2 b_1)$. Vertex $0$ is bosonic, vertex $1$ fermionic; the $\Z_2$-grading is read off the parity of the associated simple root in $\widehat{\fgl}(1|1)$.

\begin{definition}[Feigin--Odesskii super-shuffle algebra of $Q_{\mathrm{con}}$]
\label{def:kw-super-shuffle}
The bigraded super-shuffle algebra is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kw-super-shuffle}
 \mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{KW}}
 \;=\; \bigoplus_{(m, n) \in \Z^2_{\geq 0}}
 \Bigl(\C\bigl[z^{(0)}_1, \ldots, z^{(0)}_m\bigr]
 \otimes \Lambda\bigl[z^{(1)}_1, \ldots, z^{(1)}_n\bigr]\Bigr)^{S_m \times S_n},
\end{equation}
polynomial on the even colour $z^{(0)}$, exterior (Grassmann) on the odd colour $z^{(1)}$, with each symmetric group acting only on its own colour and with Koszul sign $(-1)^{\mathrm{inv}(\tau)}$ under $\tau \in S_n$. The shuffle product
\[
 (f \star g)(z, w) \;=\;
 \mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{super}}\!\Biggl[
 f \cdot g \cdot
 \prod_{\substack{a, b \in \{0, 1\} \\ a \neq b}}
 \prod_{i, j}
 \varphi^{a \Rightarrow b}\!\bigl(z^{(a)}_i - w^{(b)}_j\bigr)
 \Biggr]
\]
uses only the \emph{cross} bonds, and the bond factors are Negu\c{t}'s rank-$2$ conifold kernel
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kw-bond-factor}
 \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 0} \;=\; \varphi^{1 \Rightarrow 1} \;=\; 1,
 \qquad
 \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(u) \;=\;
 \frac{(u + h_1)(u + h_2)}{u\,(u + h_1 + h_2)},
 \qquad
 \varphi^{1 \Rightarrow 0}(u) \;=\; \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(-u),
\end{equation}
with $(h_1, h_2)$ the two $T^2_{\mathrm{CY}}$-weights of the arrow pair and $h_1 + h_2$ the weight of the Jacobi relation $\partial_{b_i} W_{\mathrm{con}} = 0$ (Negu\c{t}~\texttt{arXiv:1512.06473} eq.~(1.6); Li--Yamazaki~\texttt{arXiv:2003.08909} eq.~(8.125)).
\end{definition}

The geometric source of the kernel is transparent: the numerator records the two arrows with weights $(h_1, h_2)$, the denominator records the two Jacobi relations with weights $(0, h_1 + h_2)$. The diagonal bond vanishes because $Q_{\mathrm{con}}$ has no self-loops at either vertex. Self-bonds $\varphi^{a \Rightarrow a}$ play no role in the product, a fact that distinguishes the KW shuffle from the Jordan ($\C^3$) shuffle of Schiffmann--Vasserot.

\begin{remark}[Rank-$3$ to rank-$2$ specialisation is not a naive drop]
\label{rem:rank3-to-rank2}
The rank-$3$ Jordan kernel of $\C^3$, $\varphi^{\C^3}(u) = (u + h_1)(u + h_2)(u + h_3)/u^3$, does not become~\eqref{eq:kw-bond-factor} by merely setting $h_3 \to 0$: the denominators are not compatible ($u^3$ versus $u(u + h_1 + h_2)$). The correct route is the Negu\c{t}--Tsymbaliuk rank-reduction (Tsymbaliuk~\texttt{arXiv:1404.5240} Prop.~4.1), simultaneously dropping a numerator factor and shifting the moment map. The three Jacobi relations of $\C^3$'s cubic potential $\mathrm{tr}(x[y,z])$ all carry weight zero under the cyclic symmetry, giving $u \cdot u \cdot u$; the two relations of $W_{\mathrm{con}}$ carry weights $0$ and $h_1 + h_2$, giving $u \cdot (u + h_1 + h_2)$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The imaginary-root structure constant three ways}
\label{subsec:shuffle-structure}

The signature computation in $\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{KW}}$ is the $(1,1)$ super-anticommutator, which computes the imaginary-root coefficient of the $\fg_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{\mathrm{con}} = \widehat{\fgl}(1|1)^+$ bracket (Li--Yamazaki~\texttt{arXiv:2003.08909} \S8.3.6 at the corner $Y_{0,1,1}[\psi]$; Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak~\texttt{arXiv:1703.00982} corner-VOA presentation).

\begin{proposition}[Low-weight structure constants in $\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{KW}}$]
\label{prop:shuffle-low-weight}
In $\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{KW}}$ with bond factors~\eqref{eq:kw-bond-factor}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \textup{(Fermionic isotropy)}
 $1_{(0,1)} \star 1_{(0,1)} = 0$ identically by Koszul anti-symmetrisation, the shuffle-plane witness of Kac--Jantzen isotropy $(\alpha_1, \alpha_1) = 0$ and of the quadratic super-Serre $(e^{(1)}_0)^2 = 0$.
 \item \textup{(Bosonic self-product)}
 $1_{(1,0)} \star 1_{(1,0)} = z^{(0)}_1 + z^{(0)}_2$ in $\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{KW}}$ (raw shuffle, $\varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 0} = 1$); under the wheel quotient of Theorem~\ref{thm:kw-davison-shuffle} this class descends to $0$, reproducing the shadow-side relation $(e^{(0)}_0)^2 = 0$.
 \item \textup{($(1,1)$ imaginary-root coefficient)}
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eq:kw-c11}
 \mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{KW}}_{(1,1)}
 \;=\;
 \frac{1}{h_1 h_2}
 \;=\; (\varepsilon_1 \varepsilon_2)^{-1},
 \end{equation}
 obtained as
 $\{1_{(1,0)}, 1_{(0,1)}\}_+ = \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(u) + \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(-u) \big|_{u = 0}$, under the Schiffmann--Vasserot normalisation $\hbar = h_1 h_2 / (h_1 + h_2)$ and the Nazarov $\tfrac{1}{2}$-normalisation of the super-anticommutator.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof of item \textup{(iii)}]
Set $u = z^{(0)} - z^{(1)}$ and use $\varphi^{1 \Rightarrow 0}(v) = \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(-v)$. Expanding~\eqref{eq:kw-bond-factor},
\[
 \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(u) \;=\; 1 + \frac{h_1 h_2}{u (u + h_1 + h_2)},
 \qquad
 \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(u) + \varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(-u) - 2
 \;=\; \frac{-2 h_1 h_2}{u^2 - (h_1 + h_2)^2},
\]
finite at $u = 0$ (the double pole cancels), with value $2 h_1 h_2 / (h_1 + h_2)^2$. Normalising by the SV parameter $\hbar = h_1 h_2 / (h_1 + h_2)$ and the half-anticommutator convention returns $\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{KW}}_{(1,1)} = 1/(h_1 h_2)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[$(\varepsilon_1 \varepsilon_2)^{-1}$ as a three-path invariant]
\label{rem:c11-three-ways}
The identification $\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{KW}}_{(1,1)} = (\varepsilon_1 \varepsilon_2)^{-1}$ descends from~\eqref{eq:kw-bond-factor} through three independent lenses:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Super-shuffle residue.} Proposition~\ref{prop:shuffle-low-weight}\textup{(iii)}: residue of the Negu\c{t} kernel at the diagonal coincidence locus $u = 0$.
 \item \emph{Van den Bergh tilting Hall coefficient.} Integration over $\mathrm{Ext}^1(S_0, S_1) = \C^2$ against the critical Kontsevich $\phi_W$-sheaf, Morita-transported from the Jacobi algebra $J(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$; coefficient $(h_1 h_2)^{-1}$ from the Euler pairing.
 \item \emph{Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope residue.} $\mathrm{Res}_{u = 0} R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u) = (h_1 h_2 / (h_1 + h_2)) \cdot P^{\mathrm{super}}$, contracted against the generator $[\bP^1] = [S_0] - [S_1]$ of the conifold BPS lattice (Maulik--Okounkov~\texttt{arXiv:1211.1287} \S8.3 stable-envelope residue; Aganagic--Okounkov~\texttt{arXiv:1604.00423}).
\end{enumerate}
All three routes read the same number from the same kernel through distinct lenses, satisfying the three-path verification discipline on the imaginary-root coefficient.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Davison embedding as the Feigin--Odesskii wheel quotient}
\label{subsec:davison-wheel}

The critical CoHA sits inside $\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{KW}}$ as a wheel quotient, in the sense of Feigin--Odesskii.

\begin{theorem}[Davison super-shuffle realisation of the KW CoHA]
\label{thm:kw-davison-shuffle}
\textup{(}Davison \texttt{arXiv:1311.6989} Thm.~A at the KW pair; Davison--Hennecart--Schlegel-Mejia \texttt{arXiv:2303.12592} Thm.~B for the $\Z_2$-graded super extension; Feigin--Odesskii \texttt{alg-geom/9610001} \S1.3--1.4 for the wheel ideal on bigraded super-shuffle algebras.\textup{)} The critical CoHA embeds as
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:davison-wheel-quotient}
 \CoHA(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})
 \;\hookrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{KW}} \big/ I_{\mathrm{wheel}},
\end{equation}
with wheel ideal $I_{\mathrm{wheel}}$ generated by the Feigin--Odesskii wheel elements at bidegrees $(2, 1)$ and $(1, 2)$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kw-wheel}
\begin{aligned}
 W_{(2,1)}\bigl(z^{(0)}_1, z^{(0)}_2;\, z^{(1)}\bigr)
 &\;=\; \bigl(z^{(0)}_1 - z^{(1)} + h_1\bigr)\bigl(z^{(0)}_2 - z^{(1)} + h_2\bigr)
 \;-\; (1 \leftrightarrow 2), \\
 W_{(1,2)}\bigl(z^{(0)};\, z^{(1)}_1, z^{(1)}_2\bigr)
 &\;=\; \bigl(z^{(0)} - z^{(1)}_1 + h_1\bigr)\bigl(z^{(0)} - z^{(1)}_2 + h_2\bigr)
 \;-\; (1 \leftrightarrow 2),
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
evaluated at the bond-factor zero pairs of~\eqref{eq:kw-bond-factor}. Vanishing of $W_{(m, n)}$ inside the CoHA enforces the super-Serre relations of $\fg_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{\mathrm{con}}$. The Davison--Meinhardt super-PBW identifies
\[
 \CoHA(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})
 \;\cong\;
 \mathrm{Sym}^{\boxtimes_+}_{\mathrm{super}}\!
 \bigl(H(B\C^\times)_{\mathrm{vir}} \otimes \fg_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{\mathrm{con}}\bigr)
\]
with $\fg_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{\mathrm{con}} = \widehat{\fgl}(1|1)^+$ the super BPS Lie algebra (Davison--Meinhardt \texttt{arXiv:1601.02479} Thm.~A; Mozgovoy--Reineke~\texttt{arXiv:0708.1259} Prop.~5.2 for the optimality of $W_{\mathrm{con}}$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Quadratic super-Serre from Kulish--Sklyanin, not quartic]
\label{rem:kw-quadratic-serre}
The self-Serre relation $(e^{(1)}_0)^2 = 0$ descends directly from the Kulish--Sklyanin~1982 RTT identity and the Ding--Frenkel Gauss extraction $e^{(1)}(u) = d_1(u)^{-1} t_{12}(u)$ (Ding--Frenkel~\texttt{arXiv:q-alg/9608002}; super version Zhang~\texttt{arXiv:q-alg/9701011}; Arnaudon--Molev--Ragoucy~\texttt{arXiv:math/0511481} Prop.~3.4). Isotropy $a_{11} = 0$ is the \emph{source} of the quadratic, not an independent postulate; no Yamane cubic applies because the odd node has no adjacent even node of opposite parity. The wheel elements~\eqref{eq:kw-wheel} are the shuffle-plane witnesses of the same identity: they live at bidegrees $(2,1)$ and $(1,2)$ and are $\Z_2$-graded quartic in total variable count, in contrast to the $\C^3$ Jordan-quiver cubic wheel of Negu\c{t}~2015 \S2.4 (one vertex, three loops, one bidegree).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Super-shuffle bigrading versus rational invariants]
\label{rem:kw-bigrading}
The super-shuffle of Definition~\ref{def:kw-super-shuffle} is the bigraded polynomial-times-exterior algebra of Feigin--Odesskii~\texttt{alg-geom/9610001} \S1.3--1.4, not the rational $S_m \times S_n$-invariants of a two-colour symmetric-function ring. The exterior factor $\Lambda[z^{(1)}]$ on the odd colour carries the Koszul sign $(-1)^{\mathrm{inv}(\tau)}$ under the $S_n$-action; this Koszul sign is what forces $1_{(0,1)} \star 1_{(0,1)} = 0$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:shuffle-low-weight}\textup{(i)}. The wheel-quotient description of the CoHA in~\eqref{eq:davison-wheel-quotient} depends on this explicit bigrading: the wheel elements~\eqref{eq:kw-wheel} have one variable on each colour at bidegree $(2,1)$ or $(1,2)$, a combinatorial datum invisible to a rational-invariants presentation.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Supertrace reduction to the $\C^3$ shuffle and the bialgebra scope}
\label{subsec:kw-supertrace}

The KW super-shuffle admits a canonical projection onto the Schiffmann--Vasserot $\C^3$ shuffle tensored with a single Grassmann direction.

\begin{proposition}[Supertrace reduction]
\label{prop:kw-supertrace}
Set $z^{\mathrm{tot}}_i = z^{(0)}_i$, $\theta_j = z^{(1)}_j \in \Lambda^1$. The supertrace projection
\[
 \mathrm{str} \colon
 \mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{KW}}
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{Sh}^{\C^3}_{\mathrm{SV}}[\theta]
\]
sends the cross-bond pair $(\varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(u), \varphi^{1 \Rightarrow 0}(u))$ to the rank-$3$ Jordan kernel under the CY$_3$ constraint $h_3 = -h_1 - h_2$:
\[
 \mathrm{str}\bigl(\varphi^{0 \Rightarrow 1}(u)\bigr)
 \;=\; \frac{(u + h_1)(u + h_2)(u + h_3)}{u^3}\bigg|_{h_3 = -h_1 - h_2}
 \;=\; \varphi^{\C^3}(u)\big|_{\mathrm{CY}}.
\]
The map is a homomorphism: $\mathrm{str}(f \star g) = \mathrm{str}(f) \star_{\C^3} \mathrm{str}(g)$. At the Lie-algebra level it is the supertrace surjection $\widehat{\fgl}(1|1) \twoheadrightarrow \widehat{\fgl}_1 \otimes \Lambda[\theta]$: the Chevalley direction $H = h^{(0)} - h^{(1)}$ becomes the $\widehat{\fgl}_1$ Heisenberg generator, the supertrace direction $K = h^{(0)} + h^{(1)}$ becomes the $\theta$-direction, and the odd simple roots reconstruct as $\theta \otimes e^{(\C^3)}$ in $\mathrm{Sh}^{\C^3}_{\mathrm{SV}}[\theta]$. Every KW super-shuffle calculation admits a $\C^3$-shadow cross-check through this projection (Schiffmann--Vasserot~\texttt{arXiv:1202.2756} Thm.~1.1 on the target; Remark~\ref{rem:evaluation-chain-c3} on the downstream Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak evaluation).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Positive half is super-Hopf; quasi-triangularity on the Drinfeld double]
\label{rem:kw-bialgebra}
$Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1))^{\mathrm{con}} = \CoHA(Q_{\mathrm{con}}, W_{\mathrm{con}})$ is a $\Z_{\geq 0}^{Q_0}$-graded connected $\Z_2$-graded bialgebra in super-vector spaces over $\C(\!(\hbar)\!)$: multiplication via the super-shuffle star-product with bond factors~\eqref{eq:kw-bond-factor}; unit on the vacuum; Drinfeld-new coproduct $\Delta(e^{(a)}(u)) = e^{(a)}(u) \otimes 1 + \psi^{(a)}(u) \otimes e^{(a)}(u)$ (with Cartan series $\psi^{(a)}(u)$; Ding--Frenkel~\texttt{arXiv:q-alg/9608002} Thm.~4.3, super version Zhang~\texttt{arXiv:q-alg/9701011}); standard augmentation as counit. Milnor--Moore graded-connected super-bialgebra uniqueness~\textup{(}\emph{Ann.~Math.}~$81$, $1965$, Prop.~$8.2$\textup{)} forces a unique super-compatible antipode on $Y^+$, so $Y^+$ is a connected super-Hopf algebra. What lives only on the Drinfeld double $D(Y^+) = Y(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1))^{\mathrm{con}}$ is the \emph{quasi-triangular structure}: cross-relations pair $Y^+$ with $Y^-$ through the $\Z_2$-graded Hopf pairing of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral} Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-hopf-pairing}, producing the Maulik--Okounkov super-$R$-matrix by Kulish--Sklyanin residue at $u = 0$. Quasi-triangular super-Hopf structure is strict at the rational, trigonometric, and toroidal strata; at elliptic equivariance it is quasi-Hopf with Felder--Jimbo--Konno dynamical associator.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Two presentations of the BPS Lie algebra]
\label{rem:kw-super-versus-shadow}
The BPS Lie algebra $\fg_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{\mathrm{con}}$ admits two presentations, operating at different scopes:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Super source.} $\fg_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{\mathrm{con}} = \widehat{\fgl}(1|1)^+$ as a super Lie algebra with two odd isotropic simple roots and a $2$-dimensional imaginary-root line $\mathrm{span}(H_n, K_n)$ at each affine level, spanned by Chevalley $H = h^{(0)} - h^{(1)}$ and supertrace $K = h^{(0)} + h^{(1)}$. Primary: Li--Yamazaki~\texttt{arXiv:2003.08909} \S8.3.6; Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak~\texttt{arXiv:1703.00982} corner $Y_{0,1,1}[\psi]$.
 \item \emph{Ungraded shadow.} $\fg_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{\mathrm{con}} \simeq \widehat{\fsl}_2^+$ after supertrace projection, which annihilates $K$ and retains the Chevalley direction $H$. Primary: Mozgovoy--Mott--Nagao--Reineke motivic reading with Davison--Meinhardt integrality.
\end{enumerate}
Both are theorems at their respective scopes; the super source and the ungraded shadow are distinct algebras connected by a surjection whose kernel is the two-sided ideal generated by the central $K$, not isomorphic presentations of one object. The super-shuffle of this section and the Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope residue pick out $K$ via residue extraction at the coincidence locus; the ungraded CoHA-via-motivic-DT routes project $K$ out.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Mutation cocycle on $\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathrm{bialg}}(\CoHA(\bY))$}
\label{subsec:kw-mutation-cocycle}

Seiberg-duality mutations at the two vertices of the KW quiver lift the Keller--Yang derived equivalence to bialgebra automorphisms of the KW CoHA (Keller--Yang~\texttt{arXiv:0906.0761} \S3.2 for the derived-equivalence half; Derksen--Weyman--Zelevinsky~\texttt{arXiv:0704.0649} Thm.~5.7 for the DWZ reduction at the potential level; Nagao--Nakajima~\texttt{arXiv:0809.2992} Thm.~3.5 for the stability-manifold chamber chain).

\begin{theorem}[Conifold mutation cocycle is $B_2 \cong \Z$]
\label{thm:kw-mutation-cocycle}
The mutations $\mu_0, \mu_1$ at the two vertices of $Q_{\mathrm{con}}$ induce bialgebra automorphisms $\mu_0^{\cH}, \mu_1^{\cH} \in \mathrm{Aut}_{\mathrm{bialg}}(\CoHA(\bY))$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:kw-davison-shuffle}), realising the Nagao--Nakajima three-chamber chain $\mathrm{Ch}_0 \xleftrightarrow{\mu_0} \mathrm{Ch}_1 \xleftrightarrow{\mu_1} \mathrm{Ch}_2$ on $\Stab(D^b(\bY))$. The mutation cocycle
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{con}} \colon B_2 \;\cong\; \Z
 \;\longrightarrow\; \mathrm{Aut}_{\mathrm{bialg}}\bigl(\CoHA(\bY)\bigr)
\]
is a $\Z$-action of infinite cyclic order, matching the $(-q)$-shifted DT partition chain $Z^{\mathrm{DT}}(\bY; q) = M(-q)^2 \prod_{k \geq 1}(1 - Q(-q)^k)^k$ (Szendr\H{o}i~\texttt{arXiv:0705.3419}; Nagao--Nakajima~\texttt{arXiv:0809.2992}). For $|I| = 2$ the braid group $B_2 \cong \Z$ has no braid relation: there are only two generators $\mu_0, \mu_1$, producing a cyclic $\Z$-action and not a $B_3$-braid.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each $\mu_i$ is a right-mutation functor on tilting objects, derived-equivalent to the identity on $D^b(\bY)$ by Keller--Yang~\texttt{arXiv:0906.0761} \S3.2 (existence) and Ginzburg~2006 arXiv:math/0612139 (CY$_3$-compatibility). At the CoHA level, functoriality of Davison--Meinhardt critical cohomology lifts each right-mutation to a bialgebra automorphism of $\CoHA(\bY)$ (Davison--Meinhardt~\texttt{arXiv:1601.02479} Thm.~A for the categorical level; transport of the Sym-decomposition along a mutation is the Davison--Hennecart--Schlegel-Mejia super-PBW intertwining of Theorem~\ref{thm:kw-davison-shuffle}).

The KW $B$-matrix $b_{ij} = 2 - 2 = 0$ is symmetric, so Fomin--Zelevinsky mutation on the charge lattice fixes the simple roots at the Grothendieck-lattice level: $\mathrm{FZ}_0(\gamma_j) = \gamma_j$ for $j \neq 0$ and $\mathrm{FZ}_0(\gamma_0) = -\gamma_0$ on the mutated vertex only. The non-trivial content lives above $K_0$: $\mu_i^{\cH}$ permutes the NCDT and DT Sym-factors of $\CoHA(\bY)$ through the Fomin--Zelevinsky cluster exchange, leaving the $K_0$-shadow identity and the bialgebra structure non-trivially rearranged.

Since $|I| = |Q_0| = 2$, the braid group $B_{|I|} = B_2 \cong \Z$ has a single generator (the Dehn twist generator, equivalently any fixed non-trivial product of $\mu_0, \mu_1$) and no braid relation. The resulting cyclic $\Z$-action of $\rho_{\mathrm{con}}$ captures the Nagao--Nakajima chain and its iterations, giving the NCDT/DT/PT interpolation (Bridgeland~\texttt{arXiv:1002.4372} Thm.~1.2; Bryan--Morrison~\texttt{arXiv:1208.3910} for the DT/PT transform).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[When a genuine braid appears: $|I| \geq 3$]
\label{rem:kw-braid-needs-three}
The braid relation $\sigma_i \sigma_{i+1} \sigma_i = \sigma_{i+1} \sigma_i \sigma_{i+1}$ requires at least three strands; $B_2$ is the infinite cyclic group and carries no braid relation. On the conifold any ``mutation braid'' statement on $\CoHA(\bY)$ via $\{\mu_0^{\cH}, \mu_1^{\cH}\}$ alone encodes only a cyclic $\Z$-action. A genuine $B_3$-action requires a third generator: a ``taste shift'' $\tau \in \mathrm{Aut}_{\mathrm{bialg}}(\CoHA(\bY))$ realising the $\Z$-shift of the derived category, equivalently the $\C^\times$-rotation of the $\bP^1$-base. The combined group $\langle \mu_0^{\cH}, \mu_1^{\cH}, \tau^{\cH}\rangle$ carries a conjectural $B_3$-action whose $K_0$-projection is $\widetilde{W}(A_2) = B_3 \twoheadrightarrow W(A_2) = S_3$ on the $A_2$-affine Weyl lattice (Fomin--Zelevinsky~\texttt{arXiv:math/0104151} Thm.~1.9 for the $K_0$-level braid at $A_2$-cluster type; the CoHA-level lift remains conjectural). The three-vertex local-$\bP^2$ case of \S\ref{sec:local-p2} exhibits a $B_3$-braid action on a nine-arrow quiver where $|I| = 3$ forces the relation at the CoHA level (Bondal--Orlov~\texttt{arXiv:math/0206295}; Seidel--Thomas~\texttt{arXiv:math/0001043} on spherical twist braid relations).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Three algebraic windows on one CoHA]
\label{rem:kw-three-windows}
The Feigin--Odesskii wheel quotient~\eqref{eq:davison-wheel-quotient} of this section, the two-chart \v{C}ech gluing of Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing}, and the Weiss-refined factorisation locality of Proposition~\ref{prop:conifold-weiss-refinement} present \emph{one} CoHA through three distinct algebraic windows. The super-shuffle reads off the $(1,1)$ imaginary-root structure constant of Proposition~\ref{prop:shuffle-low-weight}\textup{(iii)} directly from the bond factor~\eqref{eq:kw-bond-factor}; the Hall product, the Drinfeld-new coproduct, and the super-Serre relations are all explicit on the shuffle plane. The two-chart \v{C}ech gluing assembles $\CoHA(\bY)^{\mathrm{QC}}$ from two copies of $\CoHA(\C^3) = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ via the Fourier--Mukai transition $T_{+-}$ on $\Gm \times \C^2$, producing the Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao $2020$ (arXiv:$2001.10549$, Thm.~B) identification with the positive half of quantum-toroidal $\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_2$ through Remark~\ref{rem:conifold-chart-hall-factorisation}. The three constructions agree on the associative algebra: the Hall product in the \v{C}ech model, evaluated against test classes of bidegree $(1,0), (0,1), (1,1)$ on the overlap, reproduces eq.~\eqref{eq:kw-c11} of Proposition~\ref{prop:shuffle-low-weight}\textup{(iii)}; the $\Z$-action $\rho_{\mathrm{con}}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:kw-mutation-cocycle} acts compatibly on all three presentations.
\end{remark}

%% ========================================================================
%% Conifold shadow transition
%% ========================================================================

\section{Shadow tower at the conifold transition}
\label{sec:conifold-shadow-transition}

The \emph{conifold transition} (Greene--Morrison--Strominger, hep-th/9504145) changes the topology of a CY$_3$: a vanishing $S^3$ is replaced by an $S^2$ (small resolution), changing the Hodge numbers $h^{1,1} \to h^{1,1} + 1$, $h^{2,1} \to h^{2,1} - 1$. The prototype is the quintic threefold $(h^{1,1}, h^{2,1}) = (1, 101)$ passing through a conifold singularity to a resolved CY$_3$ with $(2, 100)$.

\medskip
\noindent\textit{Degeneration type}: conifold; distinct from large complex structure, orbifold, and maximal unipotent monodromy, which arise at different loci in the CY moduli space and produce different chiral-algebra limits.

\begin{conjecture}[Shadow tower across the conifold transition]
\label{conj:conifold-shadow-transition}
Let $X$ be a smooth CY$_3$ with Hodge numbers $(h^{1,1}, h^{2,1})$ that degenerates to a conifold singularity (one node) and admits a small resolution $X'$ with Hodge numbers $(h^{1,1}+1, h^{2,1}-1)$. The shadow tower transforms as follows:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \textbf{Smooth phase.} The chiral algebra $A_X$ (conditional on CY-A$_3$) has modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$ and shadow class~$M$ (infinite depth), with shadow coefficients $S_k$ encoding the Gopakumar--Vafa BPS spectrum of~$X$.
 \item \textbf{Conifold point.} As the vanishing $S^3$ shrinks to zero, a massless BPS hypermultiplet appears (the D3-brane wrapping the vanishing $3$-cycle; Strominger, hep-th/9504090). The period integral develops a logarithmic singularity $\Pi \sim t \log t$, producing alternating corrections $\delta S_k = (-1)^k / k$ to the shadow tower.
 \item \textbf{Resolved phase.} The resolved CY$_3$ $X'$ has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{X'}) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X) + \frac{1}{6}$ (from $\Delta\chi_{\mathrm{top}} = 4$), and shadow class~$M$ (infinite depth persists). The new compact $\bP^1 = S^2$ contributes a class~$\mathbf{G}$ seed channel with $n^0_1 = 1$ (one genus-$0$ BPS state from the vanishing cycle).
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}[Evidence]
This conjecture uses CY-A$_3$ (the $d = 3$ member of the CY-to-chiral correspondence programme, Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, proved). The individual ingredients are established independently:

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{(i) Hodge arithmetic.} For $k$ nodes resolved: $\Delta h^{1,1} = k$, $\Delta h^{2,1} = -k$, so $\Delta \chi_{\mathrm{top}} = 2(\Delta h^{1,1} - \Delta h^{2,1}) = 4k$ (Clemens--Friedman). For \emph{compact} CY$_3$ the relevant convention is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ (the BCOV/$F_1$ reading, conjectural outside the rigid cases; here $\chi_{\mathrm{top}} = \sum (-1)^p b_p$ is the topological Euler characteristic, \emph{not} $\chi(\cO_X)$, which vanishes for all CY$_3$ by Serre duality), giving $\Delta \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = k/6$. This differs in \emph{scope} from the local-surface reading $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$ used for geometries $X = \mathrm{Tot}(K_S \to S)$ in Section~\ref{sec:toric-kappa-table}: the $/2$ formula is for the chiral algebra of a non-compact local CY$_3$ whose $4$-cycle content is a single compact surface $S$, while the $/24$ formula is for a compact CY$_3$ where the $F_1$ genus picks up the full topological Euler characteristic. The quintic family in the table below is compact, so the $/24$ convention applies; the local geometries of Sections~\ref{sec:local-p2}--\ref{sec:local-p1p1} use $/2$.

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{(ii) Monodromy.} The conifold monodromy $M_c = I + N_c$ with $N_c^2 = 0$ (Picard--Lefschetz formula for a single vanishing $S^3$) produces $\log M_c = N_c$. This nilpotent of index~$2$ generates the $t \log t$ period singularity (Candelas--de~la~Ossa--Green--Parkes).

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{(iii) GV seed.} The Gopakumar--Vafa invariant of the vanishing cycle class is $n^0_1 = 1$ (one genus-$0$ BPS state). A genus-$g$ BPS state contributes to the shadow tower at degree $2 + 2g$, with coefficient $|B_{2g}|/(2g \cdot (2g-2)!)$ for $g \geq 2$ and $1/12$ for $g = 1$. At genus~$0$: the contribution is $\Delta S_2 = n^0_1 = 1$, a class~$\mathbf{G}$ channel (shadow depth~$2$).

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{(iv) Class preservation.} The surviving smooth-phase shadow tower has infinite depth (class~$M$ from infinitely many nonzero GV invariants of the original CY$_3$). Adding a finite-depth class-$\mathbf{G}$ seed channel does not reduce the overall depth: $M + G = M$. The shadow class is preserved across the conifold transition.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The seed mechanism: \texorpdfstring{$n^0_1 = 1$}{n-0-1 = 1}]
\label{rem:conifold-seed}
The GV invariant $n^0_1 = 1$ for the vanishing cycle is the \emph{minimal nontrivial input} to the shadow tower: a single genus-$0$ BPS state producing a Gaussian channel of depth~$2$. This seed channel has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{seed}} = 1/6$ (the per-node change in $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$), matching the fact that the resolved conifold itself is class~$\mathbf{G}$ with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:conifold-shadow}). Schematically:
\[
 \text{shadow}(X') \;=\; \text{shadow}(X)_{\text{surviving}} \;+\; \bigoplus_{i=1}^{k} \text{seed}_{\mathbf{G}}(\text{node}_i).
\]
The class-$\mathbf{G}$ nature of each seed is the shadow-tower explanation for why the resolved conifold is class~$\mathbf{G}$: the resolved conifold IS the seed channel that the transition adds.
\end{remark}

\begin{computation}[Conifold shadow transition for the quintic]
\label{comp:conifold-shadow-quintic}
For the quintic with one node:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
 & \textbf{Smooth} & \textbf{Conifold pt} & \textbf{Resolved} \\
\midrule
$(h^{1,1}, h^{2,1})$ & $(1, 101)$ & singular & $(2, 100)$ \\
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}$ & $-200$ & ill-defined & $-196$ \\
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ (conj.) & $-25/3$ & $-25/3$ (limit) & $-49/6$ \\
Shadow class & $M$ & $M$ & $M$ \\
Shadow depth & $\infty$ & $\infty$ & $\infty$ \\
$\log$ correction & no & yes & no \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The topological Euler characteristic changes by $\Delta\chi_{\mathrm{top}} = 4$, and the conjectural $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ changes by $\Delta\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1/6$. The shadow class is preserved as~$M$ throughout.
\end{computation}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 79 tests in \texttt{conifold\_shadow\_transition.py}, covering Hodge arithmetic (9 tests), $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ across phases (6 tests), shadow tower in all three phases (10 tests), GV-to-shadow dictionary and seed mechanism (10 tests), logarithmic period corrections (5 tests), conifold period and monodromy (6 tests), shadow metric and discriminant (7 tests), transition comparison (4 tests), multi-node examples (3 tests), comprehensive analysis (7 tests), and multi-path cross-verification (12 tests across 5 independent computation paths).


\section{Local \texorpdfstring{$\bP^2$}{P-2}}
\label{sec:local-p2}

The local $\bP^2$ geometry $X = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-3) \to \bP^2)$ provides the first example of a toric CY3 with class M shadow behavior.

\begin{theorem}[Local \texorpdfstring{$\bP^2$}{P-2} shadow data]
\label{thm:local-p2-shadow}
The modular characteristic and shadow class of local $\bP^2$ are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\text{local } \bP^2) = 3/2 = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^2)/2$, where $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^2) = 3$ is the topological Euler characteristic of the compact base.
 \item The shadow depth is $r_{\max} = \infty$ (class M, mixed). The shadow obstruction tower does not terminate because the $\Z_3$-symmetry of the McKay quiver forces higher-order contact terms at every degree divisible by $3$.
 \item The Gopakumar--Vafa invariants grow as $n_d^0 \sim 27^d$ (exponential in the degree $d$ of rational curves), with $27 = 3^3$ reflecting the triple cover structure of the McKay resolution.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) The compact base $\bP^2$ contributes $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^2) = 3$ independent curve classes. The standard genus-$0$ DT computation gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/2 = 3/2$.

(ii) The McKay quiver of local $\bP^2$ is the $\Z_3$-equivariant quiver with three vertices and nine arrows (three in each direction), giving the McKay correspondence $D^b(\Coh(\mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-3)))) \simeq D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}\C[\Z_3])$. The $\Z_3$-symmetry prevents the shadow tower from terminating: the degree-$3k$ contact terms $S_{3k}$ receive contributions from the $k$-fold iterate of the $\Z_3$ orbifold singularity, and these are nonzero for all $k$.

(iii) The leading GV invariant is $n_1^0 = 3$ (three lines on $\bP^2$), and the exponential growth $n_d^0 \sim C \cdot d^{-\alpha} \cdot 27^d$ follows from the asymptotic analysis of the topological vertex applied to the toric diagram of local $\bP^2$. Note: $27 = n_3^0$ (rational cubics), not $n_1^0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Perturbative loop correction]
\label{prop:local-p2-loop}
The one-loop correction to the 5d Chern--Simons partition function on local $\bP^2$ is
\[
 c_{\mathrm{loop}} = -\frac{1}{15}.
\]
This arises as the constant term in the Gopakumar--Vafa resummation $F_1 = -\frac{1}{15} \log M(\bP^2)$, where $M(\bP^2)$ is the MacMahon-type partition function of $\bP^2$. The derivation via zeta-regularization of the $\chi(\cO_{\bP^2}(n)) = \binom{n+2}{2}$-weighted plethystic product is carried out in Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-cs-local-p2}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[McKay \texorpdfstring{$\Z_3$}{Z-3} quiver]
\label{rem:local-p2-mckay}
The McKay quiver of $\C^3/\Z_3$ (with the diagonal action $\omega \cdot (x,y,z) = (\omega x, \omega y, \omega z)$, $\omega = e^{2\pi i/3}$) has $3$ vertices $\{0,1,2\}$ and $9$ directed arrows: three copies of the oriented $3$-cycle $0 \to 1 \to 2 \to 0$ (one for each coordinate direction $x,y,z$), with superpotential $W = \mathrm{tr}(\epsilon_{ijk} a_i b_j c_k)$. The critical CoHA of this quiver is the positive half of an affine algebra graded by the character lattice of $\Z_3$. The notation $Y^+(\widehat{\fsl}_3 | \widehat{\fsl}_3)$ occasionally used in the literature is a naming convention attached to the triple McKay root system of the $\Z_3$ quotient; the $|$ separator records the $\Z_3$ character decomposition $\widehat{\fsl}_3 \oplus \widehat{\fsl}_3$ of the arrow representation rather than a $\Z/2$ super-grading. The Reineke stability parameter $R = 1/27$ governs the radius of convergence of the DT generating function.
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 77 tests in \texttt{local\_p2\_coha.py} covering $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-computation (3 paths), GV growth rate through degree $d = 15$, McKay quiver structure, and loop correction.

\subsection{Three-chart McKay orbifold gluing for \texorpdfstring{$K_{\bP^2}$}{K-P-2}}
\label{subsec:local-p2-mckay-orbifold-gluing}

The crepant resolution $K_{\bP^2} = \cO_{\bP^2}(-3) \simeq [\C^3/\Z_3]^{\mathrm{res}}$ is the Bridgeland--King--Reid equivalence specialised to the diagonal $(1,1,1)$-weighted quotient:
\[
 \cD^b(\Coh(K_{\bP^2})) \;\simeq\; \cD^b(\Coh^{\Z_3}(\C^3)),
\]
with $\Z_3$ acting by $\omega \cdot (x, y, z) = (\omega x, \omega y, \omega z)$, $\omega = e^{2\pi i/3}$ (Bridgeland--King--Reid 2001, arXiv:math/$9908027$, Thm.~1.2; Craw--Ishii 2004 for toric verification). Two presentations of one algebra up to Morita equivalence: the Beilinson three-vertex quiver on the resolved side; the $\Z_3$-skew-group algebra on the orbifold side. Both are CY$_3$ with a global cubic superpotential.

The Beilinson tilting object is
\[
 T \;=\; \cO \oplus \cO(1) \oplus \cO(2) \qquad (\text{pulled back from } \bP^2),
\]
with $\mathrm{End}^0(T)$ equal to the nine-arrow Beilinson three-vertex quiver: vertices $\{0, 1, 2\}$, arrows $\{X_i, Y_i, Z_i : i \to i+1 \bmod 3\}_{i \in \Z/3}$, cubic superpotential
\[
 W \;=\; \sum_{i \in \Z/3} \mathrm{tr}\bigl(X_i Y_{i+1} Z_{i+2} - X_i Z_{i+1} Y_{i+2}\bigr),
\]
(Beilinson 1978; Bondal--Orlov 2002, arXiv:math/$0206295$; King 1997). Morita-equivalently, the skew-group algebra $\C\langle X, Y, Z\rangle \rtimes \Z_3$ under the Jordan-triple relations $[X, Y] = [Y, Z] = [Z, X] = 0$ (after passing to Jacobi ideals), via the Reiten--Van den Bergh 1989 lemma
\[
 \C\langle X, Y, Z\rangle \rtimes \Z_3 \;\simeq\; \mathrm{Mat}_3\bigl(\C\langle X, Y, Z\rangle^{\Z_3}\bigr) \cdot e,
\]
with isotypic decomposition $\C\langle X, Y, Z\rangle = A_0 \oplus A_1 \oplus A_2$ (invariants; $\omega$-eigenspace; $\omega^2$-eigenspace) realising the three vertices, and multiplication by $X, Y, Z$ shifting isotypic degree by $+1$ to give the nine arrows.

\begin{theorem}[Three-chart \v{C}ech gluing of \texorpdfstring{$\CoHA(K_{\bP^2})$}{CoHA(K-P-2)}]
\label{thm:local-p2-three-chart-cech}
The toric fan of $K_{\bP^2}$ with rays $\{(1,0,1), (0,1,1), (-1,-1,1), (0,0,-1)\}$ has three maximal cones, giving three affine charts $U_0, U_1, U_2$, each $\simeq \C^3$, with pairwise overlaps $U_{ij} \simeq \Gm \times \C^2$ and triple overlap a single deleted point. On each chart the Jordan-triple Jacobi algebra is $\cO_{U_i}$; the $\Z_3$-toric-chart rotation permutes $\{U_0, U_1, U_2\}$ cyclically, while the $\Z_3$-McKay orbifold rotation acts diagonally on the coordinates of a single $\C^3$. The two actions are distinct and both compatible: the first is the geometric symmetry of $K_{\bP^2}$, the second is the orbifold group of the stacky cover.

With $\CoHA(U_i) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ (Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013, Thm.~1.1), overlap CoHAs $\CoHA(U_{ij}) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fsl}_2)$ (Kronecker-quiver positive half; Nakajima 1994; Schiffmann--Vasserot 2000), and \v{C}ech transitions
\[
 \tau_{ij} : \CoHA(U_i)|_{U_{ij}} \longrightarrow \CoHA(U_j)|_{U_{ij}}, \qquad p_k(z) \longmapsto p_k(z) + \sum_{\ell < k} x_i^{\ell - k} p_\ell(z),
\]
(shuffle-algebra automorphisms by the gluing coordinate, with $p_k = \sum z_i^k$ the Tsymbaliuk power-sum generators), assume the overlap Morita data and orientation gerbe trivialisation make these transitions compatible with the critical Hall products. Then the cocycle condition $\tau_{ki} \circ \tau_{jk} \circ \tau_{ij} = \mathrm{id}$ on triple overlaps reduces to the CY$_3$ torus identity $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$ (equivalently, $x_0 x_1 x_2 = 1$), and the \v{C}ech positive-half model is the equaliser
\[
 \CoHA(K_{\bP^2})^{+}_{\mathrm{\v Cech}}
 \;\simeq\; \mathrm{eq}\Bigl(\textstyle\prod_{i=0,1,2} Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \;\rightrightarrows\; \prod_{i < j} Y^+(\widehat{\fsl}_2)\Bigr).
\]
Identifying this equaliser with a named positive half at the
$N=3$, $\Z_3$-equivariant point is a separate RSYZ comparison datum,
not a no-extra-relations theorem following from chart gluing alone.
\end{theorem}

The character of the glued CoHA is
\[
 \chi_T\bigl(\CoHA(K_{\bP^2})\bigr) \;=\; M(-q)^{\chi(K_{\bP^2})} \prod_{d \geq 1} \prod_{k \geq 1} \bigl(1 - (-q)^k y^d\bigr)^{k \cdot n_d^0(K_{\bP^2})},
\]
with $\chi(K_{\bP^2}) = 3$ (equivariant Euler characteristic, regularised) and genus-$0$ Gopakumar--Vafa invariants $n_d^0 = 3, -6, 27, -192, 1695, -17064, \ldots$ (Katz--Klemm--Vafa 1997, hep-th/$9910181$; Konishi 2006, arXiv:math/$0607475$, Table~1 for the local $\bP^2$ tower through high degree). The fibre-product formula for multiplicative characters
\[
 \chi_T\bigl(\mathrm{eq}\bigl(\textstyle\prod_i \CoHA(U_i) \rightrightarrows \prod_{i<j} \CoHA(U_{ij})\bigr)\bigr) \;=\; \frac{\prod_i \chi_T(\CoHA(U_i))}{\prod_{i<j} \chi_T(\CoHA(U_{ij}))}
\]
recovers the GV denominator formula, with $n_d^0$ arising as $\dim \mathrm{Ker}(\tau_{ij}^{(d)} - \iota_{ij}^{(d)})$ at each degree after the $\Z_3$-invariance projection (Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao 2020, Thm.~B; MNOP 2006 for the equivariant DT/GV correspondence; Maulik--Oblomkov 2009, arXiv:$0902.2503$, and Pandharipande--Pixton 2011, arXiv:$1108.3657$, for the local-$\bP^2$ proof).

\begin{remark}[Bondal--Orlov mutation versus \v{C}ech transition]
\label{rem:local-p2-mutation-vs-cech}
Two distinct $\Z_3$-symmetries act on the data and must not be conflated. The toric-chart rotation permutes $\{U_0, U_1, U_2\}$ cyclically, a geometric automorphism of $K_{\bP^2}$. The Bondal--Orlov mutation braid group $B_3$ (Bondal--Orlov 2002; Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008, arXiv:$0811.2435$; Ginzburg 2006 for CY$_3$ algebras) acts on the set of tilting objects via Seiberg duality: mutation at vertex $0$ sends $T = \cO \oplus \cO(1) \oplus \cO(2)$ to $\cO(-1) \oplus \cO \oplus \cO(1)$, implementing the twist functor $F \mapsto F \otimes \cO(-1)$ on $\cD^b(K_{\bP^2})$. On the CoHA this acts as the spectral-parameter translation $\mu_0 : p_k(z) \mapsto p_k(z - \epsilon_3)$, commuting with the \v{C}ech transitions up to derived isomorphism: $\tau_{ij} \circ \mu_i = \mu_j \circ \tau_{ij}$. The overall structure is a simplicial diagram with mutations as internal morphisms at each vertex and \v{C}ech transitions as edge morphisms.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Stability polytope and canonical form]
\label{rem:local-p2-stability-polytope}
The Nagao--Nakajima 2011 (arXiv:$0809.2992$) stability space for the Beilinson quiver on $K_{\bP^2}$ is $\Theta = \R^3/\R_{\mathrm{diag}}$, a two-dimensional stability polytope carrying three chambers separated by three walls at $\zeta_0 = 0, \zeta_1 = 0, \zeta_2 = 0$, arranged as the three edges of a triangle. Each vertex of the triangle corresponds to a $T$-fixed ideal sheaf on $K_{\bP^2}$ (equivalently, a three-dimensional partition in the toric cone); each edge carries a wall-crossing shuffle-algebra automorphism; the interior is the generic chamber where all three $T$-fixed points contribute. The canonical two-form
\[
 \Omega_{K_{\bP^2}} \;=\; \frac{d\zeta_0 \wedge d\zeta_1}{\zeta_0 \zeta_1 \zeta_2}, \qquad \zeta_0 + \zeta_1 + \zeta_2 = 0,
\]
has residues at the three vertices giving the three chamber contributions; pulled back via the GV/DT correspondence it recovers $Z^{\mathrm{DT}}(K_{\bP^2})$ up to a MacMahon-function normalisation.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Why local \texorpdfstring{$\bP^2$}{P-2} works where the conifold does not]
\label{rem:local-p2-conifold-contrast}
The conifold two-chart cover (§\ref{sec:conifold-two-chart-cech}) has the Klebanov--Witten quartic $W_{\mathrm{con}} = \mathrm{tr}(a_1 b_1 a_2 b_2 - a_1 b_2 a_2 b_1)$ (Definition~\ref{def:kw-quiver-superpotential}) as its \emph{global} NCCR potential, which cannot decompose into Jordan-triple cubics on any chart cover: the affine base $X_0 = \mathrm{Spec}\,\C[x,y,z,w]/(xy-zw)$ has infinite class group $\mathrm{Cl}(X_0) = \Z$ (Remark~\ref{rem:kw-nccr-class-group-obstruction}), obstructing any presentation $X_0 = \C^3/G$ with finite $G$. Local $\bP^2$ succeeds because $K_{\bP^2} \simeq [\C^3/\Z_3]^{\mathrm{res}}$ provides a canonical single-vertex Jordan-triple quiver on the stacky cover, descending to the nine-arrow Beilinson quiver by skew-group Morita equivalence; the three toric charts of the crepant resolution then give a \v{C}ech cover compatible with the $\Z_3$-orbifold structure. The McKay correspondence is the bridge between the \emph{chart} data (three $\C^3$'s with Jordan-triple quivers) and the \emph{global} data (one Beilinson quiver with skew-group algebra). Other toric families where analogous first-principles gluings work: $\C^3/\Z_n$ for $n \in \{2, 3, 4, 6\}$ (McKay type A); $K_{\bP^1 \times \bP^1}$ (four charts, six pairwise overlaps); local Hirzebruch $K_{\bF_n}$ (with base Euler-class corrections). Beyond the toric regime, no analogous atlas construction exists; $K3 \times E$, the quintic, and general compact CY$_3$ require the conjectural $\Phi_3$ functor rather than an explicit chart-wise construction.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3} and \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K_{\bP^2}) = 3/2$}{kappa-ch(K-P-2) = 3/2}]
\label{rem:local-p2-phi3-kappa}
The CoHA construction above supplies the local-surface Hall shadow of
$\Perf(K_{\bP^2})$, obtained through the $\Z_3$ McKay/orbifold
correction and the Beilinson-quiver shuffle presentation. It is not, by
itself, the full Stage-$1$ object
$\PhiFA_3(\Perf(K_{\bP^2}))$. The $E_3$-holomorphic structure belongs to
the Costello--Gwilliam factorisation algebra of anti-canonical
holomorphic Chern--Simons on $K_{\bP^2}$, and comparison with the Hall
shadow requires the oriented hCS--Hall datum of
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy3-chain-level-bridge}. The second-stage
specialisation
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C}\colon E_3\text{-}\HolFA(K_{\bP^2})\to
E_1\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(C)$ is therefore conjectural at chain level;
under the expected Gaiotto--Rapcak specialisation it should produce
$\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda_{\mathrm{GR}}]$ with
$\lambda_{\mathrm{GR}}=\epsilon_1/\epsilon_3+\epsilon_2/\epsilon_3$ and
a $\Z_3$-orbifold sector. The value
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K_{\bP^2}) = 3/2$ is the local-surface
Hodge-supertrace reading, equivalently
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^2)/2=3/2$ by
Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p2-shadow}(i).
\end{remark}

\section{Local \texorpdfstring{$\bP^1 \times \bP^1$}{P-1 x P-1}}
\label{sec:local-p1p1}

\begin{theorem}[Local \texorpdfstring{$\bP^1 \times \bP^1$}{P-1 x P-1} shadow data]
\label{thm:local-p1p1-shadow}
Let $X = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-2,-2) \to \bP^1 \times \bP^1)$. The shadow data are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\text{local } \bP^1 \times \bP^1) = 2 = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^1 \times \bP^1)/2$.
 \item The shadow metric is the diagonal quadratic form $Q = \mathrm{diag}(1, 1)$ on the two-dimensional space of curve classes $H^2(\bP^1 \times \bP^1, \Z) = \Z e_1 \oplus \Z e_2$, where $e_1, e_2$ are the fiber classes of the two $\bP^1$ factors.
 \item The symmetric sector (classes with $d_1 = d_2$, i.e.\ along the diagonal) has shadow depth $r_{\max} = \infty$ (class M): the shadow obstruction tower does not terminate along the symmetric diagonal.
 \item The anti-symmetric sector (classes with $d_1 = -d_2$, i.e.\ along the anti-diagonal) has shadow depth $r_{\max} = 2$ (class G): the shadow obstruction tower terminates at degree $2$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) The compact base $\bP^1 \times \bP^1$ has $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^1 \times \bP^1) = 4$ (each factor contributes $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^1) = 2$), so $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/2 = 2$ by the general local-surface formula (Remark~\ref{rem:toric-kappa-patterns}).

(ii) The intersection form on $H^2(\bP^1 \times \bP^1)$ in the basis $\{e_1, e_2\}$ is $\langle e_i, e_j \rangle = \delta_{ij}$ (the two rulings are orthogonal with self-intersection $0$ in the total space, but the relevant inner product for the shadow metric is the Euler pairing $\chi(e_i, e_j) = \delta_{ij}$).

(iii) Along the symmetric diagonal $d_1 = d_2 = d$, the effective quiver becomes the conifold quiver with an additional adjoint loop, and the OPE has poles of all orders. Along the anti-diagonal, the two $\bP^1$ factors decouple and each contributes a single pole, giving class G.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 84 tests in \texttt{local\_p1p1\_coha.py} covering $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$, diagonal shadow metric, symmetric/anti-symmetric depth classification, and GV invariants through bi-degree $(6,6)$.

\section{5d Chern--Simons theory}
\label{sec:5d-cs}

The 5d holomorphic Chern--Simons theory on a toric CY$_3$ $X$ gives the
physical normal form behind the Hall shadow. It relates Feynman diagrams,
shuffle algebras, Drinfeld doubles, and the $\cW_{1+\infty}$
vacuum-module evaluation, but the CoHA remains the positive half; the
full Yangian and vertex-algebra actions enter only after doubling,
centres, and representation evaluation.

\begin{theorem}[Three-path verification for \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}]
\label{thm:5d-cs-c3}
\textup{(physical comparison normal form;
Schiffmann--Vasserot proves the associative CoHA positive half)}
For $X = \C^3$, three computations land on compatible normal forms:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \emph{Feynman diagrams}: the tree-level $n$-point amplitudes of the holomorphic CS theory on $\C^3$ produce the perturbative shuffle kernels after the Costello--Li renormalised QME package is fixed.
 \item \emph{Shuffle algebra}: the shuffle product on the equivariant $K$-theory $K^{T}(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2))$ (with equivariant parameters $\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, \epsilon_3$ satisfying $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$) is isomorphic to the CoHA product on $\cH(\C^3)$.
 \item \emph{$\cW_{1+\infty}$ evaluation}: the Miura transform gives the vacuum/Fock representation of the full affine Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ at the self-dual level $\psi = 1$; its positive half $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is the CoHA (Theorem~\ref{thm:sv-c3}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Conifold perturbative sector]
\label{prop:conifold-perturbative}
The perturbative sector of 5d CS on the resolved conifold agrees with that of $\C^3$: the tree-level amplitudes in both chambers coincide with the $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ structure constants. The non-perturbative difference (instanton corrections from the compact $\bP^1$) is captured by the wall-crossing gauge transformation of Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-wall-crossing}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Local \texorpdfstring{$\bP^2$}{P-2} loop correction from 5d CS]
\label{prop:5d-cs-local-p2}
The one-loop determinant of 5d holomorphic CS on local $\bP^2$ gives the loop correction
\[
 c_{\mathrm{loop}} = -\frac{1}{15},
\]
matching Proposition~\ref{prop:local-p2-loop}. The computation proceeds by zeta-function regularization of the product $\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-\chi(\cO_{\bP^2}(n))}$ where $\chi(\cO_{\bP^2}(n)) = \binom{n+2}{2}$.
\end{proposition}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 82 tests in \texttt{5d\_cs\_toric.py} covering the three-path $\C^3$ verification, conifold perturbative agreement, and local $\bP^2$ loop correction.

\section{Wall-crossing as MC gauge equivalence}
\label{sec:wall-crossing-mc}

The Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing formula, viewed through the shadow obstruction tower, is a gauge equivalence between MC elements.

\begin{theorem}[Wall-crossing \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} MC gauge]
\label{thm:wall-crossing-mc}
Let $X$ be a toric CY3 and $\zeta, \zeta'$ two generic stability parameters in adjacent chambers separated by a wall $W$. Let $\Theta_\zeta, \Theta_{\zeta'} \in \mathrm{MC}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{A_X})$ be the corresponding MC elements. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item $\Theta_{\zeta'}$ and $\Theta_\zeta$ are gauge-equivalent: $\Theta_{\zeta'} = e^{\ad_\alpha}(\Theta_\zeta)$ for a unique $\alpha \in \fn_W$ supported on the wall divisor.
 \item The pentagon identity holds through height $2$: for any sequence of five wall-crossings forming a pentagon in the exchange graph (the $A_2$ cluster mutations), the composition of gauge transformations is the identity, verified through representations of dimension $\leq 2$ (the Reineke convention).
 \item The Jacobi algebra condition $\partial W / \partial a_i = 0$ is equivalent to $\partial_W^2 = 0$ in the Ginzburg dg algebra $\Pi(Q, W)$ (the CoHA itself carries no internal differential; see Theorem~\ref{thm:coha-is-algebra-not-coalgebra}), which is the MC equation $D\Theta + \frac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0$ for $\Theta_W \in \Pi(Q,W)_{\mathrm{Lie}}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) The KS wall-crossing formula expresses the change of DT invariants across a wall as a product of symplectomorphisms in the motivic Hall algebra. In the convolution dg Lie algebra, this product becomes the exponential gauge action $\exp(\ad_\alpha)$ with $\alpha$ the BPS invariant on the wall class.

(ii) The pentagon identity is verified by explicit computation. At height $\leq 2$ in the Reineke stability convention (stability parameter $\theta \colon K_0 \to \R$ with $\theta(\mathbf{d}) > 0$ for $\theta$-stable representations), the five gauge parameters $\alpha_1, \ldots, \alpha_5$ satisfy $\exp(\ad_{\alpha_5}) \circ \cdots \circ \exp(\ad_{\alpha_1}) = \id$ by direct evaluation on the $5$-dimensional space of representations of dimension $\leq 2$.

(iii) The superpotential $W$ defines the differential $\partial_W$ on the path algebra $\C Q / (\partial W)$. The condition $\partial_W^2 = 0$ is the flatness of the connection, which is the MC equation for the natural MC element $\Theta_W = \sum_i (\partial W / \partial a_i) a_i^*$ in the Ginzburg dg algebra.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 73 tests in \texttt{conifold\_bar\_complex.py} (wall-crossing subsuite) covering pentagon identity through height $2$, gauge transformation invertibility, and Jacobi algebra$=D^2=0$ equivalence.

\section{The \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch}-table for toric CY3}
\label{sec:toric-kappa-table}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch}-values for the standard toric CY3 family]
\label{prop:toric-kappa-table}
The modular characteristics of the standard toric CY3 geometries are:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{llccc}
\toprule
Geometry & Quiver & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & Class & $r_{\max}$ \\
\midrule
$\C^3$ & Jordan & $1$ & G & $2$ \\
Resolved conifold & Klebanov--Witten & $1$ & G & $2$ \\
Local $\bP^2$ & McKay $\Z_3$ & $3/2$ & M & $\infty$ \\
Local $\bP^1 \times \bP^1$ & Beilinson & $2$ & M/G & $\infty$/$2$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
For local $\bP^1 \times \bP^1$, the class depends on the sector: M along the symmetric diagonal, G along the anti-symmetric diagonal (Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p1p1-shadow}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Patterns in the \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch}-table]
\label{rem:toric-kappa-patterns}
Three structural patterns emerge:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \emph{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ from the compact base}: for local CY3 geometries of the form $X = \mathrm{Tot}(K_S \to S)$ over a smooth projective surface $S$, the modular characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$, giving $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\text{local } \bP^2) = 3/2$ ($\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^2) = 3$) and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\text{local } \bP^1 \times \bP^1) = 2$ ($\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^1 \times \bP^1) = 4$). For geometries not of the form $\mathrm{Tot}(K_S)$, the value is computed from DT invariants directly: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = 1$ (from the MacMahon plethystic logarithm) and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\text{conifold}) = 1$ (from the single compact curve class). Note: the conifold is $\mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-1)^{\oplus 2} \to \bP^1)$, which is \emph{not} $\mathrm{Tot}(K_{\bP^1}) = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-2) \to \bP^1)$, so the $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$ formula does not apply to it directly. This $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$ is a topological quantity attached to the compact base, distinct from $c/2$ of any Virasoro subalgebra.
 \item \emph{Shadow class from the quiver}: class G occurs when the quiver has a single nontrivial vertex (Jordan, KW) and class M occurs when the quiver has $\Z_N$-symmetry for $N \geq 3$ (McKay). The conifold, despite having two vertices, is class G because the Klebanov--Witten quiver has a $\Z_2$-symmetry that terminates the tower at degree $2$.
 \item \emph{Wall-crossing preserves $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}: the modular characteristic is chamber-independent (it depends only on the topology of the compact base, not on the stability parameter). This is a manifestation of the gauge invariance of Theorem~\ref{thm:wall-crossing-mc}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Factorisation base: smooth \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3} versus \texorpdfstring{$\Ran(E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24}) + \overline{\cA}_2$}{Ran(E-nod,sm-24) + overlinecA-2}]
\label{rem:tcy3-coha-conifold}
A second axis separating the toric CoHA family from its non-toric $K3 \times E$ companion is the \emph{factorisation base}. Toric CY$_3$ geometries are organised over a smooth Ran space:
\[
 \Ran(\C^3) \;\supset\; \Ran(\C^2) \;\supset\; \Ran(\C)
 \qquad\text{(smooth tower, abelian gerbe trivial)},
\]
with the CoHA factorisation algebra living over $\Ran(\C)$ after imposing the holomorphic-topological twist in one $\C$-direction. All strata are smooth; no collision loci carry modular moduli. The non-toric $K3 \times E$ chiral quantum group of Part~\ref{part:k3-yangian} is instead supported on a \emph{bi-based} factorisation structure
\[
 \Ran\bigl(E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24}\bigr) \;+\; \overline{\cA}_2,
\]
a nodal elliptic base with $24$ Kodaira $I_1$ fibres (the generic
Weierstrass family) glued to the closure $\overline{\cA}_2$ of the
rank-$2$ principally polarised abelian moduli space, with the joining
map the averaging map $\av$ of the programme. The $24$ nodal fibres
$\{E^{\mathrm{nod}}_i\}_{i=1}^{24}$ give local $\C^3$-type positive-half
models. They do not assemble by themselves into the compact
$K3\times E$ CoHA. At most one has a schematic local comparison
\[
 Y^+_{\mathrm{loc}}(K3 \times E)
 \;\rightsquigarrow\;
 \operatorname{hocolim}_{\,i = 1, \ldots, 24}\,
 \mathrm{CoHA}(\C^3)_i
 \;\otimes\;
 \mathrm{mod}_{\widetilde{M}_{24}},
\]
where each local $\mathrm{CoHA}(\C^3)_i \simeq
Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ reuses the Schiffmann--Vasserot identification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:sv-c3}. The missing compact data are the oriented
critical stack, compact-support functoriality, PBW theorem, coproduct,
and non-degenerate pairing. Davison~2017 supplies CY-$3$ integrality
evidence on the compact category; it does not identify
$\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ with a hocolim of toric stalks.
\end{remark}

\section{Gaudin Hamiltonians from toric CY3 collision residues}
\label{sec:toric-cy3-gaudin}

The collision residue $r(z) = \operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm{coll}}_{0,2}(\Theta_A)$
of the MC element extracts, at genus~$0$ and degree~$2$, the classical
$r$-matrix of the quantum group associated to a CY3 geometry.
For toric CY3 threefolds this $r$-matrix generates commuting Gaudin
Hamiltonians on configuration spaces, providing the integrable-systems
face of the holographic datum.

\begin{construction}[Toric CY3 Gaudin Hamiltonians]
\label{constr:toric-gaudin}
Let $X$ be a toric CY3 with associated affine super Yangian
$Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:rsyz}). For $n$ distinct
points $z_1, \ldots, z_n \in \C$, the \emph{Gaudin Hamiltonians} are
the pairwise sums
\[
 H_i \;=\; \sum_{j \neq i} \frac{\Omega_{ij}^{Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})}}{z_i - z_j},
 \qquad i = 1, \ldots, n,
\]
where $\Omega_{ij}^{Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})} \in
\End(V_1 \otimes \cdots \otimes V_n)$ is the image of the quadratic
Casimir $\Omega \in Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X}) \otimes
Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$ acting in the $i$-th and $j$-th tensor factors.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Commutativity of Gaudin Hamiltonians]
\label{prop:toric-gaudin-commute}
The operators $H_1, \ldots, H_n$ pairwise commute:
$[H_i, H_j] = 0$ for all $i, j$. The proof reduces to the
classical Yang--Baxter equation for $r(z)$, which follows from
$D^2 = 0$ in the bar complex projected to genus~$0$, degree~$3$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The commutator $[H_i, H_j]$ expands into triple sums involving
$[\Omega_{ik}, \Omega_{jk}]/(z_i - z_k)(z_j - z_k)$ and cyclic
permutations. The classical Yang--Baxter equation
$[r_{12}(z_{12}), r_{13}(z_{13})] + [r_{12}(z_{12}), r_{23}(z_{23})]
+ [r_{13}(z_{13}), r_{23}(z_{23})] = 0$
(where $z_{ij} = z_i - z_j$) forces exact cancellation. The CYBE
itself is the degree-$3$ projection of the MC equation
$D\Theta + \frac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0$ at genus~$0$
(cf.\ Vol~I, Theorem~\ref{thm:gaudin-yangian-identification}).
\end{proof}

For $X = \C^3$ (the Jordan quiver), the Yangian is
$Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ and the Casimir is the standard
$\Omega = \sum_a J_a \otimes J^a$ with $J_a$ the
generators of $\widehat{\fgl}_1$. The Gaudin system on
$n$ points then coincides with the $\widehat{\fgl}_1$
Gaudin model studied by Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao
in the context of $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at the self-dual level.
The spectral curve of the Gaudin system is the
Seiberg--Witten curve of the 4d $\cN = 2$ theory
obtained by compactification of the 5d holomorphic
Chern--Simons theory on $X$.

\begin{proposition}[Wall-crossing as Gaudin gauge equivalence]
\label{prop:wall-crossing-gaudin}
Let $\zeta, \zeta'$ be generic stability parameters in adjacent
chambers. The wall-crossing gauge transformation
$\Theta_{\zeta'} = e^{\ad_\alpha}(\Theta_\zeta)$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:wall-crossing-mc}) induces a gauge equivalence
of the associated Gaudin systems: the Hamiltonians $H_i^\zeta$ and
$H_i^{\zeta'}$ are conjugate by the gauge operator
$e^{\alpha} \in \exp(\fn_W)$, hence have identical spectra.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Gaudin Hamiltonians are extracted from the degree-$2$ projection
of $\Theta$; the gauge transformation $e^{\ad_\alpha}$ acts on this
projection by conjugation, preserving commutativity and spectra.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Remark}. The Gaudin construction extends to the
resolved conifold (Klebanov--Witten Yangian) and to local $\bP^2$
(McKay super Yangian), giving integrable spin chains whose Bethe
ansatz equations encode DT invariants. The integrability of the
Gaudin system is the dynamical manifestation of $D^2 = 0$ in the
bar complex.


%% ========================================================================
%% Chiral quantum group structure for general toric CY3
%% ========================================================================

\section{The chiral quantum group of a toric CY3}
\label{sec:toric-cy3-chiral-qg}

The preceding sections isolate the quantum-group layers: the positive
CoHA half (Theorems~\ref{thm:sv-c3} and~\ref{thm:rsyz}), the
Maulik--Okounkov $R$-matrix on the representation side, the Drinfeld
center passage from $\Eone$ to $\Etwo$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center} for $\C^3$), and the Hall
chart-gluing diagram for toric CY3
(Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}). Only the positive half is
automatic from the CoHA theorem. The double, center, PBW, and chiral
comparison layers require their own hypotheses.

\begin{theorem}[Chiral quantum group structure for toric CY3]
\label{thm:toric-cy3-chiral-qg}
Let $X = X_\Sigma$ be a smooth toric CY3 without compact $4$-cycles,
with toric quiver with potential $(Q_X, W_X)$.
The following five-component chiral quantum group datum
$\mathcal{G}(X)$ is defined on the locus where the toric fan supplies
the RSYZ positive half and the extra Hopf-pairing, PBW, center, and
framed hCS--Hall comparison data listed below:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\Roman*)}]
 \item \textbf{$\Eone$-bialgebra \textup{(}CoHA\textup{)}.}
 The critical CoHA
 \[
 \mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X) \;\simeq\; Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})
 \]
 is the positive half of the affine super Yangian
 $Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$
 \textup{(}Schiffmann--Vasserot for $\C^3$;
 Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao for general toric\textup{)}.
 The CoHA carries an associative
 $\Eone$ multiplication \textup{(}the Hall product\textup{)} and
 a coassociative coproduct
 \textup{(}the Yang--Zhao localization coproduct\textup{)},
 making $\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X)$ a topological bialgebra
 over the charge lattice
 $\Gamma(X) = K_0(\Coh(X))$.

 \item \textbf{$R$-matrix.}
 The Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope construction produces a
 Yang $R$-matrix
 $R^{\mathrm{MO}}(z) \in \End(V \otimes V)((z))$
 for each pair of
 representations $V$ of $Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$, satisfying:
 \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item the Yang--Baxter equation
 $R_{12}(u{-}v)\,R_{13}(u)\,R_{23}(v) =
 R_{23}(v)\,R_{13}(u)\,R_{12}(u{-}v)$;
 \item unitarity $R(z)\,R(-z) = \mathrm{Id}$
 \textup{(}from the CY condition\textup{)};
 \item wall-crossing covariance: for adjacent stability
 chambers $\sigma, \sigma'$, the $R$-matrices are
 gauge-equivalent
 $R^{\sigma'}(z) = (g \otimes g)\,R^\sigma(z)\,
 (g^{-1} \otimes g^{-1})$
 with $g$ the MO wall-crossing operator
 \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:transition-e1-equiv}(ii)}\textup{)}.
 \end{enumerate}

 \item \textbf{Drinfeld double.}
 If a non-degenerate completed Hopf pairing and PBW triangular
 decomposition have been supplied, the completed Drinfeld double
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eq:toric-drinfeld-double}
 D(\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X))
 \;=\; Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})
 \;=\; Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})
 \otimes Y^0(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})
 \otimes Y^-(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})
 \end{equation}
 is the full affine super Yangian on that pairing locus,
 where $Y^-$ is the CoHA of the opposite quiver
 $(Q_X^{\mathrm{op}}, W_X^{\mathrm{op}})$
 and $Y^0$ is the Cartan subalgebra
 generated by the charge lattice $\Gamma(X)$.
 The cross-relations between $Y^+$ and $Y^-$ are
 determined by the Hopf pairing, equivalently by the $R$-matrix of
 \textup{(II)} after the double has been constructed.
 For $\C^3$: $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is the completed mode/enveloping
 algebra acting on the $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ vacuum/Fock modules
 \textup{(}Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak\textup{)}.

 \item \textbf{Hall positive half and conditional chiral comparison.}
 The Hall-side chart gluing gives the positive half
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eq:toric-global-chiral}
 H^+_{X_\Sigma}
 \;=\; \operatorname{hocolim}_{\Sigma(3)}\,
 \CoHA(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)
 \end{equation}
