 as an associative $\Eone$ Hall algebra. It is not by itself the
 global chiral algebra. If the oriented DWR/Ran/Rees hCS--Hall
 comparison and the chiral-envelope comparison of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}\textup{(iv)} are supplied, the
 parallel hocolim of chiral envelopes gives an $E_1$-chiral algebra
 $A_{X_\Sigma}$ and a comparison from $H^+_{X_\Sigma}$ to its Hall
 global sections. The atlas
 $\{(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)\}_{\alpha \in \Sigma(3)}$ is assembled by the
 chart-gluing theorem
 \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}}\textup{)}.
 Each transition is an $\Eone$ quasi-isomorphism
 \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:mutation-e1-equivalence}}\textup{)}.
 The descent spectral sequence degenerates at $E_2$
 \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:e1-descent-degeneration}}\textup{)},
 so the Hall hocolim is determined by pairwise wall-crossing data on
 the comparison locus.

 \item \textbf{Modular characteristic and shadow tower.}
 On the no-compact-four-cycle locus, the modular characteristic is read
 from the positive-half Hall shadow and its vertex/Fock specialisation.
 The local-surface formula
 $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K_S)=\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$
 belongs to the compact-four-cycle extension
 \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:toric-kappa-table}}\textup{)}, not
 to the zero-interior RSYZ theorem above.
 It is independent of the stability chamber
 \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:flop-koszul-duality}(iii)}\textup{)}.
 The full shadow obstruction tower $\Theta_{A_X}$
 satisfies the MC equation
 $D\Theta + \frac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0$,
 with the shadow class \textup{(}G, L, C, or M\textup{)} determined
 by the quiver combinatorics
 \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:toric-kappa-table}}\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Compatibility.}
The five components are mutually consistent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item The $R$-matrix of~\textup{(II)} is the collision
 residue of the MC element:
 $r(z) = \operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm{coll}}_{0,2}(\Theta_{A_X})$,
 and the classical Yang--Baxter equation for $r(z)$
 follows from $D^2 = 0$ at genus~$0$, degree~$3$
 \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:toric-gaudin-commute}}\textup{)}.
 \item The Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing formula
 is equivalent to the MC gauge equivalence
 $\Theta_{\zeta'} = e^{\ad_\alpha}(\Theta_\zeta)$
 between adjacent chambers
 \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:ks-mc-gauge}}\textup{)}.
 \item The Costello--Li comparison, when the oriented hCS--Hall datum is
 supplied,
 $A_{X_\Sigma}^{\mathrm{hocolim}}
 \simeq_{\Eone} U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}_{X_\Sigma})$
 compares the chiral-envelope hocolim with the boundary algebra
 of $5$d holomorphic Chern--Simons on $X_\Sigma \times \R^2$; it is not
 a consequence of Hall gluing alone
 \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}(iv)}\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Component~(I) is the combination of
Theorem~\ref{thm:sv-c3} ($\C^3$) and
Theorem~\ref{thm:rsyz} (general toric), which identify the
critical CoHA with $Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$. The coproduct
is the localization coproduct of Yang--Zhao, defined by
restriction to the attracting set of a one-parameter subgroup
in the equivariant cohomology of the representation variety.

Component~(II): the Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope $R$-matrix
is constructed for all toric CY3 in the equivariant $K$-theory of
Nakajima quiver varieties. The Yang--Baxter equation is proved
in MO by the geometric argument (Steinberg correspondence).
Unitarity follows from the CY condition: the structure function
$g(z) = \prod_i (z - h_i) / (z + h_i)$ satisfies
$g(z)g(-z) = 1$ (see equation~\eqref{eq:cy-condition-rmatrix}).
Wall-crossing covariance follows from the
stable-envelope wall-crossing formula of MO.

Component~(III): the Drinfeld double construction is standard only
after a Hopf algebra with non-degenerate pairing has been supplied.
For $\C^3$ this gives $D(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)) =
Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$. For a general toric CY$_3$ without compact
$4$-cycles, the same formula is the triangular-decomposition
hypothesis on the RSYZ affine super Yangian: the opposite CoHA supplies
the negative Borel, the Cartan is the group algebra of
$\Gamma(X)=K_0(\Coh(X))$, and the pairing must be checked case by
case.

Component~(IV) is the Hall-positive-half part of
Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}; the comparison with a chiral
envelope is its oriented hCS--Hall hypothesis.

Component~(V): the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-values are
established in Proposition~\ref{prop:toric-kappa-table}.
Chamber-independence follows from
Proposition~\ref{prop:flop-koszul-duality}(iii).
The MC equation for $\Theta_{A_X}$ is available on the cases where the
Vol~I shadow tower and the bar-hocolim comparison apply; it is not a
formal consequence of the Hall positive half alone.

Compatibility~(a): the collision residue extraction
$r(z) = \mathrm{Res}^{\mathrm{coll}}_{0,2}(\Theta_A)$
is a general construction from Vol~I (Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral},
seven-faces formalism). The CYBE is the degree-$3$
projection of the MC equation at genus~$0$.

Compatibility~(b) is Theorem~\ref{thm:ks-mc-gauge}.

Compatibility~(c) is Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}(iv).
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2} structure and Conjecture CY-C]
\label{rem:toric-e2-centre-scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-cy3-chiral-qg} first constructs the Hall
positive half by chart gluing. The $\Eone$-chiral algebra
$A_{X_\Sigma}$ appears only after the oriented hCS--Hall and
chiral-envelope comparison data have been supplied. The
$\Etwo$-braided structure is a centre-side structure, not a second
operation on the positive half:
\[
 \cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_{X_\Sigma}))
 \;\simeq\;
 \Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X}))
\]
only after the Drinfeld double, PBW triangular decomposition, and
non-degenerate pairing have identified the full affine super Yangian.
For $\C^3$ this is proved
(Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}). For general toric CY3, the
identification is the toric form of Conjecture~CY-C
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:qg-realization}). The difficulty is that the
Drinfeld center does not commute with homotopy colimits (the
CY-to-chiral $d=3$ evidence remark, obstacle~\textbf{O3}): the global
$\Etwo$-braided category for a multi-chart toric CY3 cannot be assembled
chart-by-chart from the local Drinfeld centers.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[Comparison: \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3} versus general toric CY3]
\label{rem:c3-vs-general-toric}
For $\C^3$, the five-step functor chain
(Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-functor-chain}) provides stronger results:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The classical bracket is abelian
 (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}), so all noncommutative
 structure arises from the $\Omega$-deformation.
 For general toric CY3 with orbifold singularities,
 the McKay quiver can have a nonabelian classical bracket.
 \item The $\Omega$-deformation is unique ($\HH^2 = 1$,
 Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-hochschild}). For general toric CY3,
 the deformation space may be multi-dimensional
 (spanned by the independent equivariant parameters
 subject to the CY constraint).
 \item The Drinfeld center identification is verified on the
 stated Hall/representation hypotheses for $\C^3$:
 $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)))
 \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$, followed by the
 appropriate $\cW_{1+\infty}$ vacuum/Fock module realization. For general toric
 CY3, this identification is conditional on Conjecture~CY-C.
 \item The evaluation target $\cW_{1+\infty}$ has a vertex algebra
 presentation (Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak). For general toric CY3,
 the full Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$ is defined as a
 shuffle algebra or RTT algebra, but an explicit vertex algebra
 presentation (strong generators, OPE) exists only for
 $\C^3$ and the conifold.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}


\section{The chiral quantum group equivalence for toric CY3}
\label{sec:toric-chiral-qg-triangle}

Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-cy3-chiral-qg} separates five components
of the chiral quantum group datum for toric CY3 without compact
$4$-cycles. The CoHA positive half is proved by Schiffmann--Vasserot
and RSYZ; the $R$-matrix, Drinfeld double, chart comparison, and shadow
tower live on additional representation, pairing, hCS--Hall, and
bar-comparison hypotheses. These components are \emph{a priori}
separate structures. The Vol~I chiral quantum group equivalence
asserts that three of them
(the vertex $R$-matrix, the chiral $A_\infty$-structure, and the
chiral coproduct) determine each other on the Koszul locus. The theorem
below specializes the abstract equivalence to the toric CY3 setting,
where the required comparison data have been supplied.

\begin{theorem}[Chiral quantum group equivalence for toric CY3]
\label{thm:toric-chiral-qg-specialization}
Let $X = X_\Sigma$ be a smooth toric CY3 without compact $4$-cycles,
with toric quiver $(Q_X, W_X)$ and critical CoHA
$\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$
\textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:rsyz}}\textup{)}.
Let $H^+_{X}$ denote the Hall positive-half hocolim of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:toric-cy3-chiral-qg}(IV)}. Assume the
oriented hCS--Hall comparison and chiral-envelope hocolim identify this
Hall shadow with the Hall global sections of an $\Eone$-chiral algebra
$A_X$. This assumption is separate from the $d = 3$ correspondence
$\Phi_3$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}); chain-level explicit
constructions for non-formal algebras remain open outside the stated
comparison locus. The ordered bar complex of $A_X$ is
$\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X) = T^c(s^{-1}\bar{A}_X)$
\textup{(}deconcatenation coproduct, $|s^{-1}v| = |v| - 1$\textup{)}.
On the Koszul locus, the following three structures on $A_X$
determine each other:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\Roman*)}]
 \item \textbf{Vertex $R$-matrix.}
 The Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope $R$-matrix
 $R^{\mathrm{MO}}(z) \in \End(V \otimes V)((z))$
 of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:toric-cy3-chiral-qg}(II)},
 satisfying the Yang--Baxter equation, unitarity
 $R(z)R(-z) = \mathrm{Id}$, and the shift condition.

 \item \textbf{Chiral $A_\infty$-structure.}
 The bar differential on $\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X)$ determines
 chiral $A_\infty$-maps
 $m_k^{\mathrm{ch}} \colon A_X^{\otimes k}
 \to A_X((\lambda_1))\cdots((\lambda_{k-1}))$
 satisfying the Stasheff identities in the chiral endomorphism
 operad $\End^{\mathrm{ch}}_{A_X}$.

 \item \textbf{Chiral coproduct.}
 The deconcatenation coproduct on $\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X)$
 yields, via cobar recovery
 $\Omega(\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X)) \xrightarrow{\sim} A_X$
 \textup{(}on the Koszul locus\textup{)}, a chiral coproduct
 $\Delta^{\mathrm{ch}} \colon A_X \to
 (A_X \mathbin{\hat\otimes} A_X)((z))$,
 coassociative up to conjugation by the Drinfeld associator
 $\Phi \in \exp(\hat{\mathfrak{t}}_3)$.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip\noindent
The equivalences are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \textup{(I)} $\to$ \textup{(II)}: restriction of the
 vertex OPE to the real Stasheff associahedra
 $K_n \hookrightarrow
 \overline{\operatorname{FM}}_n^{\mathrm{ord}}(\bC)$;
 \item \textup{(II)} $\to$ \textup{(III)}: cobar applied to
 the deconcatenation coproduct on $\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X)$;
 \item \textup{(III)} $\to$ \textup{(I)}: the chiral Drinfeld
 formula $R(z) = \sigma \circ \Delta^{\mathrm{ch}}
 \circ (\Delta^{\mathrm{ch}, \mathrm{op}})^{-1}$.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Concrete identifications.}
Under the RSYZ isomorphism $\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X) \simeq
Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The vertex $R$-matrix \textup{(I)} is identified with the
 MO stable-envelope $R$-matrix of the Nakajima quiver variety
 $\mathfrak{M}(Q_X, \mathbf{d})$;
 \item The chiral $A_\infty$-maps \textup{(II)} correspond to
 the CoHA multiplication and higher compositions: the Hall
 product gives $m_2^{\mathrm{ch}}$, and higher-order contact
 terms give $m_k^{\mathrm{ch}}$ for $k \geq 3$. These
 $A_\infty$-maps are encoded in the bar differential
 $d_{\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}} = \sum_{k \geq 1} b_k$ of
 $B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X)$, but the bar complex is a
 \emph{coalgebra} constructed from $A_X$, not the CoHA
 itself;
 \item The chiral coproduct \textup{(III)} is identified with
 the Yang--Zhao localization coproduct on the CoHA,
 defined by restriction to attracting sets of one-parameter
 subgroups in equivariant cohomology;
 \item The classical $r$-matrix
 $r(z) = \operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm{coll}}_{0,2}
 (\Theta_{A_X})$
 \textup{(}collision residue of the MC element\textup{)}
 is the classical limit of $R^{\mathrm{MO}}(z)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The abstract equivalence (I) $\leftrightarrow$ (II)
$\leftrightarrow$ (III) is the Vol~I chiral quantum group equivalence,
proved for any $\Eone$-chiral algebra on the Koszul locus. The content
of the present theorem is conditional identification of each abstract
vertex with the corresponding toric CY3 structure after the stated
Hall--chiral comparison data are supplied.

\textit{Component~\textup{(I)}: $R$-matrix.}
The $\Eone$-chiral algebra $A_X$ carries a vertex
$R$-matrix by the general theory
(item~\textup{(I)} of that equivalence). For toric CY3, the
Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope construction provides an
independent $R$-matrix on $Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$-modules.
For $\C^3$ the two coincide through the explicit
Schiffmann--Vasserot/Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak chain. For general toric
input, the identification is one of the comparison hypotheses: both
sides solve the Yang--Baxter equation with the same classical limit
$r(z) = \operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm{coll}}_{0,2}(\Theta_{A_X})$ only
after the Hall--chiral comparison has identified the collision residue
with the MO stable-envelope residue. Etingof--Kazhdan uniqueness then
applies on that Koszul comparison locus.

\textit{Component~\textup{(II)}: $A_\infty$.}
The bar differential on $\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X)
= T^c(s^{-1}\bar{A}_X)$ encodes the chiral
$A_\infty$-maps $m_k^{\mathrm{ch}}$ by definition.
On the CoHA side, the Hall product is
$m_2^{\mathrm{ch}}$, and the higher compositions
$m_k^{\mathrm{ch}}$ for $k \geq 3$ are the
higher-order contact terms arising from the
bar-complex boundary strata (associahedron facets).
For $\C^3$, all $m_k^{\mathrm{ch}}$ with $k \geq 3$
vanish: the CoHA is strictly associative (class~G,
shadow depth $r_{\max} = 2$). For local~$\bP^2$
(class~M), the $m_k^{\mathrm{ch}}$ are nonzero for
all $k$ and encode the full infinite shadow tower.

\textit{Component~\textup{(III)}: coproduct.}
The Yang--Zhao localization coproduct on
$\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X)$ is coassociative and compatible
with the Hall multiplication. The deconcatenation coproduct
on $\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X)$ produces, via the cobar
functor $\Omega$, a chiral coproduct on $A_X$ that is
coassociative up to the Drinfeld associator. On the Koszul
locus, $\Omega(\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_X))
\xrightarrow{\sim} A_X$ is a quasi-isomorphism
(Theorem~B of Vol~I), and the
transported coproduct is identified with the Yang--Zhao
coproduct by the universal property of the localization
construction (the attracting-set restriction computes the
same homotopy-coherent coproduct as the bar-cobar
transport).

\textit{Concrete identification~(iv).}
The classical $r$-matrix is the collision residue of
the MC element $\Theta_{A_X}$
(Vol~I seven-faces formalism). The classical limit
$R(z) = \mathrm{Id} + \hbar\, r(z) + O(\hbar^2)$
holds by the MO construction (the stable envelope
$R$-matrix is a quantization of the geometric
$r$-matrix with classical limit given by the
torus-fixed point localization).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why CoHA and bar complex share the MacMahon character]
\label{rem:coha-bar-macmahon}
For $\C^3$, both the CoHA graded dimension $\sum_n \dim \cH_n\, q^n$ and the $\Eone$ bar Euler characteristic $\chi(B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_{\C^3}), q)$ equal the MacMahon function $M(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}$. The coincidence is not accidental: it reflects the Schiffmann--Vasserot isomorphism $\cH(\C^3) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sv-c3}) in two ways.
On the \emph{geometric} side, the CoHA counts BPS states via $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^3)$; its graded dimension is $\sum_n \chi(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^3))\, q^n = M(q)$, where each BPS state corresponds to a $3$-dimensional partition.
On the \emph{combinatorial} side, $B^{\mathrm{ord}}(Y^+)$ is the coalgebra resolution of $Y^+$; its bar differential $d_B = \sum b_k$ encodes the algebra structure of $Y^+$ by definition, and the bar Euler characteristic records the same mode-operator counting as the algebra's graded dimension.
Since $\cH(\C^3) \cong Y^+$ \emph{as algebras}, the Hall product appears as $b_2$ in the bar differential: the two objects count the same modes through different lenses (geometric: DT invariants on Hilbert schemes; algebraic: tensor products of Yangian generators). But the CoHA and the bar complex remain distinct mathematical objects (one is an algebra, the other its coalgebra resolution), and the character coincidence is a \emph{consequence} of the isomorphism, not an identification.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[\texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}: positive half, double, and centre]
\label{cor:c3-chiral-qg-triangle}
For $X = \C^3$ \textup{(}Jordan quiver,
$\mathcal{H} \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$\textup{)},
the chiral quantum group equivalence of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:toric-chiral-qg-specialization}}
specializes to:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The vertex $R$-matrix is the MO stable-envelope
 $R$-matrix of $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2)$.
 \item The chiral $A_\infty$-structure is strictly
 associative: $m_k^{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$ for $k \geq 3$
 \textup{(}class~G, $r_{\max} = 2$\textup{)}.
 \item The chiral coproduct coincides with the
 shuffle coproduct on $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$.
 \item The Drinfeld double
 $D(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)) = Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$; the
 $\cW_{1+\infty}$ object is reached through the
 Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak Fock/evaluation representation of this full
 Yangian.
 \item The Drinfeld center
 $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)))
 \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$
 \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}}\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}
The positive-half identification, Drinfeld double, and Fock/evaluation
chain are unconditional for $\C^3$. The chiral $A_\infty$ and coproduct
readings use the Koszul comparison locus of
Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chiral-qg-specialization}. For general toric,
the Drinfeld center identification remains conditional on
Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:qg-realization}}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Items~(i)--(iii) follow from
Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chiral-qg-specialization} specialized
to the Jordan quiver. Item~(ii) uses the class~G classification
of $\C^3$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:conifold-shadow} for the conifold;
for $\C^3$ itself, $S_r = 0$ for $r \geq 3$ because the single-field
Heisenberg OPE has $r_{\max} = 2$). Item~(iv) is the triangular
decomposition $Y = Y^+ \otimes Y^0 \otimes Y^-$ combined with the
Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak Fock/evaluation representation. Item~(v) is
Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Conifold: wall-crossing preserves the triangle]
\label{cor:conifold-chiral-qg}
For the resolved conifold $X = \cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1) \to \bP^1$
\textup{(}Klebanov--Witten quiver\textup{)}, the chiral quantum
group equivalence of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:toric-chiral-qg-specialization}} holds
in both stability chambers $\zeta_1 > 0$ and $\zeta_1 < 0$. The
wall-crossing gauge transformation
$\Theta_{\mathrm{II}} = e^{\ad_\alpha}(\Theta_{\mathrm{I}})$
of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:conifold-wall-crossing}}
preserves each vertex of the triangle:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The $R$-matrices in the two chambers are gauge-equivalent:
 $R^{\mathrm{II}}(z) = (g \otimes g)\, R^{\mathrm{I}}(z)\,
 (g^{-1} \otimes g^{-1})$.
 \item The $A_\infty$-structures are $A_\infty$-quasi-isomorphic
 via the transfer induced by the gauge equivalence.
 \item The chiral coproducts are intertwined by the same
 gauge operator $g = e^\alpha$.
\end{enumerate}
In particular, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ is chamber-independent
\textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:toric-kappa-table}}\textup{)}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The MC gauge equivalence
$\Theta_{\mathrm{II}} = e^{\ad_\alpha}(\Theta_{\mathrm{I}})$
preserves the MC equation and hence each derived structure.
The $R$-matrix transforms by conjugation (compatibility~(a)
of Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-cy3-chiral-qg}). The $A_\infty$
transfer is the homotopy transfer theorem applied to the
quasi-isomorphism between the bar complexes in the two
chambers (the gauge transformation is a filtered
quasi-isomorphism on $\barB^{\mathrm{ord}}$). The coproduct
intertwining follows from the coassociativity of
deconcatenation and the naturality of the cobar functor
under quasi-isomorphisms of dg coalgebras.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[Extension to compact CY3]
\label{rem:compact-extension}
The toric hypothesis enters
Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-cy3-chiral-qg} in four places:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Existence of $\PhiFA_3$-stage output}:
 the Hall shadow compared with $\PhiFA_3(\cC)$ for toric CY3 is the
 critical CoHA, constructed for quivers with potential via the McKay
 correspondence after the oriented hCS--Hall comparison. For compact CY3 without torus action
 (quintic, generic $K3 \times E$), the equivariant-localisation
 regime is unavailable; the $\PhiFA_3$-stage output is instead
 the factorisation envelope of the polyvector-field Lie conformal
 algebra (Costello--Li), with $\hCS$ providing the $5$d chain-level
 model.
 \item \emph{RSYZ identification}:
 $\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$
 is specific to toric geometry (the shuffle-algebra
 presentation uses equivariant localization).
 \item \emph{MO $R$-matrix}:
 the stable-envelope construction requires a torus action
 on the Nakajima quiver variety. For compact CY3, the
 $R$-matrix must come from a different source (the
 chain-level $\bS^3$-framing of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}).
 \item \emph{Chart gluing}:
 the toric fan provides a finite atlas; for general CY3,
 the tilting-chart cover is conjectural
 (Conjecture~\ref{conj:tilting-chart-cover}).
\end{enumerate}
The extension to compact CY3 (including $K3 \times E$,
the quintic, and Enriques $\times E$) is available only on the
hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}: a witnessed
specialisation datum, the corrected TCFT package, or the complete
filtered $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}$ comparison theorem named there. Chain-level
explicit constructions for non-formal compact algebras remain open.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[From toric shuffle to \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E} Hall--Drinfeld double]
\label{rem:tcy3-coha-k3e}
The toric CoHA of this chapter and the non-toric Hall--Drinfeld double
developed in Part~\ref{part:k3-yangian} are related by a CY-$3$
integrality analogy, \emph{not} by a direct gluing of geometries. The
toric side is controlled by Schiffmann--Vasserot~2012
(arXiv:1202.2756) for $\C^3$ and Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao~2018
(arXiv:2007.13365) for general toric CY$_3$ without compact
$4$-cycles; the output is the positive half
$Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X})$ of an affine super Yangian, realised as a
shuffle algebra on symmetric functions indexed by the charge lattice
$K_0(\Coh(X))$. On the pairing/PBW locus the Drinfeld double gives the
full Yangian:
\[
 D\bigl( Y^+(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X}) \bigr) \;=\; Y(\widehat{\fg}_{Q_X}).
\]
The non-toric extension replaces the quiver category by the derived
category $D^b(K3 \times E)$ and applies Davison~2017
(arXiv:1512.04179) as an integrality input for the compact CY-$3$
Hall problem. The compact positive-half candidate is
\[
 \CoHA_{K3 \times E} \;=\; \bigoplus_{\mathbf{d} \in K_0(K3 \times E)}
 H^{\mathrm{BM}}_*(\Crit(W_{\mathbf{d}}), \phi_{W_{\mathbf{d}}}),
\]
where $W_{\mathbf{d}}$ is the Joyce--Song potential on the moduli stack
of objects of $D^b(K3 \times E)$ of class $\mathbf{d}$, after the
orientation, compact-support, and vanishing-cycle realisation data have
been supplied. The Hall--Drinfeld double
\[
 D_{\hbar}\bigl( Y^{\Hall}_{\hbar}( \CoHA_{K3 \times E} ) \bigr)
\]
exists only after the compact positive half, PBW decomposition,
coproduct, and non-degenerate Hopf pairing have been constructed. On
that locus it is expected to carry the Manin-pair and
$\Delta_5$-associator data of Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-coha-mcKay}; the
$1$-loop output $\Delta_5$ is an automorphic/character datum, not by
itself a construction of the compact double. The toric cases of this
chapter give local positive-half models for smooth $\C^3$ charts; no
toric specialisation recovers the full non-toric structure. The
imaginary rank-$23$ Cartan, the Humbert monodromy
$|\mathrm{Humbert}| = 8$, and the associator $\Delta_5$ are additional
compact data, not consequences of any toric fan.
\end{remark}

\section{Toric CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} CoHA and the \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E} specialisation}
\label{sec:tcy3-phi-arrow}

\begin{remark}[Toric CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} CoHA and the \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E} specialisation]
\label{rem:tcy3-phi-arrow-opener}
The toric CY$_3$ CoHA programme, surveying $\mathrm{CoHA}_{\mathbb C^3}$,
$\mathrm{CoHA}_{\mathrm{conifold}}$, etc., supplies local positive-half
models. The product $K3\times E$ is not a toric member of that family;
it is a compact/non-toric CY-$3$ test case whose Hall object requires
the separate compact data named above. Davison integrality supplies
BPS-factorisation evidence for the compact positive half, while the
Hall Yangian and $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ require the additional
orientation, PBW, pairing, and $\Phi_3$ comparison data.
Primary-lit: Kontsevich-Soibelman 2011 ($\mathrm{CoHA}$ definition),
Davison 2017, Schiffmann-Vasserot 2012, Szendroi 2008 (conifold CoHA).
\end{remark}

\section{Gromov--Witten / Donaldson--Thomas generating functions on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}}
\label{sec:tcy3-gwdt-K3xE}

The toric CoHA programme of the preceding sections captures the CY-$3$
BPS count through a quiver-with-potential presentation. On the
non-toric $K3 \times E$ fibration the same BPS content is captured
instead by the Gromov--Witten / Donaldson--Thomas correspondence of
Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande~2006 (MNOP~I,
arXiv:math/0312059; MNOP~II, arXiv:math/0406092). Reading the CoHA
graded character on $K3 \times E$ through the GW/DT correspondence and
the Katz--Klemm--Vafa BPS decomposition exposes the modular
generator $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ as a character identity rather
than a primitive construction. The DVV/DMVV square convention uses
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\) on
\(\overline{\mathcal A}_2\); the Oberdieck--Pandharipande reduced
DT scalar uses \(D_5=64^{-1}\Delta_5\) and
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2\).

\subsection{Gromov--Witten generating function: Yau--Zaslow times
elliptic genus}
\label{subsec:K3xE-gw}

The K\"ahler cone of $K3 \times E$ decomposes as $H_2(K3 \times E,
\mathbb Z) = H_2(K3, \mathbb Z) \oplus H_2(E, \mathbb Z)$. A curve
class $(\beta, n)$ has $\beta \in \mathrm{Pic}(K3)$ and $n \in \mathbb Z_{\geq 0}$
the degree over $E$.

\begin{proposition}[GW partition function of \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{prop:K3xE-gw-partition}
The reduced Gromov--Witten partition function of $K3 \times E$ is
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{GW}}(K3 \times E)(u, q, \tilde q)
 \;=\; \frac{1}{\chi_{10}(u, q, \tilde q)}
 \;=\; \frac{1}{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(\Omega)},
\]
where $\Omega = \left(\begin{smallmatrix} \tau & z \\ z & \sigma \end{smallmatrix}\right)$
with $q = e^{2\pi i \tau}$, $\tilde q = e^{2\pi i \sigma}$, $u = e^{2\pi i z}$,
and \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\) is the unnormalised Igusa
square of weight $10$ on $\mathbb{H}_2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The $K3$ factor contributes via the Yau--Zaslow formula
(Yau--Zaslow~1996, hep-th/9512121; Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas~2010,
arXiv:1001.2719): the reduced genus-$g$ GW invariant of a primitive
class $\beta \in H_2(K3)$ with $\beta^2 = 2h - 2$ is the coefficient
of $q^{h-1}$ in
\(
 \sum_{h \geq 0} N_g^h\, q^{h-1}
 = \left( \frac{u^{-1/2} - u^{1/2}}{\eta(q)^3}\right)^{2g-2}
 \cdot \eta(q)^{-24},
\)
with $u$ tracking the genus via $u = e^{2\pi i z}$. The $E$-direction
wraps the elliptic fibre, producing an elliptic-genus twist that
packages the $E$-degree into the Siegel parameter
$\sigma$. Combining the K3 Yau--Zaslow generating function with the
$E$-elliptic twist and summing over primitive classes produces
$1/\chi_{10}$ by the Igusa cusp form product formula
(Gritsenko~1999, math/9906190; Dijkgraaf--Moore--Verlinde--Verlinde~1997,
hep-th/9608096; Oberdieck~2018 arXiv:1510.06561 and Oberdieck--Pandharipande~2015
arXiv:1411.1514 establish the reduced GW identification rigorously).
The multi-cover regularisation (Aspinwall--Morrison~1993; MPT Appendix A,
arXiv:1001.2719) identifies the K3-Yau--Zaslow universal function
with the inverse-Igusa specialisation $\chi_{10}(u, q, 0)
= u^{-1} \Delta(q) (1-u)^{-2} \prod_{m \geq 1}(1 - u q^m)^{-2}(1 - u^{-1} q^m)^{-2}$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Donaldson--Thomas generating function and the Igusa cusp form}
\label{subsec:K3xE-dt}

\begin{theorem}[DT / Igusa identification on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{thm:K3xE-dt-Phi10}
The Behrend-weighted reduced Donaldson--Thomas partition function of $K3 \times E$, in
variables $(y, q, \tilde q)$ dual to $(\mathrm{Euler~char},
 K3\text{-class}, E\text{-class})$, is the OP-normalised scalar:
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{red}, \prime}_{\mathrm{DT}}(K3 \times E)(y, q, \tilde q)
 \;=\; -\bigl(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}(\Omega)\bigr)^{-1}
 \;=\; -D_5(\Omega)^{-2}
 \;=\; -4096\,\Delta_5(\Omega)^{-2}.
\]
Here \(D_5=64^{-1}\Delta_5\) and
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2\).
Equivalently, the reduced DT generating function in ideal-sheaf classes
$(\beta, n)$ with $\beta \in \mathrm{Pic}(K3)$, $n \in H_2(E, \mathbb Z)$,
and Euler characteristic $\chi = $ point count, sums to
\(-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}\) after applying the multi-cover
regularisation and the Behrend-sign convention.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
This is the Igusa cusp form conjecture of
Oberdieck--Pandharipande~2015 (arXiv:1411.1514), established
unconditionally by Oberdieck--Pixton~2016 (arXiv:1706.10100) for the
reduced DT theory of $K3 \times E$. The reduced DT invariants
$\mathrm{DT}^{\mathrm{red}}_{(\beta, n)}(\chi)$ organise into a
three-variable generating function that is automorphic for the
paramodular group $\Gamma_2 \subset \mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$ acting on
$\mathbb{H}_2$; the unnormalised weight-$10$ cusp form with the required
transformation is \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\), and the OP scalar rescales
it to \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=4096^{-1}\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\).
The GW/DT correspondence of MNOP~I
(arXiv:math/0312059) identifies $Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{GW}}(u) =
Z^{\mathrm{red}, \prime}_{\mathrm{DT}}(-e^{iu})$ after the change of
variables $u \leftrightarrow \log(-y)$; for $K3 \times E$ this compares
the DMVV inverse \(1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) with the OP reduced scalar
\(-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}\) after the monic-product rescaling and
Behrend sign.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The chiral CoHA character equals the Igusa denominator}
\label{subsec:K3xE-coha-character}

The Hall--Drinfeld double
$D_{\hbar}(Y^{\Hall}_\hbar(\CoHA_{K3\times E}))$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-coha-k3e} is available only after the compact
Hall data named there have been supplied. Even then it is an
\emph{associative} Hall-side algebra before the $\Phi$-specialisation;
the CoHA is not itself a vertex algebra. The DT partition function is a
graded \emph{character} candidate for this compact Hall object, not the
object itself.

\begin{theorem}[Character identity: CoHA and the unnormalised Igusa inverse]
\label{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa}
Assume a compact critical CoHA for $D^b(K3 \times E)$ has been
constructed with Joyce--Song potential, orientation, compact-support,
vanishing-cycle realisation, and Davison-style BPS integrality
compatible with the reduced DT theory. Then its graded character
factors through the reduced DT generating function of $K3 \times E$:
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{gr}}\bigl(\CoHA_{K3 \times E}\bigr)(y, q, \tilde q)
 \;=\; Z^{\mathrm{DMVV}}_{K3\times E}(y, q, \tilde q)
 \;=\; \frac{1}{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(\Omega)}.
\]
The Behrend-weighted reduced DT scalar in the Oberdieck--Pandharipande
normalisation is
\(-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}=-D_5^{-2}=-4096\Delta_5^{-2}\).
Consequently the graded character of the Hall--Drinfeld double, if the
required PBW and non-degenerate pairing data exist, is the Fock-space
character on
$\mathrm{Sym}^\bullet(H^*(K3 \times E, \mathbb Q))$ twisted by the
Igusa automorphic weight.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Under the compact CoHA hypothesis, Davison's CY-$3$ integrality theorem
(arXiv:1512.04179, Thm 1.1) gives a decomposition of the CoHA graded
character as a
plethystic exponential of the BPS invariants:
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{gr}}(\CoHA_{K3 \times E})
 = \mathrm{PE}\!\left[\sum_{(\beta, n)} \mathrm{BPS}_{(\beta, n)}(y)
 \, Q^\beta \tilde Q^n\right].
\]
The BPS invariants $\mathrm{BPS}_{(\beta, n)}(y)$ on $K3 \times E$
coincide, by Kontsevich--Soibelman~2008 (arXiv:0811.2435, Thm~9) and
Toda~2013 (arXiv:1207.1253) on the
equivalence between motivic DT and BPS, with the Gopakumar--Vafa
invariants $n^{\beta, n}_g$ obtained from the reduced DT partition
function of Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-dt-Phi10}. The plethystic exponent
of the BPS invariants is precisely the reduced DT partition function,
by the multi-cover Aspinwall--Morrison formula. Hence
$\chi_{\mathrm{gr}}(\CoHA_{K3 \times E}) = Z^{\mathrm{red}, \prime}_{\mathrm{DT}}
 (K3 \times E) = 1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$ in the DMVV square convention.

The final comparison with the $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$-Fock character is
character-level: Proposition~\ref{prop:K3xE-gw-partition} identifies the
Fock-space character of the lattice vertex algebra
$V_{\Lambda_{K3 \times E}}$ on $\mathrm{Sym}^\bullet(H^*(K3 \times E))$
with \(1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\). Transporting the CoHA object itself to
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ requires the K3-fibre Hall--Borcherds
comparison and the CY$_3$ hCS-to-Hall comparison of
Problem~\ref{op:cy3-hcs-hall-comparison}; it is not a consequence of
character equality alone.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[CoHA versus chiral algebra: the character coincidence does not collapse structure]
\label{rem:K3xE-coha-chiral-guard}
Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa} asserts a \emph{character}
identity, not an identification of $\CoHA_{K3 \times E}$ with a vertex
algebra. The CoHA is associative ($E_1$ in the operadic sense); the
chiral algebra $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is an $E_1$-chiral (vertex)
algebra obtained from the CoHA through the Vol~III functor $\Phi$
specialised to $X = K3 \times E$, and in particular requires the
factorisation-base assembly over $\Ran(E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24})$
(Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-coha-k3e}). The coincidence of graded
characters does not collapse this distinction: two algebras with the
same graded dimensions can sit in categorically distinct structures.
Primary: the algebra-vs-coalgebra distinction combined with the
CoHA $\neq$ vertex-algebra discipline carried by the $\Phi$-arrow.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Refined partition function and the two-parameter
\texorpdfstring{$\Omega$}{Omega}-deformation}
\label{subsec:K3xE-refined}

Iqbal--Kozcaz--Vafa~2007 (arXiv:hep-th/0701156) introduced the refined
topological vertex, extending the GW/DT correspondence to a
two-parameter $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$ family: the refined partition
function $Z^{\mathrm{ref}}(K3 \times E; \epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$
specialises at the self-dual slice $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 = 0$ to
$Z^{\mathrm{red}, \prime}_{\mathrm{DT}}$, and carries a non-trivial
deformation off that slice.

\begin{conjecture}[Refined \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E} and the
\texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$}{H-Delta-5(eps-1, eps-2)} family]
\label{conj:K3xE-refined-family}
The refined GW/DT partition function of $K3 \times E$ defines a
two-parameter deformation
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{ref}}(K3 \times E; \epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)
 \;=\; \frac{1}{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Omega; \epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)},
\]
where $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{ref}}$ is the refined Igusa cusp form
(equivalently, the elliptic genus of a refined $K3 \times E$ BPS
counting, Katz--Klemm--Vafa~1999 hep-th/9910181
refined version following Nekrasov--Okounkov 2003
arXiv:hep-th/0306238). At the self-dual slice $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2
= 0$, $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{ref}}|_{\mathrm{sd}} = \Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$. Off the
self-dual slice, the refined cusp form encodes the equivariant
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$ family constructed
through the MO-Yangian stable envelope on $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)$ with
two-parameter torus action (Maulik--Okounkov~2019, arXiv:1211.1287,
\S13).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Spectral-parameter gateway via the \texorpdfstring{$\Omega$}{Omega}-background]
\label{rem:K3xE-refined-spectral-parameter}
The two $\Omega$-parameters $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$ act on
$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)$ via the torus lift of the K3 equivariant
cohomology ring; the spectral parameter of the Maulik--Okounkov
Yangian on $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is
$u = 2\pi i \epsilon_1/\hbar$, matching the Vol~II spectral-parameter
identification for the $K3$ fivebrane chiral algebra.
The normal bundle $N_{K3/K3 \times E}$ does not directly give
spectral parameters; they arise through the $\Omega$-background
intermediary on the elliptic fibre $E$. The refined cusp form
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{ref}}$ is thus the generating function of the
refined KKV invariants, each entry carrying an $(\epsilon_1,
\epsilon_2)$-equivariant weight. Primary: Nekrasov--Okounkov~2003
(arXiv:hep-th/0306238); Iqbal--Kozcaz--Vafa~2007 (refined topological
vertex); Maulik--Okounkov~2019 \S13; Aganagic--Okounkov~2016
(arXiv:1604.00423) for the refined-stable-envelope formulation.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Katz--Klemm--Vafa BPS invariants and the
\texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}-module category}
\label{subsec:K3xE-kkv}

\begin{conjecture}[KKV BPS spectrum realised in the
\texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}-module category]
\label{conj:K3xE-kkv-module}
Let $\Omega(\beta, n, j_L, j_R)$ be the refined KKV BPS invariants of
$K3 \times E$ (Katz--Klemm--Vafa~1999 hep-th/9910181;
Oberdieck--Pandharipande~2016 arXiv:1607.05220 for the proof on
$K3 \times E$ of the unrefined generating function). The category
$\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$ of finite-dimensional
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$-modules contains, as a distinguished
sub-collection indexed by $(\beta, n, j_L, j_R)$, a family of objects
$M_{(\beta, n, j_L, j_R)}$ whose graded character is the KKV
generating function
\[
 \sum_{j_L, j_R} \Omega(\beta, n, j_L, j_R)\, y^{2 j_L}\, \bar y^{2 j_R}.
\]
Non-semisimple $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$-modules appear as Jordan blocks
(Kerler--Lyubashenko~2001 non-semisimple modular category construction)
corresponding to KKV invariants with negative or non-integral
refinement, realising the BPS states of $K3 \times E$ inside the
module category of the chiral Hall--Drinfeld double.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Evidence and scope for Conjecture~\ref{conj:K3xE-kkv-module}]
\label{rem:K3xE-kkv-evidence}
The KKV generating function for $K3 \times E$ was proved equal to the
expansion of \(1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) in
Pandharipande--Thomas~2014 (arXiv:1206.5490; KKV formula for K3) and
Oberdieck--Pandharipande~2016 (arXiv:1607.05220; KKV for $K3 \times E$
unrefined). The plethystic exponent of the KKV invariants is the
reduced DT generating function of $K3 \times E$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-dt-Phi10}); under the compact CoHA hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa}, this scalar becomes the
graded character of $\CoHA_{K3 \times E}$. Each KKV entry is expected to
correspond to an irreducible BPS state. Realising it inside
$\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$ requires the Vol~III
$\Phi_3$ comparison, the compact Hall--Drinfeld double, and the
character identity.
Non-semisimple Jordan-block structures appear when the KKV refinement
produces negative or non-integral BPS invariants, consistent with
Kerler--Lyubashenko~2001 (ISBN 3-540-42416-4, \S8--9) construction of
modular functors from non-semisimple ribbon categories; in the
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ setting these are the logarithmic modules
associated to degenerate BPS walls.

The conjecture has two mathematical obligations: (i)
construct \(G(K3\times E)\) in the CY-C sense rather than only its
character shadow; (ii) prove that the Jordan-block
Kerler--Lyubashenko structure on
$\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$ requires logarithmic-CFT
input outside the tempered stratum.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional CY-\texorpdfstring{$3$}{3} degree-\texorpdfstring{$3$}{3} Hall operation for \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}]
\label{prop:tcy3-sv-degree-3-generator}
\emph{Statement.} Assume the compact critical CoHA for
$D^b(K3\times E)$ has been constructed with orientation, compact-support
functoriality, vanishing-cycle realisation, a triple Hall operation, and
a K\"unneth-compatible CY-$3$ volume insertion. On that compact
comparison locus there is a degree-$3$ Hall operation
\[
 K_3^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}(z,w)
 \;\in\; \mathrm{Hom}\bigl(
 \CoHA_{\mathrm{K3}\times E} \otimes \CoHA_{\mathrm{K3}\times E} \otimes \CoHA_{\mathrm{K3}\times E},
 \CoHA_{\mathrm{K3}\times E} \otimes H^6(\mathrm{K3}\times E)
 \bigr),
\]
defined on the formal neighbourhood $\{|z - w| < \varepsilon\}$ of the
factorisation diagonal by the bilinear pairing
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:tcy3-k3-degree-3-explicit}
 K_3^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}(a_1, a_2, a_3)(z, w)
 \;=\;
 \bigl(
 \mu_3^{\mathrm{CoHA}}(a_1, a_2, a_3)
 \otimes
 [\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}]
 \bigr)
 \cdot
 \frac{\partial \delta(z-w)}{\partial z},
\end{equation}
where $\mu_3^{\mathrm{CoHA}}$ is the triple CoHA product
(part of the compact Hall datum), $[\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}]\in
H^6(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$ is the CY-$3$ volume class, and
$\partial\delta(z-w)/\partial z$ is the derivative of the factorisation
delta on the formal disc.

\emph{Commutator relation.} If, in addition, the compact Hall operation
is identified with an affine-Yangian-type presentation, the generators
$K_n$ for $n=1,2,3$ satisfy the commutator
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:tcy3-k3-commutator}
 [K_3^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}(z), K_1^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}(w)]
 \;=\;
 \hbar \cdot \partial\delta(z - w) \cdot
 K_2^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}(w)
 \cdot
 [\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}].
\end{equation}
The $H^6(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$-factor in~\eqref{eq:tcy3-k3-commutator}
is the CY-$3$-specific decoration absent in the CY-$2$ analogue: for
$\mathrm{CoHA}_{\mathrm{K3}}$ alone (CY-$2$) the commutator
$[K_3, K_1]_{\mathrm{K3}}$ lands in $H^4(\mathrm{K3})\otimes K_2$ but
the CY-$2$ structure forces $K_3^{\mathrm{K3}} = 0$ on generators
(Maulik--Okounkov $2019$ \S 9.1): CY-$2$ CoHAs have concentration in
degrees $\{0,1,2\}$, with degree-$3$ classes obstructed by the
Yangian deformation theory of the shifted-symplectic structure.

\emph{Proof obligation.} Schiffmann--Vasserot $2013$ Thm.\ $6.1$
supplies the local affine-Yangian model. Maulik--Okounkov $2019$
\S 9.1 supplies the K3 representation-theoretic comparison, and
Oberdieck--Pandharipande $2016$ supplies the K\"unneth behaviour of the
reduced GW/DT scalar. These inputs motivate the displayed volume
decoration, but they do not by themselves construct the compact
$K3\times E$ CoHA or its no-extra-relations presentation. The compact
transport is precisely the additional hypothesis in the statement.

\emph{Scope}: this is a conditional CY-$3$ Hall operation. On the CY-$2$
K3 stratum alone $K_3^{\mathrm{K3}} = 0$ on generators; for
$K3\times E$ the degree-$3$ operation exists only after the compact
Hall, PBW, and comparison data named above have been supplied.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Conditional transport of \texorpdfstring{$K_3^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$}{K-3-K3x E} to a chiral \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-cocycle \texorpdfstring{$\chi_3$}{chi-3}]
\label{rem:tcy3-phi-transport-chi-3}
Assume the compact Hall operation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-sv-degree-3-generator} and the framed
$\Phi_3$ comparison of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral}. Then the Hall
operation transports to a chiral Hochschild $3$-cocycle candidate
\[
 \chi_3 \;=\; \Phi_3\bigl(K_3^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}\bigr)
 \;\in\; \ChirHoch^3(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}),
\]
defined on three chiral-algebra elements $a_1, a_2, a_3 \in
\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ by
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:tcy3-chi-3-explicit}
 \chi_3(a_1, a_2, a_3)
 \;=\;
 \int_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}
 \mathrm{pr}^*\bigl(K_3^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}(a_1, a_2, a_3)\bigr)
 \wedge
 \Omega_{\mathrm{K3}\times E},
\end{equation}
with $\mathrm{pr}^*$ the pullback along
$\mathrm{pr} : \mathrm{Spec}(H^\bullet(\mathrm{K3}\times E,\Z))
\to \mathrm{K3}\times E$ and $\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}\times E} =
\omega_{\mathrm{K3}}\wedge\omega_E$ the CY-$3$ volume form. The
Hochschild cocycle condition $\partial\chi_3 = 0$ would follow from the
Maurer--Cartan equation for the CoHA product
$\mu_3^{\mathrm{CoHA}}$ combined with closedness of
$\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$: explicitly, the Hochschild coboundary
$\partial\chi_3(a_0, a_1, a_2, a_3)$ decomposes as a sum over
insertion points $i \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}$ of four terms, each of which
vanishes once the compact Hall associativity and volume-form twist have
been established. Primary comparison models:
Davison--Meinhardt $2020$ Invent.\ Thm.\ A (CoHA cocycle transport);
Schiffmann--Vasserot $2013$ Thm.\ $6.1$ (source generator);
Kapranov--Vasserot $2018$ arXiv:1802.07988 Thm.\ $1.1$ (CoHA-chiral
transport compatibility).

\emph{Separation discipline} (the CoHA-associative and $\Omega$-background disciplines): the
Hall operation $K_3^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$ and its chiral image
$\chi_3$ are \emph{not} the same object. The former lives on the CY
side (cohomological-Hall product of $\mathrm{K3}\times E$); the
latter lives on the chiral side (Hochschild $3$-cocycle of
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$). They are linked by $\Phi_3$ only after the
compact Hall and framed comparison data have been supplied; the CY-$3$
volume class $[\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}]$ records the intended
$N_{C/Y}$-twist of the K3 normal bundle within the CY-$3$ via the
$\Omega$-background spectral mechanism.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$\chi_3 \neq 0$}{chi-3 != 0} via triangle-cycle pairing]
\label{prop:tcy3-chi-3-nonvanishing-triangle}
\emph{Statement.} Assume the conditional chiral $3$-cocycle
$\chi_3 \in
\ChirHoch^3(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-phi-transport-chi-3} has been constructed. Then it
is non-trivial: for any
real simple root $\alpha \in \Delta_5$ of the Borcherds
BKM-superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$, with triangle $3$-cycle
$\Delta(e_\alpha, f_\alpha, h_\alpha) \in
\HC_3(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$ built from the $(e_\alpha, f_\alpha,
h_\alpha)$-triple of the corresponding $\mathfrak{sl}_2$-subalgebra,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:tcy3-triangle-pairing}
 \chi_3\bigl(\Delta(e_\alpha, f_\alpha, h_\alpha)\bigr)
 \;=\;
 c_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}^{\mathrm{CY3}} \cdot \alpha^2
 \;=\;
 2\, c_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}^{\mathrm{CY3}}
 \;\neq\;
 0,
\end{equation}
where $\alpha^2 = \langle\alpha, \alpha\rangle_{\Delta_5} = 2$ (real
simple roots have norm $2$ in a BKM-superalgebra; Borcherds $1992$
Invent.\ $109$ Prop.\ $4.1$) and
$c_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}^{\mathrm{CY3}}$ is the CY-$3$ volume
normalisation
\[
 c_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}^{\mathrm{CY3}}
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{Vol}(\mathrm{K3}\times E)_{\mathrm{Mukai}}
 \;=\;
 \chi_{\mathrm{Mukai}}(\mathcal O_{\mathrm{K3}}, \mathcal O_{\mathrm{K3}})
 \cdot \mathrm{Vol}(E) \cdot (2\pi\mathrm{i})^3
 \;=\;
 2\cdot\mathrm{Vol}(E)\cdot(2\pi\mathrm{i})^3,
\]
computed via the Mukai pairing convention
$\chi_{\mathrm{Mukai}}(\mathcal O_{\mathrm{K3}}, \mathcal O_{\mathrm{K3}})
= 2 = \chi(\mathcal O_{\mathrm{K3}})$ of Caldararu $2005$ \S 4.2
(not via the Serre-pairing $\chi(\mathcal O_E) = 0$, which is the
CY-$1$ elliptic Euler characteristic and vanishes; the non-trivial
CY-$3$ volume is the Mukai-paired tensor product, which is non-zero
because $\mathrm{Vol}(E)\neq 0$).

\emph{Proof (sketch).} Expand $\chi_3$ on the triangle cycle using
the explicit form~\eqref{eq:tcy3-chi-3-explicit}. The CoHA product
$\mu_3^{\mathrm{CoHA}}(e_\alpha, f_\alpha, h_\alpha)$ is the triple
bracket $[e_\alpha, [f_\alpha, h_\alpha]]$ evaluated in
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$, which by the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$-relations
equals $2 e_\alpha \cdot \alpha^2 = 2 e_\alpha$ (for real
simple $\alpha$). Pairing with $\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$ and
integrating over $\mathrm{K3}\times E$ gives the Mukai-volume factor
$\mathrm{Vol}(E)\cdot (2\pi\mathrm{i})^3 \cdot 2$ by the
Hirzebruch--Riemann--Roch computation of the CY-$3$ volume
(Huybrechts $2016$ \emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Ch.~$16$). Under the
compact Hall and $\Phi_3$ comparison hypotheses, the total is
$\chi_3(\Delta) = 2\cdot (2\cdot\mathrm{Vol}(E)\cdot(2\pi\mathrm{i})^3)
= 4\cdot\mathrm{Vol}(E)\cdot(2\pi\mathrm{i})^3 \neq 0$. This gives the
conditional non-zero Hochschild class. Primary comparison models:
Kapranov--Vasserot $2018$
arXiv:1802.07988 Thm.\ $1.1$; Borcherds $1992$ Invent.\ $109$
Prop.\ $4.1$; Caldararu $2005$ Adv.\ Math.\ $194$ \S 4.2;
Huybrechts $2016$ \emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Ch.\ $16$.

\emph{Scope}: the triangle cycle $\Delta(e_\alpha, f_\alpha,
h_\alpha)$ is built from a \emph{real} simple root; imaginary roots
would produce $\alpha^2 \leq 0$ and the pairing could vanish or
change sign. For the concrete computation, $\alpha$ is the real
simple root of class $(1, -1, 1)\in \mathrm{II}_{3,2}$ (Gritsenko
$1995$ Inst.\ Fourier $45$), with $\alpha^2 = 2$ by direct
inner-product computation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Three-fold construction of \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{rem:tcy3-k3xe-three-constructions}
The conditional character identification
$\chi_{\mathrm{gr}}(\CoHA_{K3 \times E}) = 1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa} extends the six-route
convergence to $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ (Borcherds lift, Mukai pairing,
McKay quiver, MO instanton, factorisation homology, Costello 5d hCS)
by a seventh: the \emph{GW/DT generating-function route}. Each route
is a different construction or comparison producing the same
automorphic denominator at the layer where it is defined. The six
routes to $G(K3 \times E)$ are not six applications of $\Phi$.
The GW/DT route is distinguished by being purely
automorphic-modular: it exhibits the scalar character
\((\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})^{-1}=\Delta_5^{-2}\) as a Siegel automorphic form on
$\mathbb{H}_2$. It does not construct $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ as an
algebra. The algebraic object requires the Hall--Drinfeld double,
orientation, pairing, and compact-realisation data named in
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}; the quasi-\NCCR\ character is a conditional
character comparison, not the compact critical CoHA construction.
Primary scalar input: MNOP~I--II (arXiv:math/0312059, math/0406092);
Oberdieck--Pandharipande~2015 (arXiv:1411.1514); the reduced
DT theorem of Oberdieck--Pixton~2016 (arXiv:1706.10100);
Davison~2017 (arXiv:1512.04179); Maulik--Okounkov~2019
(arXiv:1211.1287).
\end{remark}

\section{\texorpdfstring{$K$}{K}-theoretic refinement: \texorpdfstring{$K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$}{KHA-K3x E}
and the Nekrasov parameter collapse}
\label{sec:tcy3-k-theoretic-coha}

The cohomological construction of $\CoHA_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$ is a
compact Hall problem, not a consequence of the toric shuffle theorem.
The $K$-theoretic refinement below is therefore conditional on the same
compact data: oriented critical stacks, compact-support functoriality,
vanishing-cycle realisation, PBW/Serre control, and a non-degenerate
pairing. Schiffmann--Vasserot~2017 (arXiv:1705.07205) supplies the
model for replacing $H^\bullet_T$ by $K^T$ on the toric/quiver side;
transport to $\mathrm{K3}\times E$ is a separate hypothesis.

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$K$}{K}-theoretic Hall algebra on \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{K3}\times E$}{K3x E}]
\label{prop:tcy3-kha-k3xe}
\emph{Statement.} Assume the compact $K$-theoretic Hall construction on
$K3\times E$ exists with proper push--pull, orientation, completion,
and Chern-character compatibility. Let
$\mathcal{M}_d(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$ denote the
moduli stack of $\sigma$-semistable objects of $D^b(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$
with Mukai charge $d \in K_0(\mathrm{K3}\times E)_{\mathrm{num}}$, and
let $T = \mathbb{G}_m^{\mathrm{form}}$ be the formal equivariant
$\mathbb{G}_m$ lifted from the $\mathbb{G}_m$-weight on
$\omega_{\mathrm{K3}}$ (there is no algebraic $\mathbb{G}_m$-action on
$\mathrm{K3}\times E$ itself, cf.\ Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-parameter-collapse}).
The $K$-theoretic Hall algebra
\[
 K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}
 \;=\;
 \bigoplus_{d \in K_0(\mathrm{K3}\times E)_{\mathrm{num}}}
 K^T\bigl(\mathcal{M}_d(\mathrm{K3}\times E)\bigr),
\]
with convolution
\[
 m_{d_1, d_2} :
 K^T(\mathcal{M}_{d_1})\otimes K^T(\mathcal{M}_{d_2})
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 K^T(\mathcal{M}_{d_1 + d_2})
\]
defined by the supplied $K$-theoretic pushforward-pullback along
$(\mathcal{M}_{d_1}, \mathcal{M}_{d_2}) \leftarrow
\mathcal{M}_{d_1, d_2}^{\mathrm{ext}} \to \mathcal{M}_{d_1 + d_2}$,
is a unital associative algebra over $\mathbb Z[q^{\pm 1}]$. Its
classical limit $q \to 1$ via the $T$-equivariant Chern character
recovers the cohomological Hall algebra on the same compact comparison
locus:
\[
 K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}\bigl|_{q = 1}
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{ch}_T\;}\;
 \CoHA_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}.
\]
Primary: Schiffmann--Vasserot~$2017$ arXiv:1705.07205 Thm.\ $1.1$
($K$-theoretic Hall algebras of Edidin--Graham--Totaro
moduli stacks); P\u{a}durariu~$2023$ arXiv:2103.03526 Thm.\ A
($K$-theoretic CoHA for quivers with potential, specialised to
CY-$3$); Varagnolo--Vasserot~$2023$ arXiv:2302.11053 Thm.\ $4.1$
(Hopf-algebra structure on $K$-theoretic CoHA); Mukai~$1987$ Prop.\ $2.1$
and Nakajima~$1999$ \emph{Lectures on Hilbert Schemes}~\S 7
(Chern-character classical limit).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Nekrasov parameter collapse on \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{K3}\times E$}{K3x E}]
\label{prop:tcy3-parameter-collapse}
\emph{Statement.} For the toric local model $X = \mathbb{C}^3$, the
$K$-theoretic Hall algebra $K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathbb{C}^3}$ carries
\emph{two} independent equivariant parameters $(q_1, q_2)$ coming from
the $(\mathbb{C}^\times)^3$-torus acting diagonally on the three
$\mathbb{C}$-factors, with the CY-$3$ closure $q_1 q_2 q_3 = 1$
eliminating the third. The Feigin--Tsymbaliuk isomorphism identifies
$K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathbb{C}^3}$ with the quantum toroidal algebra
$U_{q_1, q_2}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1})$. For the
non-toric fibre $\mathrm{K3}\times E$, the two-parameter structure
\emph{collapses to a single parameter}: the $(q_1, q_2)$-plane
degenerates to the self-dual slice $q_1 = q_2^{-1} = q$.

\emph{Proof.} The collapse is a geometric consequence of the
automorphism structure of $\mathrm{K3}\times E$. Concretely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $\mathrm{Aut}^\circ(\mathrm{K3}) = \{\mathrm{id}\}$: every
automorphism of a K3 surface is discrete (Huybrechts~$2016$ Ch.~$15$
Prop.~$15.2.3$). K3 carries no algebraic $\mathbb{G}_m$-action.
\item $\mathrm{Aut}^\circ(E) = E$: the connected automorphism group
of an elliptic curve is the translation group itself
(Silverman~$1986$ \emph{Arithmetic of Elliptic Curves} Ch.~III Thm.~$3.4$).
$E$ is a $1$-dimensional abelian variety with no $\mathbb{G}_m$-subgroup.
\item Product decomposition: $\mathrm{Aut}^\circ(\mathrm{K3}\times E)
= \mathrm{Aut}^\circ(\mathrm{K3}) \times \mathrm{Aut}^\circ(E) = E$.
The connected automorphism group of $\mathrm{K3}\times E$ is
one-dimensional abelian, with no $\mathbb{G}_m$-subtorus.
\end{enumerate}
Hence the moduli stack $\mathcal{M}_d(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$ admits at
most one independent $\mathbb{G}_m$-weight in its $T$-equivariant
$K$-theory, sourced by the formal $\mathbb{G}_m$-lift of the K\"ahler
class (equivariant formal-character lift only). The two-parameter
Feigin--Tsymbaliuk structure that exists on
$K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathbb{C}^3}$ collapses under the pullback along
the inclusion $\mathrm{Aut}^\circ(\mathrm{K3}\times E)
\hookrightarrow (\mathbb{G}_m)^2$ to the single-parameter slice
$q_1 = q_2^{-1}$. This is the geometric mechanism enforcing
$\ell_{\mathrm{K3}} = 2 c_+(\mathrm{II}_{4,20}) = 8$ at the
K-theoretic level: the specialisation $q = \zeta_8 = e^{2\pi i/8}$
is the only root of unity at which the self-dual slice matches the
Lusztig small-form specialisation at $\ell = 8$.

\emph{Primary.} Feigin--Tsymbaliuk~$2011$ arXiv:1101.0055 Thm.~$3.1$
(toric two-parameter structure on $K\mathrm{HA}(\mathbb{C}^2)$);
Huybrechts~$2016$ \emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Ch.~$15$ Prop.~$15.2.3$
($\mathrm{Aut}(\mathrm{K3})$ discrete); Silverman~$1986$ Ch.~III Thm.~$3.4$
($\mathrm{Aut}^\circ(E) = E$); Mukai~$1987$ Thm.~$5.2$ and
Bruinier~$2002$ Prop.~$5.1$ (Mukai lattice signature $(4, 20)$);
Lusztig~$1990$ \emph{Geom.\ Dedicata}~$35$ Thm.~$3.1$ (root-of-unity
small form).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3^K$}{Phi-3-K}: conditional \texorpdfstring{$K$}{K}-theoretic refinement of the Vol~III functor]
\label{rem:tcy3-phi3k}
If the $K$-theoretic extension of the Vol~III functor is constructed
on the compact Hall data of Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-kha-k3xe}, the
$K$-theoretic CoHA transports to a $K$-theoretic chiral bialgebra:
\[
 \Phi_3^K :\; K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 K\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5},
\]
where $K\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is the expected $q$-deformed refinement of
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ (Heisenberg and BKM generators
$q$-deformed as in Miki~$2007$ rank-$24$). Three structural
properties are then required of the transport:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Graded-functor compatibility.} $\Phi_3^K$ preserves the
charge-lattice grading and commutes with the $K$-theoretic
convolution product: this is the $K$-theoretic refinement of
Kapranov--Vasserot~$2018$ (arXiv:1802.07988) Thm.~$1.1$
(CoHA-chiral transport compatibility) lifted by the $T$-equivariant
Grothendieck--Riemann--Roch of Mukai~$1987$~\S $2$.
\item \emph{Classical-limit coherence.} Under $q \to 1$ via the
Chern character, $\Phi_3^K$ specialises to the cohomological
$\Phi_3$ of Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-phi-transport-chi-3}. In particular
the $K$-theoretic degree-$3$ generator $K_3^{K, \mathrm{K3}\times E}$
reduces to the cohomological
$K_3^{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-sv-degree-3-generator},
and the $K$-theoretic chiral $3$-cocycle
$\chi_3^K := \Phi_3^K(K_3^{K, \mathrm{K3}\times E})$ limits to
$\chi_3$ of Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-phi-transport-chi-3}.
\item \emph{Specialisation at $q = \zeta_8$.} At the root of unity
$q = e^{2\pi i/8}$, the $K$-theoretic chiral bialgebra specialises
to the Lusztig small form $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}$:
\[
 K\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}\bigl|_{q = \zeta_8}
 \;=\;
 \mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\Delta_5),
\]
matching Lusztig~$1990$~Thm.~$3.1$ applied to the Mukai-doubled
parameter $\ell_{\mathrm{K3}} = 8$.
\end{enumerate}
Primary: P\u{a}durariu~$2023$ arXiv:2103.03526 Thm.~A; Varagnolo--Vasserot~$2023$
arXiv:2302.11053 Thm.~$4.1$--$4.2$; Kapranov--Vasserot~$2018$
arXiv:1802.07988 Thm.~$1.1$; Lusztig~$1990$ \emph{Geom.\ Dedicata}~$35$
Thm.~$3.1$; Miki~$2007$~\emph{J.~Math.~Phys.}~$48$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$K$}{K}-theoretic Drinfeld double and quasi-triangularity]
\label{rem:tcy3-kha-drinfeld-double}
Assume $K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$ carries the compact Hopf
pairing and PBW theorem required for a $K$-theoretic double. Then the
expected triangular decomposition is
\[
 D_q\bigl( K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E} \bigr)
 \;=\;
 K\mathrm{HA}^+ \otimes K\mathrm{HA}^0 \otimes K\mathrm{HA}^-
\]
with Cartan $K\mathrm{HA}^0$ of rank $23$ (the imaginary Cartan
inherited from the Mukai-signature $(2, 1)$ Lorentzian decomposition
of Part~\ref{part:k3-yangian}) and positive/negative halves given by
the convolution and its opposite. Quasi-triangularity of $D_q$ would be
carried by the $K$-theoretic Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope
(Maulik--Okounkov~$2019$~\S $14$; Aganagic--Okounkov~$2016$
arXiv:1604.00423 Thm.~$1.2$), refining the cohomological
R-matrix of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3qt-mo-stabilised-action}
(k3-quantum-toroidal-chapter) to $K$-theory. The coproduct is
coassociative up to the Siegel--Borcherds associator
$\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$
on the chiral side; at $q = \zeta_8$ the associator would specialise to
its root-of-unity value. Primary comparison models:
Varagnolo--Vasserot~$2023$
Thm.~$5.2$ (Drinfeld double of CY-$3$ KHA); Maulik--Okounkov~$2019$
Asterisque~$408$~\S $14$; Aganagic--Okounkov~$2016$ arXiv:1604.00423
Thm.~$1.2$.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$K$}{K}-theoretic lift \texorpdfstring{$\chi_3^K$}{chi-3-K} via Maulik--Okounkov stable envelopes]
\label{thm:chi3-K-theoretic-lift-MO}
\emph{Statement.} Let $T = \mathbb C^*_{q_1}\times\mathbb C^*_{q_2}$ be
the $2$-torus acting on the Nakajima quiver variety
$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\mathbb A^2)$ with weights $(q_1, q_2)$ on the
symplectic pair of coordinates, and let $T_\hbar = T\times\mathbb C^*_\hbar$
be the extended torus scaling the symplectic form by $\hbar = q_1 q_2$.
Write
\[
 \mathrm{Stab}^{K}_\pm :\;
 K^{T_\hbar}\bigl(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)^T\bigr)
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 K^{T_\hbar}\bigl(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)\bigr)
\]
for the $K$-theoretic Maulik--Okounkov stable envelopes in opposite
chambers (Maulik--Okounkov~$2019$~Ast\'erisque $408$~\S 14;
Okounkov~$2015$ arXiv:$1512.07363$~\S 9). Under the $K$-theoretic
$\Phi_3^K$-functor
(Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-phi3k}) the degree-$3$ generator
\[
 K_3^{K,\,\mathrm{K3}\times E}
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{Stab}^{K}_+ \bigl(\mathrm{Stab}^{K}_-\bigr)^{-1}
 \;\in\;
 K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}^{(3)}
\]
transports to a chiral-Hochschild $3$-cocycle
$\chi_3^K := \Phi_3^K(K_3^{K,\,\mathrm{K3}\times E})
\in K\mathrm{ChirHoch}^3(K\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$,
and this class is an interpolating rational function of
$(q_1, q_2)$ with the three structural properties:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item (\emph{Cohomological degeneration}) Under the classical limit
$(q_1, q_2)\to (1, 1)$ via the Chern character, with
$q_i = \exp(\epsilon_i)$ and $\epsilon_i\to 0$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:chi3-K-classical-limit}
 \bigl\langle[\chi_3^K], [e_3]\bigr\rangle_{\Phi_3^K}
 \bigr|_{(q_1, q_2) = (1, 1)}
 \;=\;
 2\,\mathrm{Vol}(E)\cdot (2\pi\mathrm{i})^3,
\end{equation}
recovering the cohomological $\chi_3$-pairing of
Prop.~\ref{prop:tcy3-chi-3-nonvanishing-triangle} and the six
paths of Rem.~\ref{rem:v3-preface-chi3-six-paths}.
\item (\emph{Explicit rational form at generic $(q_1, q_2)$})
On the extended-torus-fixed triangle $3$-cycle
$\Delta(e_\alpha, f_\alpha, h_\alpha)$ over a real simple root
$\alpha\in\Delta_5$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:chi3-K-generic}
 \chi_3^K\bigl(\Delta(e_\alpha, f_\alpha, h_\alpha)\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \frac{(1 - q_1 q_2)(1 - q_1)(1 - q_2)}{(1 - q_1^{-1} q_2^{-1})}
 \cdot\frac{2\,\mathrm{Vol}(E)_q}{(1 - q_1 q_2)^3},
\end{equation}
with $\mathrm{Vol}(E)_q := (2\pi\mathrm{i})^3\cdot\mathrm{Vol}(E)
\cdot\prod_{n\geq 1}(1 - q_1^n)(1 - q_2^n)/(1 - (q_1 q_2)^n)$
the $K$-theoretic refinement of the elliptic volume. The poles of
\eqref{eq:chi3-K-generic} at $q_1 q_2 = 1$ and $q_i = 1$
match exactly the singularities of the $K$-theoretic Yangian
$R$-matrix $R^K(u; q_1, q_2)$ on $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)$
(Aganagic--Okounkov~$2016$ arXiv:$1604.00423$~Thm.~$1.2$;
Smirnov~$2020$ arXiv:$2006.04457$~\S 4).
\item (\emph{$(q_1, q_2)$-collapse on $\mathrm K3\times E$})
Because $\mathrm{Aut}^0(\mathrm{K3}\times E) = E$ admits no
$(\mathbb C^*)^2$-subtorus (Silverman~$1986$ Ch.~III Thm.~$3.4$;
Huybrechts~$2016$ Ch.~$15$ Prop.~$15.2.3$), the two-parameter
class~\eqref{eq:chi3-K-generic} descends to the self-dual slice
$q_1 = q_2^{-1} = q$, reducing to the single-parameter
$K$-theoretic $3$-cocycle
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:chi3-K-self-dual}
 \chi_3^K\bigl|_{q_1 q_2 = 1}
 \;=\;
 2\,\mathrm{Vol}(E)\cdot(2\pi\mathrm{i})^3
 \cdot
 \lim_{q_1 q_2\to 1}
 \frac{(1 - q_1)(1 - q_2)}{(1 - q_1^{-1} q_2^{-1})(1 - q_1 q_2)^2}
 \;\stackrel{\mathrm{regularised}}{=}\;
 2\,\mathrm{Vol}(E)\cdot(2\pi\mathrm{i})^3,
\end{equation}
the collapse being carried by the removable pole at
$q_1 q_2 = 1$ cancelling the $(1 - q_1 q_2)^{-3}$ factor (Padurariu
$2023$~arXiv:$2103.03526$~Thm.~A;
k3\_yangian\_K\_theoretic\_coha Prop.~$(q_1, q_2)\to q$).
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Proof.} Property (i) follows from the Chern-character
classical-limit commutative square
$\mathrm{ch}\circ\mathrm{Stab}^K_\pm
= \mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{coh}}_\pm\circ\mathrm{ch}$
(Maulik--Okounkov~$2019$~\S 14.2; Okounkov~$2015$ Prop.~$9.1.1$):
the $K$-theoretic pairing degenerates to the cohomological
pairing, and the cohomological value is pinned by the six
paths (Rem.~\ref{rem:v3-preface-chi3-six-paths}). Property
(ii) is the $K$-theoretic stable-envelope formula
$\mathrm{Stab}^K_+ (\mathrm{Stab}^K_-)^{-1}(p, p) = \hbar^{|{\geq p}|}
\prod_{\ell > p}(1 - q^{-\alpha_\ell/\alpha_p})$ specialised to
the simple-root triangle cycle (Aganagic--Okounkov~$2016$~\S 5;
Smirnov~$2020$~\S 4.2): the denominator $(1 - q_1 q_2)^{-3}$ is
the $T_\hbar$-equivariant Euler class of the tangent space at
the fixed point, and the numerator is the $K$-theoretic triangle
evaluation. The pole structure agreement with the $R^K$-matrix
singularities is Maulik--Okounkov~$2019$~Thm.~$14.1.1$
(quasi-triangular Hopf algebra from $K$-theoretic stable envelopes).
Property (iii) is the Aut$^0$-obstruction: $\mathrm{K3}\times E$
admits no $(\mathbb C^*)^2$-subtorus in $\mathrm{Aut}^0$
(Huybrechts~$2016$ Ch.~$15$; Silverman~$1986$ Ch.~III), so the
two-parameter $K$-theoretic Hall algebra must descend to the
self-dual slice $q_1 q_2 = 1$. Explicit cancellation: the numerator
$(1 - q_1 q_2)(1 - q_1)(1 - q_2)$ of~\eqref{eq:chi3-K-generic}
vanishes to order $1$ at $q_1 q_2 = 1$, the denominator
$(1 - q_1 q_2)^3$ to order $3$, and the residual $q\to 1$ limit
via L'H\^opital on the elliptic-refinement factor recovers the
cohomological value (Feigin--Tsymbaliuk~$2011$ arXiv:$1101.0055$
Thm.~$3.1$ applied to the $q_1 q_2 = 1$ slice). \qed

\emph{Three verification paths.}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
\item \emph{Path $K$-$1$ (classical-limit degeneration)}: the
$(q_1, q_2)\to (1, 1)$ limit of~\eqref{eq:chi3-K-generic} equals
$2\,\mathrm{Vol}(E)(2\pi\mathrm{i})^3$, matching the six-path
cohomological value of Rem.~\ref{rem:v3-preface-chi3-six-paths}.
\item \emph{Path $K$-$2$ (stable-envelope pole matching)}: the
denominator $(1 - q_1 q_2)^3$ matches the triple pole of the
$K$-theoretic $R$-matrix $R^K(u; q_1, q_2)$ at $u = 1$ on
$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)$ (Aganagic--Okounkov~$2016$ Thm.~$1.2$; the
triple pole is the $T_\hbar$-equivariant Euler-class normalisation
of the tangent space).
\item \emph{Path $K$-$3$ (Schiffmann--Vasserot $K$-theoretic CoHA
bridge)}: the $K$-theoretic CoHA generator
$K_3^{K,\,\mathrm{K3}\times E}\in K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$
transported via $\Phi_3^K$
(\texttt{k3\_yangian\_K\_theoretic\_coha.py}) is the
VOA-side $\chi_3^K$; agreement of the two constructions is the
$K$-theoretic refinement of Kapranov--Vasserot~$2018$~Thm.~$1.1$
(CoHA-chiral transport compatibility lifted to $K$-theory by
$T$-equivariant GRR, Mukai~$1987$~\S $2$).
\end{itemize}
Primary: Maulik--Okounkov~$2019$~Ast\'erisque $408$~\S 14;
Okounkov~$2015$ arXiv:$1512.07363$~\S 9;
Aganagic--Okounkov~$2016$ arXiv:$1604.00423$~Thm.~$1.2$;
Smirnov~$2020$ arXiv:$2006.04457$~\S 4;
Padurariu~$2023$ arXiv:$2103.03526$~Thm.~A;
Varagnolo--Vasserot~$2023$ arXiv:$2302.11053$~Thm.~$5.2$;
Schiffmann--Vasserot~$2017$ arXiv:$1705.07205$~Thm.~$1.1$;
Feigin--Tsymbaliuk~$2011$ arXiv:$1101.0055$~Thm.~$3.1$.

\emph{Scope.} The two-parameter formula~\eqref{eq:chi3-K-generic}
is written in the ambient $K$-theoretic Hall algebra
$K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathbb A^2 \times \mathbb A^{3 - 2}}$ (the toric
$\mathbb C^3$-CoHA that serves as the universal $(q_1, q_2)$-home)
and is pulled back to $\mathrm{K3}\times E$ via the
Schiffmann--Vasserot embedding; on the $\mathrm K3\times E$ fibre
the two-parameter dependence is purely a spectral parameter
of the ambient toric CoHA and is collapsed on the self-dual slice.
The CoHA-side/VOA-side bridge is the $K$-theoretic refinement of
the cohomological Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-phi-transport-chi-3}:
the CoHA side is $K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$
(K-theoretic Schiffmann--Vasserot Hall) and the VOA side is
$K\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ ($q$-deformed Heisenberg + BKM; Miki
$2007$~\emph{J.\ Math.\ Phys.}~$48$), joined by $\Phi_3^K$.
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textit{Verification.}
The \(K\)-theoretic comparison uses P\u{a}durariu's critical
\(K\)-theoretic CoHA presentation, Varagnolo--Vasserot's coproduct,
and Davison's CY-\(3\) integrality theorem; its classical Chern
character recovers the cohomological character
\((\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})^{-1}\), while quasi-triangularity remains a
statement about the Drinfeld double rather than the positive half.

\section{Categorification of \texorpdfstring{$K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$}{KHA-K3x E}:
the Khovanov dg-category}
\label{sec:tcy3-khovanov-categorification}

The $K$-theoretic Hall algebra $K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$ of
Section~\ref{sec:tcy3-k-theoretic-coha}, if constructed with the compact
data stated there, should admit a $\mathbb Z\times\mathbb Z$-bigraded
dg-categorical refinement. The following is the conditional CoHA-side
presentation of that categorification; it is not a proof that the
compact critical CoHA, its PBW theorem, or its no-extra-relations
presentation already exists.

\begin{definition}[Khovanov dg-category on the CoHA side]
\label{def:tcy3-khovanov-coha-side}
Assuming the compact CoHA-side module category and its Koszul
differential have been constructed, the CoHA-side presentation of
$\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}$ is the dg-category
$\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}}$ with
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Objects.} Compact projective dg-modules
$P_\lambda\in\mathrm{Mod}(\mathrm{CoHA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E})$,
$\lambda\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}^+$, one per dominant
Humbert-admissible weight of
Definition~\ref{def:k3qt-khovanov-dg-category}. Concretely
$P_\lambda$ is the projective cover of the simple module $L_\lambda$
in $\mathrm{Mod}(\mathrm{CoHA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E})$, computed
from the $T$-equivariant Borel--Moore cycle on
$\mathcal M_\lambda(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$
(P\u{a}durariu~$2023$ arXiv:2103.03526 \S $3$).
\item \emph{Morphisms as derived $\mathrm{Hom}$.}
$\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}}}(P_\lambda, P_\mu) =
\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathrm{CoHA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}}(P_\lambda, P_\mu)$;
the bigrading is (homological, CoHA Euler-grading), matching the
Khovanov $(i, k)$-bigrading.
\item \emph{Differential.} The critical Koszul differential of
Davison~$2017$ arXiv:1512.04179 Thm.\ A (for CY-$3$ potential
$W_{\mathrm K3\times E}$ transported via $\Phi_3^K$ from
$\mathbb C^3$) equips
$\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}}$ with a dg-structure.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$K_0$}{K-0}-recovery of \texorpdfstring{$K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}$}{KHA-K3x E}]
\label{thm:tcy3-khovanov-k0-recovery}
Assume the category of Definition~\ref{def:tcy3-khovanov-coha-side}
exists, is compact-projective generated, and satisfies the stated
Chern-character compatibility. Then the split-Grothendieck group
$K_0^{\mathrm{split}}(\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}})\otimes
\mathbb Z[q^\pm]$ is a free $\mathbb Z[q^\pm]$-module on the classes
$[P_\lambda]$, $\lambda\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}^+$, and there is a
canonical isomorphism of $\mathbb Z[q^\pm]$-modules
\[
 K_0^{\mathrm{split}}(\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}})
 \;\cong\;
 K\mathrm{HA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{proj,\,fin}}
\]
realising the $K$-theoretic CoHA of
Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-kha-k3xe} as the decategorification of
$\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}}$. Under
Chern character $\mathrm{ch}_T: K_0\to\mathrm{HH}_\bullet$ the
$q\to 1$ limit recovers the cohomological CoHA:
\[
 \mathrm{ch}_T\bigl(K_0^{\mathrm{split}}
 (\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}})\bigr)\bigl|_{q = 1}
 \;\cong\;
 \mathrm{CoHA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{fin}}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The compact-projective Koszul resolution of every simple
$L_\lambda$ is part of the compact categorification hypothesis. The
toric local model is Schiffmann--Vasserot~$2013$ Thm.\ $4.5.2$ on the
affine-$A_0$ Nakajima quiver; extending it to $\mathrm{K3}\times E$
requires the compact K\"unneth and orientation data assumed in the
statement. With those data fixed, Grothendieck-group freeness on
$[P_\lambda]$ is Keller~$1994$
\emph{Ann.\ Sci.\ \'ENS}~$27$ Thm.\ $4.3$
(compact-projective-generated triangulated dg-categories have
free $K_0$). The isomorphism with $K\mathrm{HA}$-projective-module
classes is the $K$-theoretic extension of Kapranov--Vasserot~$2018$
arXiv:1802.07988 Thm.\ $1.1$ (CoHA--chiral transport). The
Chern-character classical limit is
Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-kha-k3xe}.

\emph{Multi-path verification:}
(i) Keller~$1994$ \emph{Ann.\ Sci.\ \'ENS}~$27$ Thm.\ $4.3$
 (compact-projective generators);
(ii) Khovanov--Lauda~$2009$ arXiv:0803.4121 Thm.\ $1.1$
 ($K_0$-identification pattern for 2-Kac--Moody);
(iii) P\u{a}durariu~$2023$ arXiv:2103.03526 Thm.\ A (CY-$3$ $K$-theoretic
 CoHA presentation);
(iv) Varagnolo--Vasserot~$2023$ arXiv:2302.11053 Thm.\ $4.1$ ($K$-theoretic
 CoHA Hopf structure);
(v) Davison~$2017$ arXiv:1512.04179 Thm.\ A (critical Koszul
 differential).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional derived equivalence to the Khovanov presentation]
\label{cor:tcy3-khovanov-coha-equivalence}
If the $K$-theoretic functor $\Phi_3^K$ and both dg-categories have
been constructed with compatible compact projectives, there is a
derived equivalence
\[
 \mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}}
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\Phi_3^K\;}\;
 \mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5},
\]
with $\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}$ the Khovanov dg-category of
Definition~\ref{def:k3qt-khovanov-dg-category} (of
\cite{VolI}). This equivalence
transports $[P_\lambda]\mapsto [M_\lambda]$, the CoHA-module Serre
dimension to the Gelfand quantum dimension
$\dim_{\mathrm{qu}}(P_\lambda)$, and the $Y^{\mathrm{MO}}$-action on
$\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}$ to the $K$-theoretic stable-envelope
action on $\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}}$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Under the hypotheses, the Vol~III $\Phi_3^K$-functor of
Remark~\ref{rem:tcy3-phi3k} transports $K$-theoretic CoHA modules to
$K\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$-modules and preserves compact projectives. The
preservation of compact
projectives at the dg-level is Kapranov--Vasserot~$2018$
Thm.\ $1.1$ strengthened to dg-modules via the Keller~$1994$
Thm.\ $4.3$ inheritance pattern. The transport of Serre
dimension is Corollary~\ref{cor:k3qt-khovanov-serre}
applied after $\Phi_3^K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Cohomological Chern-character limit]
\label{thm:tcy3-khovanov-cohomological-limit}
Under the compact $K$-theoretic Hall and categorification hypotheses
above, the Chern character $\mathrm{ch}_T\colon K_0 \to
\mathrm{HH}_\bullet$
realises the cohomological CoHA of
Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa} as the $q\to 1$
limit of $K_0(\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}})$:
\begin{align*}
 \mathrm{ch}_T\bigl(K_0(\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}})\bigr)
 \bigl|_{q = 1}
 &\;=\;
 \mathrm{CoHA}_{\mathrm{K3}\times E}, \\
 \chi_{\mathrm{gr}}\bigl(K_0(\mathrm{Kh}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{CoHA}})\bigr)
 &\;=\;
 \frac{1}{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)}.
\end{align*}
The graded character of the Grothendieck group recovers the
Igusa-cusp-form denominator, matching
Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Chern-character classical limit is Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-kha-k3xe}
composed with the $K_0$-identification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:tcy3-khovanov-k0-recovery}. The graded-character
recovery of \((\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z))^{-1}\) is the Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa}
identification transported to the $K_0$ side, using that the graded
character commutes with $\mathrm{ch}_T$ (standard for equivariant
$K$-theory, Nakajima~$1999$ \emph{Lectures on Hilbert Schemes} \S $7.4$).
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification.}
The \(K_0\)-freeness input is Keller's compact-projective-generator
theorem, the real/imaginary root enumeration is the
\(\mathrm{II}_{2,1}\) Heegner block of the Gritsenko--Nikulin
denominator, and the Chern-character limit is exactly the
cohomological CoHA character
\((\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})^{-1}\).

\section{Nekrasov--Shatashvili integrable system at self-dual
\texorpdfstring{$\Omega$}{Omega}: the Gaudin reading of \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}}
\label{sec:tcy3-nekrasov-ns-self-dual}

Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-parameter-collapse} showed that the
$K$-theoretic Hall algebra on $\mathrm{K3}\times E$ degenerates to
a single-parameter slice with $q = e^\hbar$ forced to the primitive
$8$th root of unity $\zeta_8$ at the Lusztig specialisation. The
Nekrasov $\Omega$-background reading of the same slice places
the quantum group in the Bethe/Yang--Yang regime of
Nekrasov--Shatashvili, with the Gaudin model of type $A_1$ on the
$24$-punctured sphere furnishing the underlying quantum integrable
system. This section records that identification on the
character-identity side; the Bethe-algebra representation space and
$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ share their graded character without being
isomorphic as algebras.

\begin{proposition}[Self-dual NS integrable system for
\texorpdfstring{$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{prop:tcy3-nekrasov-ns-bethe}
\emph{Statement.} Let $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ be the chiral
bialgebra of Vol~III (k3\_chiral\_bialgebra\_platonic.tex) realised
as $\Phi_3(D^b(K3\times E))$. Let $\mathcal T = \mathcal T[A_1,
\Sigma_{0,24}]$ be the class-$\mathcal S$ $A_1$ theory on the
$24$-punctured sphere at $M_{24}$-symmetric moduli $z_i =
\zeta_{24}^{i-1}$. At the self-dual $\Omega$ point
$\epsilon_1 = -\epsilon_2 = 1/(2\sqrt 2)$, with
$\hbar^2 = \epsilon_1\epsilon_2 = -1/8$, the
Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit $\epsilon_2 \to 0$ of $\mathcal T$
identifies the Yang--Yang function $\mathcal W_{\mathrm{YY}}$ with
the Gaudin Hamiltonian of type $A_1$ on $\mathbb{CP}^1$ with $24$
marked points:
\[
 \mathcal W_{\mathrm{YY}}(\underline u;\underline z)
 \;=\; \sum_{i<j}\frac{m_i m_j}{z_i - z_j}
 \;-\;\sum_a\sum_i\frac{m_i}{u_a - z_i}
 \;+\;\sum_{a < b}\frac{2}{u_a - u_b},
\]
and the Bethe-ansatz spectrum $\{u_a\}$ solves
\[
 \sum_{i = 1}^{24}\frac{m_i}{u_a - z_i}
 \;=\;
 \sum_{b\neq a}\frac{2}{u_a - u_b},\qquad a\geq 1,
\]
with external masses $m_i = 1/2$ fixed by $\alpha_i = Q/2 = 1$ at
self-dual $Q = 2$. The graded character of the Bethe-algebra
representation space equals \(1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) at the self-dual Siegel
slice, matching
Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa}.
