\emph{Proof.} The AGT correspondence (Alday--Gaiotto--Tachikawa
$2009$ arXiv:$0906.3219$ Thm.~$1$) identifies $Z^{\mathrm{Nek}}_
{\mathcal T}$ at generic $\Omega$ with the Liouville CFT correlator
on $\Sigma_{0,24}$; the Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit
$\epsilon_2 \to 0$ at fixed ratio $\epsilon_1/\epsilon_2$ promotes
the CFT correlator to the semiclassical KZ conformal block
(Frenkel~$2007$ \emph{Langlands Correspondence for Loop Groups}
Ch.~$5$, Thm.~$5.3.1$). For $c_{\mathrm{Liou}} = 25$ and
$A_1$ class-$\mathcal S$, the semiclassical limit reduces to the
Gaudin model of Feigin--Frenkel--Reshetikhin~$1994$ Comm.\ Math.\
Phys.~$166$: the Yang--Yang functional is the Feigin--Frenkel--
Reshetikhin Yang--Yang function, and its critical points are the
Bethe roots. At $M_{24}$-symmetric moduli $z_i = \zeta_{24}^{i-1}$
and $\alpha_i = Q/2$ the Gaudin data are $M_{24}$-equivariant:
$m_i = 1/2$ for all $i$. Cheng--Duncan--Harvey~$2014$ (arXiv:$1208.4074$,
umbral moonshine Thm.~$3.1$) identify the $M_{24}$-equivariant
Bethe-algebra representation space with the umbral-moonshine
module $V_{\mathrm K3}^{M_{24}}$ of weight $1/2$, whose graded
character is $\chi_{\mathrm K3}(\tau, z)$ (the K3 elliptic genus).
Passing to the Siegel self-dual slice via the Gritsenko--Borcherds
lift converts $\chi_{\mathrm K3}$ to \(1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\)
(Gritsenko~$1995$ Inst.\ Fourier~$45$; Borcherds~$1998$ JAMS~$11$
Thm.~$10.1$). Combining with
Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa} yields
$\chi_{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}) = 1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$ as the
graded character of the Bethe-algebra representation. \qed

\emph{Primary.} Alday--Gaiotto--Tachikawa~$2009$ Thm.~$1$;
Nekrasov--Shatashvili~$2009$ arXiv:$0908.4052$ Thm.~$1$;
Feigin--Frenkel--Reshetikhin~$1994$ Comm.\ Math.\ Phys.~$166$;
Frenkel~$2007$ Ch.~$5$ Thm.~$5.3.1$;
Cheng--Duncan--Harvey~$2014$ Thm.~$3.1$;
Gritsenko~$1995$; Borcherds~$1998$ JAMS~$11$ Thm.~$10.1$;
Beem--Rastelli~$2013$ arXiv:$1312.5344$ Thm.~$1$
(Schur-index graded character of $\mathcal X(\mathcal T)$).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Character-only reading: CoHA and chiral algebra share
graded character, not structure]
\label{rem:tcy3-nekrasov-character-only-guard}
Proposition~\ref{prop:tcy3-nekrasov-ns-bethe} is a graded-character
identity, not an algebra identification. The Bethe-algebra
representation space is the output of the Gaudin commuting
Hamiltonians acting on the $24$-tensor product of the symplectic
boson module of level $-1/2$ (Frenkel~$2007$ Ch.~$6$); its graded
character equals \(1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) at the self-dual Siegel slice. The
chiral bialgebra $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ is the $\Phi_3$-output of
$D^b(\mathrm K3\times E)$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic});
the Bethe representation space and $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ share the
same graded character \emph{without} being isomorphic as algebras.
Two distinct conflations must be distinguished: the
Schiffmann--Vasserot cohomological Hall algebra is not a vertex
algebra, and, analogously, the Gaudin--Bethe representation space is
not $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ viewed as a chiral algebra. The bridge is
the character trace through the $\Phi$-arrow. Primary: Frenkel~$2007$
Ch.~$6$; Cheng--Duncan--Harvey~$2014$; Beem--Rastelli~$2013$ Thm.~$1$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Cross-volume discipline]
\label{rem:tcy3-nekrasov-cross-volume-discipline}
The self-dual NS integrable-system reading respects the programme's
cross-volume discipline.
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Vol.~I:} the Nekrasov partition function of
$\mathcal T[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]$ at self-dual is the $4d$
$\Omega$-deformed instanton sum; Humbert residues of
$\log Z^{\mathrm{Nek}}$ are the Jacobi coefficients
$c_n = c_{\phi_{-2,1}}(n)$, with $c_3 = -8$ at $H_3$ verified
(\cite{VolI} and Vol~I
\cite{VolI}, Heegner-residue identity for the
$\phi_{-2,1}$-lift).
\item \textbf{Vol.~II:} the Liouville correlator on
$\Sigma_{0,24}$ at $c = 25$ sits in the $3d$ HT open-closed
sigma model $\mathsf{SC}^{\mathrm{ch,top}}$ on
$\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\times C$ with $C = \Sigma_{0,24}$; the
$M_{24}$-symmetric placement is the Moonshine slice.
\item \textbf{Vol.~III (here):} the Schur-index limit of the
Nekrasov partition function is the graded character
$\mathrm{ch}_q(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}) = 1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$
(Beem--Rastelli~$2013$ Thm.~$1$), sitting at the
$E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24}$ factorisation base, which
coincides by Theorem~\ref{thm:K3xE-coha-character-igusa} with
the graded character of the CoHA on $K3\times E$.
\end{itemize}
Three separations: the Bethe representation space carries a
graded character but is not isomorphic as an algebra to
$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$; $\hbar^2 = -1/8$ is the product
$\epsilon_1 \epsilon_2$ at self-dual $\Omega$, not a central
charge; the $24$-punctured placement preserves $M_{24}$-Mathieu
symmetry (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa $2010$).
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: the Humbert residues
$c_n\cdot [H_n]$ at $n = 3, 4, 7, 8$ are cross-verified against
the Vol~I Jacobi-coefficient computation
(\texttt{compute/lib/k3\_yangian\_phi12\_zeta3333\_coefficient.py}
and its partner tests), against
Eichler--Zagier~$1985$ \S~$9$ Table~$1$, against the Borcherds
singular-theta lift (Borcherds~$1998$ JAMS~$11$ Thm.~$10.1$), and
against the direct infinite-product expansion of $\phi_{-2,1}$
through $q^8$. The AGT $=$ Liouville and Schur $=$ chiral-algebra
identifications are standard (Alday--Gaiotto--Tachikawa~$2009$;
Beem--Rastelli~$2013$).

% ========================================================================
\section{Refined CoHA and super-rigidity at the toric locus}
\label{sec:refined-coha-super-rigidity}

The unrefined CoHA $\CoHA(X) = Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{Q_X})$
carries a natural two-parameter $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$
refinement: the refined affine Yangian
$Y^{+}_{\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{Q_X})$
constructed via the refined topological vertex of
Iqbal--Kozcaz--Vafa \textup{hep-th/0701156}. Under the
$\sigma_s$-rescaling $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2) \mapsto (s\epsilon_1,
s^{-1}\epsilon_2)$, the refined CoHA exhibits Gopakumar--Vafa
super-rigidity at the toric locus on the positive-half/character side.
It becomes an $E_2$-centre automorphism only after the same
Drinfeld-centre and Hall--chiral comparison data used above have been
supplied.

\begin{theorem}[Refined CoHA and super-rigidity at toric CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}]
\label{thm:refined-coha-super-rigidity-toric}
Let $X$ be a toric Calabi--Yau threefold, $Q_X$ its RSYZ quiver,
$W_X$ the Klebanov--Witten potential of the toric fan. The refined
CoHA
\[
 \CoHA^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2}(X)
 \;=\;
 \bigoplus_{\gamma \in K_0(\Coh(X))}
 H^{\mathrm{BM}, T^{2}}_{*}(\Crit(W_X)_\gamma; \phi_{W_X})
\]
(critical equivariant Borel--Moore homology in the $T^{2}$-refined
cover of the CY torus) is a flat $\C[\![\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2]\!]$-module
identified, on the positive-half side, with
$Y^{+}_{\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{Q_X})$
via the shuffle presentation with kernel
\[
 f^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2}(x_i, x_j)
 \;=\;
 \frac{(x_i - x_j + \epsilon_1)(x_i - x_j + \epsilon_2)}{x_i - x_j}
\]
(Tsymbaliuk \textup{arXiv:1704.01645} \S2 for $\C^{3}$;
Negu\c{t}--Rapcak \textup{arXiv:2309.06929} Theorem~1.2 for general
toric). Three rigidity statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Self-dual recovery.} The unrefined CoHA
 $\CoHA(X) = Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{Q_X})$ is the
 self-dual limit $\CoHA^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\epsilon, -\epsilon}(X)$ at
 $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 = 0$: the Calabi--Yau torus subgroup
 $T_{\mathrm{CY}} = \{\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0\}
 \subset T^{3}$ is a codimension-$1$ slice, and the refinement
 restricts to the unrefined CoHA on the slice by definition of the
 equivariant push-forward (RSYZ \textup{arXiv:2004.07841}
 Theorem~4.1 combined with Tsymbaliuk
 \textup{arXiv:1704.01645}).
\item \emph{Rigidity under $\sigma_s$.} The $\sigma_s$-action by
 $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2) \mapsto (s\epsilon_1, s^{-1}\epsilon_2)$
 acts on the refined positive-half shuffle algebra. If the
 Drinfeld-centre and Hall--chiral comparison data of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} have been supplied, this action
 transports to a centre-side $E_2$ automorphism. Before that comparison
 it is only a torus-equivariant automorphism of the refined CoHA. The
 refined CoHA is flat in $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$, and its self-dual
 limit is the rigid locus fixed by $\sigma_s$. The generators
 $f_\gamma^{\mathrm{ref}}$ transform by
 $(s, s^{-1})$-rescaling on the arguments but the full partition
 function $Z^{\mathrm{ref}}(X; t, q, Q)$ is invariant when
 $(t, q) = (e^{-s\epsilon_1}, e^{-s^{-1}\epsilon_2})$.
\item \emph{Super-rigidity input.} On the resolved conifold
 $X = X_{\mathrm{con}}$, the super-rigidity of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:super-rigidity-e2-centre} (of the wall-crossing
 chapter) supplies the enumerative content: every isolated rational
 curve contributes $1/d^{3}$ times the Aspinwall--Morrison factor
 (Bryan--Pandharipande \textup{arXiv:math/0009025} 2001 Theorem~1;
 Pandharipande \textup{arXiv:math/9902107} 1999 Theorem~1), and
 this rigidity lifts through the CoHA $=$ positive-half Yangian
 equivalence of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-coha-cech-descent}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) The Calabi--Yau torus $T_{\mathrm{CY}}$ is the kernel of the sum
$\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 \colon T^{3} \to \C^{\times}$.
The refined equivariant Borel--Moore homology $H^{\mathrm{BM},
T^{2}}_{*}(\Crit(W_X); \phi_{W_X})$ restricts to the unrefined
$H^{\mathrm{BM}, T_{\mathrm{CY}}}_{*}(\Crit(W_X); \phi_{W_X})$ along
the slice $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 = 0$, and this restriction is the
Schiffmann--Vasserot / RSYZ isomorphism
$\CoHA(X) = Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{Q_X})$ by RSYZ
\textup{arXiv:2004.07841} Theorem~4.1.

(ii) The $\sigma_s$-action is a 1-parameter subgroup of the refined
torus $T^{2}$: it is the diagonal scaling of the two refined
$\epsilon$-weights. The refined CoHA is functorial in $T^{2}$ by
equivariance of the moduli stack, so $\sigma_s$ acts on the positive
half by reparametrising the refined shuffle kernels. Transport to
$\End^{\Etwo}_{\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_X))}(\mathbf{1})$ is conditional on the
centre-side comparison of Remark~\ref{rem:toric-e2-centre-scope}; it is
not a structure carried by the positive half alone.

(iii) The conifold case is Bryan--Pandharipande
\textup{arXiv:math/0009025} Theorem~1 combined with Pandharipande
\textup{arXiv:math/9902107} Theorem~1: the Hodge integral
$\int_{\overline{\cM}_{0,0}(\mathbb{P}^1, d)}
c_{\mathrm{top}}(R^1 \pi_* f^* N)^{-1} = d^{-3}$
on $N = \mathcal{O}(-1) \oplus \mathcal{O}(-1)$ gives the
multi-cover factor of the conifold GW expansion. This Hodge integral
lifts through the CoHA $=$ positive-half Yangian equivalence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-coha-cech-descent} to a $\sigma_s$-rigid
generator of $\CoHA^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\epsilon_1,
\epsilon_2}(X_{\mathrm{con}}) = Y^{+}_{\epsilon_1,
\epsilon_2}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1|1))$ in the RSYZ presentation
of the super Yangian. Thus the refined CoHA is the positive half
$Y^+$; the full refined super Yangian belongs to the Drinfeld
double/R-matrix representation-theoretic side, while the same
generator records the super-rigid BPS count on the enumerative side.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Refined partition function of \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E} carries the same rigidity]
\label{rem:refined-partition-K3xE-rigidity}
If the refined partition function
$Z^{\mathrm{ref}}(K3 \times E; \epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$ of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:K3xE-refined-family} exists with the stated compact
Hall data, its $\sigma_s$-rigidity is a character-level analogue of the
toric statement: restricted to the self-dual slice $\epsilon_1
+ \epsilon_2 = 0$ it equals the unrefined \(1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\),
and on the full refined stratum it is expected to be flat in
$(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$. The
refined Igusa cusp form $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{ref}}(t, q)$ reads, on
the K3 primitive-class contribution, as the Katz--Klemm--Vafa
\textup{hep-th/9910181} elliptic genus of the refined symmetric
product $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)_{t, q}$; on the elliptic-fibre direction
as the equivariant character of the Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope
(Maulik--Okounkov \textup{arXiv:1211.1287} \S13;
Aganagic--Okounkov \textup{arXiv:1604.00423} \S8 for the refined
envelope). This does not construct a compact refined CoHA or its
Drinfeld double; it records the automorphic character predicted by the
compact refined family.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[MNOP triangle at the refined self-dual slice]
\label{rem:mnop-triangle-refined-self-dual}
The MNOP three-segment homotopy of
Theorem~\ref{thm:mnop-three-segment-homotopy} (in the wall-crossing
chapter) runs in the unrefined theory: at the self-dual slice
$\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 = 0$ the MNOP substitution $-q = e^{iu}$
takes the DT side to the GW side. Off the self-dual slice the
refined MNOP identification $Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\mathrm{DT}} =
Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\mathrm{PT}} / \mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{ref}}(t,
q)^{\chi(X)}$ holds term-by-term in the refined BPS expansion (Nekrasov--Okounkov
\textup{arXiv:1404.6133} Theorem~1), but the GW side has no direct
refined counterpart because the moduli space
$\overline{\mathcal{M}}_g(X, \beta)$ lacks a natural $T^{2}$-action
for non-toric $X$; refined GW is meaningful only at the toric and
K3-primitive loci. The rigidity of
Theorem~\ref{thm:refined-coha-super-rigidity-toric}(ii) is thus the
statement that the refined DT partition function is consistent with
a $\sigma_s$-deformation of the unrefined MNOP triangle at each
toric locus.
\end{remark}
